\mathfrak{F}\colon\mathcal{U}\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}
corresponding to
(\mathfrak{I}_{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}},\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}},\mathfrak{e}_{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}})\in\mathbf{SGL}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq})
is an equivalence.

4.
Free extensions of symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-categories

Recall from Notation
3.17
that
\mathcal{V}
denotes the symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,2)}
-category freely obtained from
\mathrm{Fin}
by adjoining the 1-morphism
\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{V}}
, the
C_{2}
-equivariant 1-morphisms
\mathfrak{e}_{\mathcal{V}}
, the 2-morphism
\beta_{\mathcal{V}}
, and the
C_{2}
-equivariant structure on the resulting 2-morphism
\tilde{\beta}_{\mathcal{V}}
.
In order to identify
\mathcal{V}
with
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}
, we need to understand how a free symmetric monoidal extension of an
{(\infty,2)}
-category looks like. In this section we fix
n\geq 1
and consider the problem of describing extensions of symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-categories obtained by freely adjoining spaces of new
n
-morphisms.

4.1.
Definition of free extensions

Notation 4.1
.

Recall Definition
2.6
. For a pair of spaces
(X,\partial X)
, i.e. a map of spaces
i\colon\partial X\to X
we denote by
\partial(X\times\Theta_{n})
, leaving
\partial X
and
i
implicit in the notation, the
{(\infty,n)}
-category
X\times\mathrm{O}_{n-1}\sqcup_{\partial X\times\mathrm{O}_{n-1}}\partial X\times\Theta_{n}
; we have a canonical
{(\infty,n)}
-functor
\partial(X\times\Theta_{n})\to X\times\Theta_{n}
.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
n\geq 1
, let
(X,\partial X)
be a pair of spaces, let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
, and let
F\colon\partial(X\times\Theta_{n})\to\mathcal{C}
be an
{(\infty,n)}
-functor, whose datum is equivalent to a commutative square of spaces as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{n-1},\mathcal{C}^{n\simeq})^{\simeq}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F|_{\partial X\times\Theta_{n}}}
restriction
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{X\times\mathrm{O}_{n-1}}}

We denote by
\mathcal{C}[X,\partial X]^{\mathrm{sm}}
, leaving
F
implicit in the notation, the symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-category representing the limit-preserving and accessible functor

\mathrm{Fun}^{\otimes}(\mathcal{C},-)^{\simeq}\times_{\mathrm{Fun}(\partial(X\times\Theta_{n}),-)^{\simeq}}\mathrm{Fun}(X\times\Theta_{n},-)^{\simeq}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})\to\mathcal{S}.

If
\partial X=\emptyset
, we just write
\mathcal{C}[X]^{\mathrm{sm}}
.

Informally,
\mathcal{C}[X,\partial X]^{\mathrm{sm}}
is the free symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-category obtained from
\mathcal{C}
by adjoining “
X
many” new
n
-morphisms, with sources and targets of all depths specified by the map
X\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{n-1},\mathcal{C}^{n\simeq})^{\simeq}
, and by identifying “
\partial X
” many of them with the
n
-morphisms of
\mathcal{C}
specified by the map
\partial X\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
.

Notation 4.3
.

Let
k\geq 0
, let
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{0}\to\dots\to\mathcal{C}_{k}=\mathcal{D}
be a sequence of morphisms in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
, and for
1\leq i\leq k
let
(X_{i},\partial X_{i})
be a pair of spaces and
F_{i}\colon\partial(X_{i}\times\Theta_{n})\to\mathcal{C}_{i-1}
be an
{(\infty,n)}
-functor.
If the canonical map
\mathcal{C}_{i-1}[X_{i},\partial X_{i}]^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\mathcal{C}_{i}
is an equivalence for all
1\leq i\leq k
, we say that
\mathcal{D}
is an
iterated extension
and write
\mathcal{D}\simeq\mathcal{C}[X_{1},\partial X_{1};\dots;X_{k},\partial X_{k}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
, or shortly
\mathcal{D}\simeq\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
.

Example 4.4
.

For
n=1
we have
\mathcal{U}\simeq\mathrm{Fin}[*\sqcup\mathbf{B}C_{2}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
for the functor

F\colon\partial((*\sqcup\mathbf{B}C_{2})\times\Theta_{1})\simeq(*\sqcup\mathbf{B}C_{2})\times\mathrm{O}_{0}\simeq(*\sqcup\mathbf{B}C_{2})\times\left\{s_{0},t_{0}\right\}\to\mathrm{Fin}

restricting to the constant functor at
\underline{0}
on
\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times s_{0}\sqcup*\times t_{0}
, the constant functor at
\underline{1}
on
*\times s_{0}
, and the inclusion
\mathbf{B}C_{2}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fin}
on
\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times t_{0}\simeq\mathbf{B}C_{2}
.

For
n=2
we have
\mathcal{V}\simeq\mathcal{U}[*\ ;\ \mathbf{B}C_{2},*]^{\mathrm{sm}}
: the first extension is given by

F_{1}\colon\partial(*\times\Theta_{2})\simeq\mathrm{O}_{1}\to\mathcal{U}

sending
s_{0},t_{0}\mapsto\underline{1}
, and sending
s_{1}\mapsto((\mathfrak{t}_{\mathcal{U}}\mu)\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}})(\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}}\sqcup\mathfrak{e}_{\mathcal{U}})
and
t_{1}\mapsto\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}}
; the result of this extension is adjoining the 2-morphism
\beta_{\mathcal{V}}
. The second extension, using the pair of spaces
(\mathbf{B}C_{2},*)
, is given by

F_{2}\colon\partial(\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\Theta_{2})\simeq\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathrm{O}_{1}\sqcup_{*\times\mathrm{O}_{1}}*\times\Theta_{2}\to\mathcal{U}[*],

whose description is precisely given by the diagram before Definition
3.16
.

4.2.
Multigraded categories

In the study of extensions of symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-categories as in Definition
4.2
and Notation
4.3
, it is convenient to consider the “old
n
-morphisms” as living in grading 0, and the “new
n
-morphisms” as living in positive grading. We introduce some definitions to make this precise.
In the rest of the section we denote by
M
an
ungroup-like
abelian monoid as in Definition
2.9
.

Definition 4.5
.

An
M
-graded
{(\infty,n)}
-category
is an
{(\infty,n)}
-category
\mathcal{C}
together with an
{(\infty,n)}
-functor
\mathfrak{d}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathbf{B}^{n}M
. We denote by
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}:=(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})_{/\mathbf{B}^{n}M}
the presentable
{(\infty,1)}
-category of small
M
-graded
{(\infty,n)}
-categories; we denote by
\mathrm{Fun}_{M}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})^{\simeq}
the morphism space between
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
.
For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
we usually denote by
\delta
the composite map of spaces

\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\overset{\mathfrak{d}}{\to}\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathbf{B}^{n}M)^{\simeq}\simeq M,

and refer to it as an
M
-grading
on
\mathcal{C}
. We present an object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
as a pair
(\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{d})
or as a pair
(\mathcal{C},\delta)
. For
a\in M
we denote by
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{a}
the fibre at
a
of
\delta
, which we think of as the space of all
n
-morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
of grading
a
.

We note that
\delta
sends invertible
n
-morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
to invertible
n
-morphisms in
\mathbf{B}^{n}M
; since
M
is ungroup-like abelian, the subspace of invertible
n
-morphisms of
\mathcal{C}
is contained in
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{0}
.
We also note that, since
\mathbf{B}^{n}M
is a symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-category, the overcategory
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}=(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})_{/\mathbf{B}^{n}M}
inherits a symmetric monoidal structure; moreover we have an equivalence of presentable
{(\infty,1)}
-categories
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M})\simeq\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})_{/\mathbf{B}^{n}M}
.

We further observe that a map of spaces
\delta\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to M
is a valid
M
-grading, making
(\mathcal{C},\delta)
into an object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
, precisely if
\delta
is additive along compositions in all of the
n
directions; and for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
, we have can upgrade
(\mathcal{C},\delta)
to an object in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
(in an essentially unique way) if moreover
\delta
is additive along symmetric monoidal products of
n
-morphisms.

Definition 4.6
.

We denote by
\Delta^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
the category of multisimplices of the
n
-fold simplicial set
\mathbf{B}^{n}M
from Example
2.10
, i.e.
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}\to(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}
is a left fibration corresponding to the functor
\mathbf{B}^{n}M\colon(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\to\mathcal{S}
. We denote by
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{M}\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}
the opposite, right fibration. An object in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
or
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{M}
is usually denoted
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
with
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
and
\nu\in M^{\underline{\mathbf{k}}}\simeq\mathbf{B}^{n}M([\mathbf{k}])
.

A morphism in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
or
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{M}
is inert/active if the underlying morphism in
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}
or
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}
is inert/active. We denote by
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int}}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
and
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{M,\mathrm{int}}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{M}
the subcategories spanned by all objects and inert morphisms.

We remark that, for
M\neq 0
,
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{M}
is
not
equivalent to
(\mathbf{\Delta}_{M})^{n}
.

Definition 4.7
.

We let
s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}:=\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M},\mathcal{S})
and refer to it as the
\infty
-category of
M
-graded
n
-fold simplicial spaces. For
X\in s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
and
a\in X([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
, the total grading of
a
is defined as the total grading of
\nu
(see Notation
2.11
).

Remark 4.8
.

We can equivalently define
s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
as the overcategory
s^{n}\mathcal{S}_{/\mathbf{B}^{n}M}
;
with this second description,
s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
is automatically endowed with a symmetric monoidal structure.
Explicitly, the tensor product of
X,Y\in s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
is the functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}\to\mathcal{S}
sending the object
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
to
\coprod_{y+z=x}X([\mathbf{k}],y)\times Y([\mathbf{k}],z)
.

Just as
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}\subseteq s^{n}\mathcal{S}
is a full and reflective subcategory of
s^{n}\mathcal{S}
(see Subsection
2.2
), we can regard
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
as the full and reflective subcategory of
s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
spanned by objects
X
satisfying the following conditions:

•

(
M
-Segal condition) The composite functor
X_{\mathrm{int}}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{M,\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{op},n}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}\overset{X}{\to}\mathcal{S}
is right Kan extended from the full subcategory
\mathbf{\Delta}_{M,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}^{\mathrm{op},n}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}_{M,\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{op},n}
spanned by objects
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
with
\mathbf{k}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{n}
.

•

(Constancy and completeness condition) The restriction of
X
to the full subcategory
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0}\subseteq\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
satisfies the constancy and completeness conditions from Subsection
2.2
. Here
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0}
denotes the full subcategory of
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
corresponding to the submonoid
0\subset M
.

Moreover
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
is a symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory of
s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
; in fact, an object
X\in s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
lies in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
if and only if its image under the symmetric monoidal forgetful functor
s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}\to s^{n}\mathcal{S}
lies in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
.

Remark 4.9
.

One can characterise
M
-graded
{(\infty,n)}
-categories as those
M
-graded
n
-fold simplicial spaces that are local with respect to a suitable set of maps of
M
-graded simplicial spaces, in a way which is analogous to Remark
2.3
. In particular
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
is an accessible localisation of
s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
.

Remark 4.10
.

The abelian monoid structure on
M
yields a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathcal{S}_{/M}
, and
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}
can be identified with
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{S}_{/M}}
in the sense of
[
6
]
.

Notation 4.11
.

For a monoid homomorphism between ungroup-like abelian monoids
f\colon M\to M^{\prime}
we denote by abuse of notation also by
f
the induced functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M^{\prime}}
, inducing by restriction a functor

f^{*}\colon s^{n}_{M^{\prime}}\mathcal{S}\to s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
; the left adjoint to
f^{*}
, given by left Kan extension along
f
, is denoted
f_{!}
.

In the setting of Notation
4.11
, and in the light of Remark
4.8
, the functor
f_{!}\colon s^{n}\mathcal{S}_{/\mathbf{B}^{n}M}\to s^{n}\mathcal{S}_{/\mathbf{B}^{n}M^{\prime}}
sends
(X\overset{\mathfrak{d}}{\to}\mathbf{B}^{n}M)\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
to the composite

(X\overset{\mathfrak{d}}{\to}\mathbf{B}^{n}M\overset{\mathbf{B}^{n}f}{\to}\mathbf{B}^{n}M^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M^{\prime}};

from the point of view of the grading, we replace
\delta\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to M
with
f\circ\delta
.
Similarly,
f^{*}\colon Y\mapsto Y\times_{\mathbf{B}^{n}M^{\prime}}\mathbf{B}^{n}M
.
From this we see that
f_{!}
is symmetric monoidal and
f^{*}
is lax symmetric monoidal, and
f_{!}
and
f^{*}
restrict to an adjunction
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M^{\prime}}
. In particular we obtain adjunctions

f_{!}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{CAlg}(s^{n}_{M^{\prime}}\mathcal{S})\colon f^{*};\quad f_{!}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M^{\prime}})\colon f^{*}.

Example 4.12
.

The initial map of monoids
\iota\colon 0\to M
gives rise to a fully faithful functor
\iota_{!}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M})
, with essential image given by the
M
-graded symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-categories all of whose
n
-morphisms have vanishing
M
-grading. The right adjoint
\iota^{*}
sends
(\mathcal{C},\delta)
to the sub-
{(\infty,n)}
-category of
\mathcal{C}
comprising all
i
-morphisms for
i\leq n-1
, and all
n
-morphisms of vanishing
M
-grading.
Conversely, the terminal map
\tau\colon M\to 0
gives rise to the functor
\tau_{!}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M})\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
forgetting the
M
-grading, with right adjoint given by
\tau^{*}\colon\mathcal{C}\mapsto\mathcal{C}\times\mathbf{B}^{n}M
.

Remark 4.13
.

Let
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}[X,\partial X]^{\mathrm{sm}}
be an extension as in Definition
4.2
. Then an
M
-grading
\delta\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{D})^{\simeq}\to M
is the same as a functor
\mathfrak{d}\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathbf{B}^{n}M
, so it is uniquely determined by an
M
-grading
\delta|_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to M
, a map
\delta_{X}\colon X\to M
, and homotopy filling the diagram of spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F|_{\partial X\times\Theta_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta|_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{X}}

Note that, since
M
is discrete, such a homotopy, if it exists, is essentially unique.

Definition 4.14
.

Recall Notation
4.3
. We say that an iterated extension of symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-categories
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
is
\mathbb{N}
-admissible
if there exists a
\mathbb{N}
-grading
\delta\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{D})^{\simeq}\to\mathbb{N}
such that the composite

\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{D})^{\simeq}\overset{\delta}{\to}\mathbb{N}

is constantly 0, and each composite
X_{i}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{D})^{\simeq}\overset{\delta}{\to}\mathbb{N}
is constantly 1. By the previous remarks, such an
\mathbb{N}
-grading, if it exists, is essentially unique.

Both extensions
\mathrm{Fin}\to\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{V}
from Example
4.4
are
\mathbb{N}
-admissible.

4.3.
Truncation and
n
-groupoid cores of extensions.

Definition 4.15
.

We say that a subset
I\subseteq M
is a
coideal
if
0\in I
and if for all
a,b\in M
with
a+b\in I
we have
a,b\in I
.
We denote by
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
the full subcategory of
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
spanned by objects
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
with
\nu
having total grading in
I
(see Notation
2.11
): note that this is stronger than just requiring all coordinates of
\nu
to be in
I
. We define similarly
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{I}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{M}
. We denote by
\iota^{I,M}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
the inclusion functor.
We denote by
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}
the subcategory of
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
spanned by inert morphisms, and by
\iota^{I,M}_{\mathrm{int}}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int}}
the inclusion functor.

We denote by
s^{n}_{I}\mathcal{S}:=\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I},\mathcal{S})
, and by
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I}\subseteq s^{n}_{I}\mathcal{S}
the full and reflective subcategory spanned by objects
X
satisfying the following conditions:

•

(
I
-Segal condition) the restriction of
X
along the inclusion
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
is right Kan extended from the full subcategory
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}
spanned by objects
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
with
\mathbf{k}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{n}
;

•

(Constancy and completeness condition) the restriction of
X
on the full subcategory
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0}\subseteq\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
satisfies the constancy and completeness conditions from Subsection
2.2
.

An object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I}
can be thought of as an
I
-graded partial
{(\infty,n)}
-category, in the sense that
n
-morphisms are endowed with an
I
-grading, and compositions of
n
-morphisms (in any direction) is only defined if the sum of the gradings still lies in
I
.
It is possible to exhibit
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I}
as an accessible localisation of
s^{n}_{I}\mathcal{S}
, similarly to Remark
4.9
; in particular
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I}
is presentable.

Definition 4.16
.

Let
I\subseteq M
be a coideal.
An
M
-graded
{(\infty,n)}
-category
\mathcal{C}
is
I
-truncated
if the canonical map
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\times_{M}(M\setminus I)\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{n-1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\times(M\setminus I)
is an equivalence.

We note that
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
is
I
-truncated if and only if for all
a\in M\setminus I
the map
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{a}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{n-1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
given by restriction is an equivalence. Roughly speaking, the last condition means that for all pairs of
(n-1)
-morphisms
s,t
sharing all levels of sources and targets, and for all
a\in M\setminus I
, the space of
n
-morphisms from
s
to
t
of grading
a
is contractible.

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
I\subseteq M
be a coideal.
Let
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
, let
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}
denote the full subcategory of
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}
spanned by arrows with target in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
, and let

(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}
denote the full subcategory of
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int}})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}
spanned by arrows with target in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}
. Then the inclusion
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}\hookrightarrow(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}
is an initial functor, i.e. limits of diagrams parametrised by
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}
can be computed upon restriction to
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}
.

Proof.

We compute, for any
(\alpha\colon([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\to([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime}))\in(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}
, the overcategory
((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/})_{/\alpha}
: this is the same as the category whose objects and morphisms are described as follows:

•

an object is a factorisation
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\overset{\beta}{\to}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}],\nu^{\prime\prime})\overset{\gamma}{\to}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})
of
\alpha
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
, with
\beta
inert and with
([\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}],\nu^{\prime\prime})\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
;

•

a morphism from
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\overset{\beta}{\to}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}],\nu^{\prime\prime})\overset{\gamma}{\to}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})
to
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\overset{\beta^{\prime}}{\to}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}],\nu^{\prime\prime\prime})\overset{\gamma^{\prime}}{\to}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})
is an inert morphism
\epsilon\colon([\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}],\nu^{\prime\prime})\to([\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime\prime}],\nu^{\prime\prime\prime})
with
\epsilon\beta=\beta^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\epsilon=\gamma
.

The category
((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/})_{/\alpha}
has a terminal object, given by the unique factorisation
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\overset{\beta}{\to}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}],\nu^{\prime\prime})\overset{\gamma}{\to}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})
of
\alpha
with
\beta
inert and with
\gamma
active: here we crucially use that
I
is a coideal to conclude that, since
\beta
is an active morphism in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M}
with target in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
, also the source
([\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}],\nu^{\prime\prime})
of
\beta
lies in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
.

This shows that
((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/})_{/\alpha}
is weakly contractible, and the claim follows from Quillen’s Theorem A.
∎

Corollary 4.18
.

Let
I\subseteq M
be a coideal.
Then the Beck–Chevalley transformation in the following diagram is an equivalence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{n}_{I}\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{n,\mathrm{op}}_{I,\mathrm{int}},\mathcal{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{n,\mathrm{op}}_{M,\mathrm{int}},\mathcal{S}).}
restrict
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota^{I,M})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota^{I,M}_{\mathrm{int}})_{*}}
restrict
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Proof.

The value at
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int}}
of the given natural transformation applied to
X\in s^{n}_{I}\mathcal{S}
is the comparison map of spaces
\lim_{(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}}X\to\lim_{(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}}X
, which is an equivalence by Lemma
4.17
.
∎

We stress that the vertical functors in the diagram of Corollary
4.18
are fully faithful inclusions, since they are given by right Kan extension from a full subcategory; moreover the horizontal functors detect equivalences, since they are given by restriction to a wide subcategory.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
I\subseteq M
be a coideal
and let
X\in s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
. Then
X
is an
I
-truncated
M
-graded
{(\infty,n)}
-category if and only if both of the following hold:

(1)

X
is right Kan extended from its restriction on
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
;

(2)

X|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I}
.

In other words, the right Kan extension functor
(\iota^{I,M})_{*}\colon s^{n}_{I}\mathcal{S}\to s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
restricts to a fully faithful inclusion
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
with essential image given by the full subcategory of
I
-truncated
M
-graded
{(\infty,n)}
-categories.

Proof.

Let
X\in s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
, and let
X_{\mathrm{int}}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int}}
denote the restriction of
X
on
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int}}
. Then both the hypothesis (2) or the hypothesis “
X\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
” imply the (same) constancy and completeness conditions for
X
, as these are conditions on
X|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0}}
.

Assume henceforth that
X
does satisfy these constancy and completeness conditions. Then (2) is equivalent to requiring that
X_{\mathrm{int}}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}}
be right Kan extended from the full subcategory
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}
. Instead “
X\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
” is equivalent to requiring that
X_{\mathrm{int}}
be right Kan extended from the full subcategory
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int}}
; the further hypothesis “
X
is
I
-truncated” is then equivalent to requiring that
X_{\mathrm{int}}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}}
is in turn right Kan extended from the full subcategory
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}
. Moreover, by Corollary
4.18
, (1) is equivalent to requiring that
X_{\mathrm{int}}
be right Kan extended from the full subcategory
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{M,\mathrm{int}}
. Hence both (1)-(2) and “
X
is an
I
-truncated
M
-graded
{(\infty,n)}
-category” are equivalent to the requirement that
X_{\mathrm{int}}
be right Kan extended from
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}
.
∎

Notation 4.20
.

We denote by
T_{I}\colon s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}\to s^{n}_{M}\mathcal{S}
the
I
-truncation functor
, i.e. the composite
(\iota^{I,M})_{*}(\iota^{I,M})^{*}
. It restricts to an endofunctor of
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
.

Corollary 4.21
.

Let
I\subseteq M
be a coideal
and let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
. Then the unit of the adjunction
\mathcal{C}\to T_{I}(\mathcal{C})
induces equivalences
\mathcal{C}^{n\simeq}\simeq T_{I}(\mathcal{C})^{n\simeq}
and
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{a}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},T_{I}(\mathcal{C}))^{\simeq}_{a}
for all
a\in I
. Equivalently, a functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
is a
T_{I}
-equivalence if and only if it induces equivalences
\mathcal{C}^{n\simeq}\simeq\mathcal{D}^{n\simeq}
, and equivalences
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{a}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{D})^{\simeq}_{a}
for all
a\in I
.

Proof.

Since
(\iota^{I,M})_{*}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
is fully faithful, a
T_{I}
-equivalence is the same as a morphism
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
which is sent to an equivalence along restriction to
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I}
. We can further restrict
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
to the wide subcategory
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}
, and using that both
\mathcal{C}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}}
and
\mathcal{D}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int}}}
are right Kan extended from
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}
, we obtain that a
T_{I}
-equivalence is the same as a morphism
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
evaluating to an equivalence (of spaces) on objects in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{I,\leq 1}
: objects in this subcategory have the following forms:

•

([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
for
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{n})
with at least one
k_{i}=0
(and necessarily
\nu=0\in M^{0}
); the equivalences
\mathcal{C}([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\overset{\simeq}{\to}\mathcal{D}([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
for such objects combine to the equivalence
\mathcal{C}^{n\simeq}\simeq\mathcal{D}^{n\simeq}
;

•

([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
with
\mathbf{k}=1^{n}
and
\nu=a\in I
; the equivalence
\mathcal{C}([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\overset{\simeq}{\to}\mathcal{D}([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
is then the same requirement as the equivalence
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{a}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{D})^{\simeq}_{a}
.

∎

Lemma 4.22
.

Let
I\subseteq M
be a coideal.
Then the symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
induces under localisation a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I}
, such that
(\iota^{I,M})^{*}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I}
is symmetric monoidal, and its right adjoint
(\iota^{I,M})_{*}
is lax symmetric monoidal. In particular we have an induced adjunction

(\iota^{I,M})^{*}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{I})\colon(\iota^{I,M})_{*},

whose right adjoint is still fully faithful.

Proof.

By
[
10
, Proposition 2.2.1.9, Example 2.2.1.7]
, it suffices to check that for every
T_{I}
-equivalence
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
and any
\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M}
, the
{(\infty,n)}
-functor
\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{D}
is again a
T_{I}
-equivalence. By Lemma
4.19
, it suffices to check equivalence on
n
-groupoid cores and on spaces of
n
-morphisms of gradings in
a\in I
; using that
I
is a coideal, both statements are evident.
∎

Corollary 4.23
.

Let
I\subseteq M
be a coideal
and let
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
be an extension of symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-categories. Assume that there is an
M
-grading
\delta\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{D})^{\simeq}\to M
such that the composites
X_{i}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{D})^{\simeq}\to M
have values in
M\setminus I
. Then the canonical symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
induces an equivalence
T_{I}(\mathcal{C})\simeq T_{I}(\mathcal{D})
and an equivalence
\mathcal{C}^{n\simeq}\simeq\mathcal{D}^{n\simeq}
.

Proof.

We may consider
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
as a morphism in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M})
.
By Lemma
4.19
, once
T_{I}(\mathcal{C})\to T_{I}(\mathcal{D})
is an equivalence, also
\mathcal{C}^{n\simeq}\to\mathcal{D}^{n\simeq}
is an equivalence. For the first, we let
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{M})
be
I
-truncated and using Notation
4.3
we compute the following chain of equivalences of spaces, for
1\leq i\leq k
:

\begin{split}\mathrm{Fun}^{\otimes}_{M}(T_{I}(\mathcal{C}_{i}),\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}&\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\otimes}_{M}(\mathcal{C}_{i},\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}\\
&\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\otimes}_{M}(\mathcal{C}_{i-1},\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}\times_{\mathrm{Fun}_{M}(\partial(X_{i}\times\Theta_{n}),\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}}\mathrm{Fun}_{M}(X_{i}\times\Theta_{n},\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}\\
&\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\otimes}_{M}(\mathcal{C}_{i-1},\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}\times_{\mathrm{Fun}(X_{i}\times\mathrm{O}_{n-1},\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}}\mathrm{Fun}(X_{i}\times\mathrm{O}_{n-1},\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}\\
&\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\otimes}_{M}(\mathcal{C}_{i-1},\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\otimes}_{M}(T_{I}(\mathcal{C}_{i-1}),\mathcal{E})^{\simeq}.\end{split}

This implies the equivalence
T_{I}(\mathcal{C}_{i-1})\simeq T_{I}(\mathcal{C}_{i})
; the result follows by combining all these equivalences and using
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{0}
and
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}_{k}
.
∎

Example 4.24
.

As promised in Remark
3.18
, since the extension of symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,2)}
-categories
\mathcal{V}\simeq\mathcal{U}[*;\mathbf{B}C_{2},*]^{\mathrm{sm}}
is
\mathbb{N}
-admissible, Corollary
4.23
implies that the
{(\infty,1)}
-functor
\mathfrak{J}\colon\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{V}^{2\simeq}
is an equivalence. Similarly, the extension of symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,1)}
-categories
\mathcal{U}\simeq\mathrm{Fin}[\mathbf{B}C_{2}\sqcup*]^{\mathrm{sm}}
is
\mathbb{N}
-admissible, so that
\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}\simeq\mathcal{U}^{\simeq}\simeq\mathcal{V}^{\simeq}
. We will therefore pretend that
\mathrm{Fin}
,
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
have the same space of objects, namely
\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}
; and that
\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V}
have the same space of 1-morphisms.

We conclude the subsection with a result on
M
-graded localisations.

Definition 4.25
.

We consider the object
(*\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}M)\in\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
and denote by
\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}
the overcategory
(\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})_{/(*\hookrightarrow\mathbf{B}M)}
: its objects are relative categories
(\mathcal{C},W)
with an
M
-grading on
\mathcal{C}
that vanishes on
W
.

For a coideal
I\subseteq M
we further denote by
\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{I}
the full and reflective subcategory of
\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}
spanned by objects
(\mathcal{C},W)
with
\mathcal{C}
being
I
-truncated.

We observe that for
(\mathcal{C},W)\in\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}
, the
M
-grading
\mathfrak{d}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathbf{B}M
induces an
M
-grading on the localisation
L(\mathcal{C},W)\to\mathbf{B}M
.

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
I\subseteq M
be a coideal.
Let
(\mathcal{C},W)\to(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},W^{\prime})
be a morphism in
\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}
restricting to an equivalence
W\xrightarrow{\simeq}W^{\prime}
and to a
T_{I}
-equivalence
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. Then the induced functor
L(\mathcal{C},W)\to L(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},W^{\prime})
is a
T_{I}
-equivalence.

Proof.

For simplicity, we consider
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{I}
as a full subcategory of
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}
via
(\iota^{I,M})_{*}
.
The following commutative square of right adjoint functors, on left, admits a corresponding commutative square of left adjoint functors, on right:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{I};}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((-),(-)^{\simeq})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((-),(-)^{\simeq})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{I}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}

The left-bottom composition sends the morphism
(\mathcal{C},W)\to(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},W^{\prime})
to an equivalence because already the left vertical functor
T_{I}
does; hence the top-right composition sends the same morphism to an equivalence; in other words, the image
L(\mathcal{C},W)\to L(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},W^{\prime})
along the top horizontal functor is a
T_{I}
-equivalence.
∎

4.4.
Extensions iterated over a finite set

Definition 4.27
.

For an extension
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
as in Notation
4.3
, we regard
\mathcal{D}
as an object in
(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}))_{\mathcal{C}/}
; for a finite set
S
we let
\mathcal{C}[S|(\underline{X},\underline{\partial X})]^{\mathrm{sm}}
denote the image along the target functor
(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}))_{\mathcal{C}/}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
of the
S
-fold coproduct of
\mathcal{D}
with itself inside
(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}))_{\mathcal{C}/}
.

The notation in Definition
4.27
is due to the fact that
\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
agrees with an extension of the form

\mathcal{C}[S\times X_{1},S\times\partial X_{1};\dots;S\times X_{k},S\times\partial X_{k}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
; roughly speaking, the difference between
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
and
\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
is that in the second case we extend
\mathcal{C}
by adjoining
S
copies of the new generating
n
-morphisms.

Definition 4.28
.

Let
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
be an extension endowed with an
M
-grading
\mathfrak{d}\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathbf{B}^{n}M
that restricts to the zero grading on
\mathcal{C}
. We
\mathfrak{d}^{S}\colon\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\mathbf{B}^{n}M^{S}
as the
M^{S}
-grading characterised by the requirement that for all
s\in S
we have a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})_{\mathcal{C}/}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{n}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{n}M^{S}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\text{\textsuperscript{th} inclusion}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\text{\textsuperscript{th} inclusion}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{d}^{S}}

Roughly speaking, to distinguish the
S
copies of new
n
-morphisms we put them in “linearly independent” gradings, and to achieve this we use
M^{S}
as the monoid for gradings instead of
M
.
In fact we may regard
\mathbf{B}^{n}M^{S}
as the coproduct in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
of
S
copies of
\mathbf{B}^{n}M
; the
M
-grading
\mathfrak{d}^{S}
is then the following composite, whose second arrow is the assembly map along the functor
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})_{\mathcal{C}/}
endowing a symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-category with the constant functor from
\mathcal{C}
at the monoidal unit:

\coprod_{S}^{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})_{\mathcal{C}/}}\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}\xrightarrow{\coprod_{S}\mathfrak{d}}\coprod_{S}^{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})_{\mathcal{C}/}}\mathbf{B}^{n}M\to\coprod_{S}^{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})}\mathbf{B}^{n}M.

Notation 4.29
.

We denote by
\mathfrak{S}_{S}
the group of automorphisms of a finite set
S
; and we let
1^{S}
denote the
\mathfrak{S}_{S}
-invariant element
(1,\dots,1)\in\mathbb{N}^{S}
.

We observe that the grading
\mathfrak{d}^{S}
admits (together with its source and target) a natural
\mathfrak{S}_{S}
-equivariant structure. In particular, if
M=\mathbb{N}
, we have an action of
\mathfrak{S}_{S}
on the space
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\simeq}_{1^{S}}
.
The main goal of this subsection is to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 4.30
.

Let
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
be an
\mathbb{N}
-admissible symmetric monoidal extension, let
S
be a finite set, and consider the following commutative diagram in
(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}))^{\mathbf{B}\mathfrak{S}_{S}}
, whose bottom row has trivial action

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}.}
fold
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{d}^{S}}
fold (sum)
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{d}}

Then the following canonical map of spaces admits a (homotopy) section

\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\simeq}_{1^{S}}\ /\mathfrak{S}_{S}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]_{\mathbb{N}})^{\simeq}_{|S|}.

The advantage of the description of
\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
in Definition
4.27
in terms of an
S
-fold coproduct inside
(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}))_{\mathcal{C}/}
comes from the fact that the assignment
S\mapsto\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
gives rise to a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{Fin}\to((\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}))_{\mathcal{C}/},\sqcup)
. Precomposing with the symmetric monoidal inclusion
\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fin}
, and postcomposing with the lax symmetric monoidal target functor
((\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})_{\mathcal{C}/},\sqcup)\to(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}),\sqcup)
, we obtain a lax symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}),\sqcup)
.

The forgetful functor
(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}),\sqcup)\to(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)},\times)
is symmetric monoidal by
[
10
, Proposition 3.2.4.7]
. Combining this with the previous, we obtain a lax symmetric monoidal functor
\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}\colon\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
; the latter functor still sends a finite set
S
to (the underlying
{(\infty,n)}
-category of)
\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
. We will also consider
\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
as a lax symmetric monoidal functor to
s^{n}\mathcal{S}
.

A similar construction makes
S\mapsto\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{S}
into a symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}\subset s^{n}\mathcal{S}
, by observing that
\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{S}
is the
S
-fold coproduct of
\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}
with itself in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
. In fact
\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{(-)}
takes values in
n
-fold simplicial sets.

Definition 4.31
.

For a finite set
S
we consider the coideal
\left\{0,1\right\}^{S}\subset\mathbb{N}^{S}
, and we denote by
\mathbf{B}^{n}01^{S}
the subsimplicial set of
\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{S}
comprising all multisimplices of total grading in
\left\{0,1\right\}^{S}
(see Notation
2.11
).

The assignment
S\mapsto\left\{0,1\right\}^{S}
gives a symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to(\mathrm{Fin},\times)
, and the assignment
S\mapsto\mathbf{B}^{n}01^{S}
gives a symmetric monoidal subfunctor
\mathbf{B}^{n}01^{(-)}\colon\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to s^{n}\mathcal{S}
of
\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{(-)}
.

Definition 4.32
.

For an
\mathbb{N}
-admissible symmetric monoidal extension
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
,
we denote by
\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]_{01}^{\mathrm{sm}}\colon\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to s^{n}\mathcal{S}
the lax symmetric monoidal functor obtained as pullback of the cospan

\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\mathbf{B}\mathbb{N}^{(-)}\leftarrow\mathbf{B}01^{(-)}
of lax symmetric monoidal functors
\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to s^{n}\mathcal{S}
.
We further define

\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}:=\underset{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\ \mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}\in s^{n}\mathcal{S}.

The fact that the cartesian symmetric monoidal structure on
s^{n}\mathcal{S}
preserves colimits in each variable implies that
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}},s^{n}\mathcal{S})
has a Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure
[
10
, Subsection 2.2.6]
, and the lax symmetric monoidal functor
\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}
is precisely a commutative algebra object in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}},s^{n}\mathcal{S})
; since
\mathrm{colim}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}},s^{n}\mathcal{S})\to s^{n}\mathcal{S}
is symmetric monoidal, we obtain a commutative algebra structure on
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}
.

Moreover we may consider
\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
, and hence also
\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}
, as a lax symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to s^{n}\mathcal{S}_{/\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}}
, by first mapping
\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to\mathrm{Fin}_{/*}\subset\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{Fin})
, which is a lax symmetric monoidal functor, and the repeating the rest of the construction at the level of arrow categories.

Notation 4.33
.

We denote by
\mathfrak{r}\colon\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}\to\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}

the resulting morphism in

\mathrm{CAlg}(s^{n}\mathcal{S})
. Composing with
\mathfrak{d}\colon\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}
makes
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}
into a commutative algebra in
\mathbb{N}
-graded
n
-fold simplicial spaces.

Lemma 4.34
.

\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}
is an
\mathbb{N}
-graded
{(\infty,n)}
-category.

Proof.

Since colimits in
s^{n}\mathcal{S}
are computed pointwise, for all
[\mathbf{k}]\in(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}
we have

\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}([\mathbf{k}])\simeq\mathrm{colim}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}([\mathbf{k}]).

For an inclusion of finite sets
S\hookrightarrow T
, the induced functor
\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\mathcal{C}[T|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
is compatible with the
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading and
\mathbb{N}^{T}
-grading on the source and target, along the induced inclusion of monoids
\mathbb{N}^{S}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{N}^{T}
; moreover Corollaries
4.21
and
4.23
imply that the map
\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}([\mathbf{k}])\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}[T|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}([\mathbf{k}])
is an inclusion of components, namely all components of the space
\mathcal{C}[T|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}([\mathbf{k}])
whose total grading, which is an element in
\left\{0,1\right\}^{T}
, lies inside the image of
\left\{0,1\right\}^{S}\hookrightarrow\left\{0,1\right\}^{T}
. This shows that the functor

\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}([\mathbf{k}])\colon\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}\to\mathcal{S}

is the left Kan extension along the inclusion
\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}
of the functor

\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}\to\mathcal{S},\quad\quad S\mapsto\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{1^{S}},

where the index “
1^{S}
” selects
\mathbf{k}
-multisimplices of total grading
1^{S}\in\mathbb{N}^{S}
. It follows that
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}([\mathbf{k}])
is equivalent to

\mathrm{colim}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}}\ \mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{1^{(-)}}\simeq\coprod_{r\geq 0}\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{1^{\underline{r}}}\ /\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{r}},

where the last formula gives a decomposition of
\mathbf{k}
-multisimplices according to their
\mathbb{N}
-valued total grading.
In particular, if
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{n})
has at least one entry
k_{i}=0
, then only the total grading
r=0
is allowed, leading to the equivalence
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}([\mathbf{k}])\simeq\mathcal{C}([\mathbf{k}])
; this implies in particular that
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}
satisfies the constancy and completeness conditions.

For the
\mathbb{N}
-Segal condition we first
write
\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{1^{\underline{r}}}
as a disjoint union of spaces
\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{\nu}
for varying
\nu\in(\mathbb{N}^{\underline{r}})^{\underline{\mathbf{k}}}
of total grading
1^{\underline{r}}
; this decomposition is given by the map
\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{1^{\underline{r}}}\to\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{\underline{r}}([\mathbf{k}])_{1^{\underline{r}}}
. Note that the datum of
\nu\in\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{\underline{r}}([\mathbf{k}])_{1^{\underline{r}}}
is the same as a partition of
\underline{r}
into
\underline{\mathbf{k}}
subsets. The action of
\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{r}}
permutes among each other the subspaces of
\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{1^{\underline{r}}}
corresponding to partitions
\nu
that yield the same splitting of
r
as a sum of
\underline{\mathbf{k}}
natural numbers, so that
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}([\mathbf{k}])_{r}\simeq\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{1^{\underline{r}}}\ /\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{r}}
decomposes as a disjoint union of subspaces corresponding to elements
\check{\nu}\in\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}([\mathbf{k}])_{r}
. For each
\nu\in\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{\underline{r}}([\underline{\mathbf{k}}])_{1^{\underline{r}}}
, the stabiliser in
\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{r}}
of the subspace
\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{\nu}
is the subgroup
\mathfrak{S}_{\nu}\subset\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{r}}
stabilising the partition
\nu
; regarding
\nu
as a map of finite sets
\nu\colon\underline{r}\to\underline{\mathbf{k}}
, this is the product
\prod_{i\in\underline{\mathbf{k}}}\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{-1}(i)}
. The
\mathbb{N}^{\underline{r}}
-Segal condition for
\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
allows us to identify
\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}])_{\nu}
as the limit of the spaces
\mathcal{C}[\underline{r}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}])_{\nu^{\prime}}
indexed by the category
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{\mathbb{N}^{\underline{r}},\mathrm{int},\leq 1})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}\simeq(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{\mathrm{int},\leq 1})
, which is a weakly contractible poset. Quotienting by the action of
\mathfrak{S}_{\nu}
, and denoting by
\check{\nu}\in\mathbb{N}^{\underline{\mathbf{k}}}
the image of
\nu
along the fold map
(\mathbb{N}^{\underline{r}}\to\mathbb{N})^{\underline{\mathbf{k}}}
, we obtain the analogous identification of
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}([\mathbf{k}])_{\check{\nu}}
as the limit over the category
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{\mathbb{N},\mathrm{int},\leq 1})_{([\mathbf{k}],\check{\nu})/}\simeq(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{\mathrm{int},\leq 1})
of the spaces
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}])_{\check{\nu}^{\prime}}
. In this last step we use that, for diagrams in
\mathcal{S}
, colimits over
\mathbf{B}\mathfrak{S}_{\nu}
commute with limits over a weakly contractible category.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.30
.

By Lemma
4.34
we have that
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}
is a symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-category; moreover the functor

\mathcal{C}\simeq\mathcal{C}[\underline{0}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}\to\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}},

induced by the symmetric monoidal inclusion
\left\{0\right\}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}
upon taking colimits of restrictions of
\mathcal{C}[-|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}
, is symmetric monoidal. The same functor is also homotopic, via the map
\underline{0}\hookrightarrow\underline{1}
in
\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}
, to the following composite map of
n
-fold simplicial spaces, whose source and targets are
{(\infty,n)}
-categories, and whose middle term does
not
have a symmetric monoidal structure:

\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}[\underline{1}|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{01}\to\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}.

The second arrow of the last composition can be restricted to
\underline{X}
at the level of
n
-morphism spaces, compatibly over
\underline{\partial X}
.
The universal property of
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
then gives rise to a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathfrak{s}\colon\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}},

and the composite of
\mathfrak{s}
with the symmetric monoidal functor
\mathfrak{r}
from Notation
4.33
is homotopic to the identity of
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
, as can be checked by restricting to
\mathcal{C}
and to the spaces
\underline{X}
.

Proposition
4.30
now follows from considering spaces of
n
-morphisms, using also the computation from the proof of Lemma
4.34
specialised to
\mathbf{k}=1^{n}
.
∎

Proving that the map from Proposition
4.30
is a retraction is sufficient for our purposes, but we conjecture that the given map is in fact an equivalence. Equivalently, we conjecture that the functors
\mathfrak{r}
and
\mathfrak{s}
from the proof of Proposition
4.30
are inverse equivalences between
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\partial{\underline{X}}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
and
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\partial{\underline{X}}]^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{inj}}}
. These statements should follow from the following stronger conjecture, by considering spaces of
n
-morphisms of grading
1^{S}
in
\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
and grading
|S|
in
\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
, and by observing that the left vertical functor in the diagram gives an
\mathfrak{S}_{S}
-fold covering between these two spaces since the right vertical functor obviously gives gives an
\mathfrak{S}_{S}
-fold covering between the corresponding spaces.

Conjecture 4.35
.

The following commutative square in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
, coming from the symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-functor
\mathcal{C}\to*
and the maps of spaces
\underline{X}\to*
, is a pullback square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}[S|\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*[S|*]^{\mathrm{sm}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{n}(\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq})^{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*[*]^{\mathrm{sm}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}.}
fold
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
fold
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

5.
Truncated categories via presheaves

In this section, for
n\geq 1
, we study more closely the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01}\subset s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}
, where we abbreviate by “
01
” the coideal
\left\{0,1\right\}\subset\mathbb{N}
. We start with a quick analysis of the case
n=1
. An object
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{01}
has an underlying
{(\infty,1)}
-category
\mathcal{C}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}}
, and the space
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{1}
of all morphisms of grading 1 is moreover endowed with composition laws on left and on right with morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
of grading 0, so that assigning to each pair of objects
(x,y)\in\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{0},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
the fibre at
(x,y)
of the map
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{1}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{0},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
gives rise to a presheaf over
(\mathcal{C}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}})\times(\mathcal{C}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}})^{\mathrm{op}}
. We will indeed construct a pullback square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}}
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)\times(-)^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}

where
d_{0}\colon\mathscr{P}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
is the bicartesian fibration from Subsection
2.4
. For
n\geq 2
we will have a similar formula, where
(-)\times(-)^{\mathrm{op}}
will be suitably replaced by another symmetric monoidal functor
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
.

Throughout the section we denote by
\iota\colon 0\to\mathbb{N}
and
\tau\colon\mathbb{N}\to 0
the initial and terminal maps of monoids, see Example
4.12
; and we denote by
T_{0}=\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\colon s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}\to s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
the truncation functor. We consider
s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}
as a full subcategory of
s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
via the fully faithful functor
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})_{*}
associated with the inclusion
\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{01}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{\mathbb{N}}
.

5.1.
Expressing
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01}
as a pullback

Definition 5.1
.

We denote by
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{1}
the full subcategory of
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{01}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{\mathbb{N}}
spanned by objects of total grading
1\in\mathbb{N}
. Concretely,
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{1}
if and only if
\nu\in\mathbb{N}^{\underline{\mathbf{k}}}
has exactly one coordinate equal to 1 and all other coordinates equal to 0. We similarly define
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}:=(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{1})^{\mathrm{op}}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{01}
. We let
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{1,\mathrm{int}}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{1}
denote the wide subcategory spanned by inert morphisms.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
X\in s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
and
Y\in s^{n}\mathcal{S}\simeq s^{n}_{0}\mathcal{S}
.
We let
\mathcal{K}^{n}(X)\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{01}
be a right fibration corresponding to
X|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{01}}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{01}\to\mathcal{S}
.
We let
\mathcal{T}^{n}(Y):=\mathcal{K}^{n}(\iota_{*}(Y))
, and by abuse of notation, we define
\mathcal{T}^{n}(X)
as
\mathcal{T}^{n}(\iota^{*}(X))\simeq\mathcal{K}^{n}(T_{0}(X))
.
The unit of the adjunction
X\to\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(X)\simeq T_{0}(X)
gives rise to a right fibration
\mathcal{K}^{n}(X)\to\mathcal{T}^{n}(X)
, and we denote by
\kappa^{n}(X)\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}(X))
the corresponding presheaf.
We further set
\mathcal{L}^{n}(Y):=\mathcal{K}^{n}(\tau^{*}(Y))
, and
\lambda^{n}(Y):=\kappa^{n}(\tau^{*}(Y))\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}(\tau^{*}(Y)))\simeq\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}(Y))
for the corresponding presheaf.

For
X\in s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
, the total grading of an object in
\mathcal{K}^{n}(X)
is defined as that of its projection in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{01}
, and it is therefore equal to
0
or
1
. For
j=0,1
we denote by
\mathcal{K}^{n}_{j}(X)
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{K}^{n}(X)
spanned by objects of total grading
j
. We introduce similar notation
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{j}(X)
,
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{j}(Y)
and
\mathcal{L}^{n}_{j}(Y)
. We denote by
\kappa^{n}_{j}(X)\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{j}(X))
the restriction of
\kappa^{n}(X)
to
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{j}(X)
; and we set
\lambda^{n}_{j}(Y):=\kappa^{n}_{j}(\tau^{*}(Y))\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{j}(\tau^{*}(Y)))\simeq\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{j}(Y))
.

We say that a morphism in
\mathcal{K}^{n}(X)
is
inert
if its projection to
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{01}
is inert. We use the index “
(-)_{\mathrm{int}}
” to denote wide subcategories spanned by inert morphisms.

For
Y\in s^{n}\mathcal{S}
we observe that
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{0}(Y)
is the opposite of the category of simplices of
Y
, i.e.
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{0}(Y)\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}
is a right fibration corresponding to
Y
.
We also observe that
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{0}(Y)
is a
left closed
full subcategory of
\mathcal{T}^{n}(Y)
, i.e. a morphism in
\mathcal{T}^{n}(Y)
with target in
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{0}(Y)
also has source in
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{0}(Y)
; this is essentially because the total grading is weakly increasing along morphisms in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}
. In particular, right Kan extension from
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(Y)^{\mathrm{op}}
exhibits
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(Y))
as the full subcategory of
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}(Y))
spanned by presheaves whose restriction on
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{0}(Y)
is equivalent to the terminal presheaf, i.e. the constant presheaf with value
*\in\mathcal{S}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
X\in s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}\subset s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
; then the following holds:

(1)

\kappa^{n}_{0}(X)\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{0}(X))
is the terminal presheaf.

If moreover
\iota^{*}(X)\in s^{n}\mathcal{S}
satisfies the Segal condition, then the following hold:

(2)

X
satisfies the
01
-Segal condition from Definition
4.15
if an only if the presheaf
\kappa^{n}_{1}(X)\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(X))
is local with respect to
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(X)_{\mathrm{int}}
.

Proof.

Let
([\mathbf{k}],\nu,x)
be an object in
\mathcal{T}^{n}(X)
, with
\nu\in\mathbb{N}^{\underline{\mathbf{k}}}
and
x\in T_{0}(X)([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
. Then the value at
([\mathbf{k}],\nu,x)
of
\kappa^{n}(X)
is the fibre at
x
of the map of spaces
X([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\to T_{0}(X)([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
. By Corollary
4.21
the previous map of spaces is an equivalence if
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{0}
, i.e. if
([\mathbf{k}],\nu,x)\in\mathcal{T}^{n}_{0}(X)
, thus proving (1).

For (2), the condition that
\kappa^{n}(X)
sends inert morphisms in
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}
to equivalences can be rephrased as follows: for any inert morphism
f\colon([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})\to([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}
the following square of spaces is cartesian (all maps between vertical fibres are equivalences):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X([\mathbf{k}],\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{0}(X)([\mathbf{k}],\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{0}(X)([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime}).}

We may equivalently only require the squares corresponding to inert morphisms in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}
of the form
([1^{n}],1)\to([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
to be cartesian: indeed each object
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}
admits an essentially unique morphism of this special form of which it is the target.

The hypothesis that
\iota^{*}(X)
satisfies the Segal condition can be rephrased as the requirement that
T_{0}(X)\in s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}\subset s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
satisfies the
01
-Segal condition, whence we may in particular expand

T_{0}(X)([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\simeq\lim_{(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0,\mathrm{int},\leq 1})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}}X,\qquad T_{0}(X)([1^{n}],1)=\lim_{(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0,\mathrm{int},\leq 1})_{([1^{n}],1)/}}X,

and the above square, for
([\mathbf{k}^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})=([1^{n}],1)
, is cartesian if and only if
X([\mathbf{k}],\nu)\simeq\lim_{(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{01,\mathrm{int},\leq 1})_{([\mathbf{k}],\nu)/}}X
, i.e. if the
01
-Segal condition for
X
holds at
([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
.
∎

Motivated by Lemma
5.3
, we give the following definition.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
X\in s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
and
Y\in s^{n}\mathcal{S}
.
We define
\dot{\mathcal{K}}^{n}(X)
as the localisation
L(\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X),\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X)_{\mathrm{int}})\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
of
\mathcal{K}^{n}(X)
at inert morphisms (see Subsection
2.7
). Similarly, we define
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(Y):=\dot{\mathcal{K}}^{n}(\iota_{*}(Y))
, and by abuse of notation we define
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(X)
as
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\iota^{*}(X))
. Finally, we set
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)=\dot{\mathcal{K}}(\tau^{*}(Y))
.

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{\mathbb{N}}
we denote by
\dot{\kappa}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{C}))
the presheaf corresponding to
\kappa^{n}_{1}(\mathcal{C})
, which is local with respect to inert morphisms by Lemma
5.3
.
Equivalently,
\dot{\kappa}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
corresponds to the functor
\dot{\mathcal{K}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
obtained as localisation of
\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathcal{C})
, which is again a right fibration by Lemma
2.19
.

Similarly, for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
, we denote by
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}))
the presheaf corresponding to
\lambda^{n}_{1}(\mathcal{C})
, and by abuse of notation, for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{\mathbb{N}}
, we denote by
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
the presheaf
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{C}))\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}))
.

Proposition 5.5
.

We have a pullback square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\dot{\mathcal{K}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})\simeq(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n},\dot{\kappa}^{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}

Proof.

The functor
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}\colon s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
can be promoted to a functor that we still denote
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}\colon s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}\to\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, sending
X\mapsto(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(X),\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(X)_{\mathrm{int}})
, and point (1) of Lemma
5.3
gives rise to a pullback square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{n}\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}\to\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1})\simeq(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1},\kappa^{n}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}}

where the bottom arrow is the pullback of the bicartesian fibration
d_{0}\colon\mathscr{P}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
along the functor

d_{0}\colon\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}\subset\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
.

Let moreover
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}
spanned by objects
((\mathcal{C},W),A)
such that
A\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C})
is
W
-local. Then on the one hand
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}
can be identified with the following pullback on left in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)};}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}\to\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1})\simeq(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1},\kappa^{n}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}}

On the other hand point (2) of Lemma
5.3
, together with the observation that the completeness and constancy condition on
X\in s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}
only depend on
\iota^{*}(X)
, allows us to identify
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01}
as the preimage of
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}
along
(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1},\kappa^{n}_{1})
, obtaining the pullback on right. We conclude by gluing the last two pullback squares.
∎

We conclude this subsection with two basic lemmas. The first regards isomorphism classes of objects in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
, and will allow us to limit our study of mapping spaces in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
in the next two subsections. Note that for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
we have
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{n-1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\simeq\iota_{*}(\mathcal{C})([1^{n}],1)
and
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\simeq\tau^{*}(\mathcal{C})([1^{n}],1)
.

Lemma 5.6
.

For
X\in s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
, the natural map of spaces
X([1^{n}],1)\to\dot{\mathcal{K}}^{n}(X)^{\simeq}
, sending
x\mapsto([1^{n}],1,x)
, is surjective on
\pi_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
([\mathbf{k}],\nu,x)\in\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X)
; then the inert morphism
([1^{n}],1)\to([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
in
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
admits a cartesian lift
([1^{n}],1,x^{\prime})\to([\mathbf{k}],\nu,x)
in
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathcal{C})
; this morphism is inert and hence it is inverted in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
.
∎

Conjecture 5.7
.

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
the maps
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{n-1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
and
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
from Lemma
5.6
are in fact equivalences of spaces.

The second lemma is a computation of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(Y)
and
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)
for
Y\in\mathcal{S}\subset\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
Y\in\mathcal{S}
; then
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(Y),\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)\in\mathcal{S}
, and the map of spaces
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(Y)
can be identified with the restriction map
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{n},Y)^{\simeq}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{n-1},Y)^{\simeq}
, or equivalently, with the diagonal map
Y\to Y^{S^{n-1}}
.

Proof.

The functor
(\tau^{*}(Y)\to\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\tau^{*}(Y)\simeq\iota_{*}(Y))\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{\mathbb{N}}\to\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{S})
restricts to the constant functor at
(Y\to Y^{S^{n-1}})
on
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{1}
. It follows that the functor
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(Y)
is equivalent to the product functor
Y\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(*)\to Y^{S^{n-1}}\times\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(*)
. Since
\mathcal{L}^{n}_{1}(*)\simeq\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(*)\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}
has a terminal object
([1^{n}],1)
, which admits an inert map to any other object in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}
, we have that every morphism of
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}
is inverted in
L(\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1},\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1,\mathrm{int}})
, and in particular
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(*)\simeq\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(*)\simeq|\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}|\simeq*
.
∎

5.2.
Mapping spaces in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}(\mathcal{C})

In this subsection we fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
and aim at computing mapping spaces in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}(\mathcal{C})
.

Definition 5.9
.

An inert morphism
[k]\to[k^{\prime}]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
is
left inert
, respectively
right inert
, if its image is
\left\{0,\dots,k\right\}
, respectively
\left\{k^{\prime}-k+1,\dots,k^{\prime}\right\}
. For
X\in s\mathcal{S}
or
X\in s_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
, a morphism in
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(X)
is left/right inert if its projection to
\mathbf{\Delta}
is left/right inert. We use the indices “
(-)_{\mathrm{l}\mathrm{int}}
” and “
(-)_{\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}
” to denote wide subcategories spanned by left/right inert morphisms.

Recall Subsection
2.5
.
For
Y\in s\mathcal{S}
we have an isomorphism
\mathcal{T}_{0}(Y)\simeq\mathcal{T}_{0}(Y^{\mathrm{op}})
covering
i_{\mathbf{\Delta}}
; in particular we have an isomorphism
\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{l}\mathrm{int}}\simeq\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})_{\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}
.

Let
([k],\nu)\in\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
, and expand
\nu=(0,\dots,1,\dots,0)
, where the “1” is in position
1\leq i\leq k
. Consider the overcategory
(\mathbf{\Delta}_{0})_{/([k],\nu)}
, i.e. the full subcategory of
(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbb{N}})_{/([k],\nu)}
spanned by arrows with source in
\mathbf{\Delta}_{0}
; then
(\mathbf{\Delta}_{0})_{/([k],\nu)}
naturally splits as the disjoint union of two subcategories, accounting for maps
([k^{\prime}],0)\to([k],\nu)
whose underlying map
[k^{\prime}]\to[k]
has image contained in
\left\{0,\dots,i-1\right\}
or in
\left\{i,\dots,k\right\}
. Each of these subcategories has a terminal object, given by the left inert morphism
[i-1]\to[k]
and the right inert morphism
[k-i+1]\to[k]
, respectively.

Definition 5.10
.

We denote by
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{o}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\to\mathbf{\Delta}\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}_{0}
the two functors selecting the sources of the terminal objects of the two subcategories in the splitting of
(\mathbf{\Delta}_{0})_{/(-)}
. Using the notation above, we have
\mathfrak{a}([k],\nu)=[i-1]
and
\mathfrak{o}([k],\nu)=[k-i+1]
.

We denote by
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}\colon\mathfrak{a}\Rightarrow\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}}
and
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}\colon\mathfrak{o}\Rightarrow\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}}
the canonical natural transformations, attaining left, respectively right inert morphisms as values: here we consider all functors
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{o},\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}}
as functors
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}
. By abuse of notation, we also denote by
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}
the natural transformation
\tau\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}\colon\tau\mathfrak{a}\simeq\mathfrak{a}\Rightarrow\tau
of functors
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{0}
, and similarly we denote
\tau\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}
just by
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}
.

We observe that
\mathfrak{a}\times\mathfrak{o}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\to\mathbf{\Delta}\times\mathbf{\Delta}
is an equivalence of categories. Moreover, for
Y\in s\mathcal{S}
, the restriction
\iota_{*}(Y)|_{\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}}\in\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{1})
agrees with the pullback of
Y\boxtimes Y\in\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{\Delta}\times\mathbf{\Delta})
along the functor
\mathfrak{a}\times\mathfrak{o}
. Equivalently, the right fibration
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{1}(Y)\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
is the pullback of the right fibration
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y)\times\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y)\to\mathbf{\Delta}\times\mathbf{\Delta}
, so that in particular we have an equivalence of
{(\infty,1)}
-categories
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{1}(Y)\simeq\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y)\times\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y)
. Along this equivalence, the wide subcategory
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{1}(Y)_{\mathrm{int}}
corresponds to the wide subcategory
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y)_{\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}\times\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y)_{\mathrm{l}\mathrm{int}}
. It follows that
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}(Y)
is equivalent to the product of localisations
L(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y),\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y)_{\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}})\times L(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y),\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y)_{\mathrm{l}\mathrm{int}})
; and
we may rewrite the second factor as
L(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y^{\mathrm{op}}),\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y^{\mathrm{op}})_{\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}})
.

Moreover, the functor
c_{\mathbf{\Delta}}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}\times\mathbf{\Delta}\to\mathbf{\Delta}
from Subsection
2.5
can be identified with the composite
\mathbf{\Delta}\times\mathbf{\Delta}\xrightarrow{(\mathfrak{a}\times\mathfrak{o})^{-1}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}\mathbf{\Delta}
. Hence for
Y\in s\mathcal{S}
the right fibration
\mathcal{L}^{1}_{1}(Y)\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
, which is the pullback of the right fibration
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y)\to\mathbf{\Delta}
along
\tau\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\to\mathbf{\Delta}
, corresponds to the right fibration
(c_{\mathbf{\Delta}})^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y))\to\mathbf{\Delta}\times\mathbf{\Delta}
. Along the equivalence
\mathcal{L}^{1}_{1}(Y)\simeq(c_{\mathbf{\Delta}})^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y))
, the wide subcategory
\mathcal{L}^{1}_{1}(Y)_{\mathrm{int}}
corresponds to the wide subcategory of
(c_{\mathbf{\Delta}})^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(Y))
spanned by morphisms whose projection to
\mathbf{\Delta}\times\mathbf{\Delta}
is a pair of a right inert and a left inert morphism.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
; then
L(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}})\simeq\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

We start by constructing a functor
\mathbf{F}\colon\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
; equivalently, we want a functor
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}\times\mathbf{\Delta}
over
\mathbf{\Delta}
. After straightening the two cartesian fibrations
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathbf{\Delta}
and
\mathcal{C}\times\mathbf{\Delta}\to\mathbf{\Delta}
, we have two functors
\mathcal{C},\mathrm{const}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, the first taking values in
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, and we have to provide a left lax natural transformation between the two, by which we mean a functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})^{\text{l-lax}}
whose images along
d_{1}
and
d_{0}
are
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathrm{const}_{\mathcal{C}}
, respectively (see Definition
2.17
).

We start by considering the functor
\mathbf{G}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}\to\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})^{\text{r-lax}}
sending the object
[k]
to the functor
*\to[k]
,
*\mapsto k
exhibiting
*
as the maximum of
[k]
. The image along
\mathbf{G}
of a morphism
f\colon[k]\to[k^{\prime}]
in
