\mathbf{\Delta}
is forced to be the following square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[k]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[k^{\prime}]}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(k)\to k^{\prime}}

We may compose
\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{op}}
with the functor
\mathrm{Fun}(-,\mathcal{C})\colon((\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})^{\text{r-lax}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})^{\text{l-lax}}
, and then further compose with the endofunctor of the
{(\infty,1)}
-category
\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})^{\text{l-lax}}
sending
(\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
to
(\mathcal{D}^{\simeq}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
. This gives the left lax natural transformation
\mathcal{C}\Rightarrow\mathrm{const}_{\mathcal{C}}
, giving in turn the functor
\mathbf{F}\colon\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
.

By construction
\mathbf{F}
sends
([k],x)\in\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})
to
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}
, where
([0],x^{\prime})\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})
is a cartesian lift of the right inert morphism
[0]\xrightarrow{k}[k]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
. In particular
\mathbf{F}
is essentially surjective, since any
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}
is equivalent to
\mathbf{F}([0],x^{\prime})
; and
\mathbf{F}
sends right inert morphisms in
\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C})
to equivalences, inducing a functor
\hat{\mathbf{F}}\colon L(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}})\to\mathcal{C}
.

It is left to prove that
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
is fully faithful. We fix an object
\mathbf{x}=([0],x)\in\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})
and we denote by
W(\mathbf{x})
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{x}/}
spanned by right inert morphisms, and by
\pi(\mathbf{x})\colon W(\mathbf{x})\to\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{x}/}
the natural inclusion.
Let
\mathbf{y}=([k],y)
be some object in
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})
, and let
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}=([0],y^{\prime})
be the source of a cartesian lift of the right inert morphism
[0]\to[k]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
. Recall from Subsection
2.7
that
d_{0}\pi(\mathbf{x})_{!}(*)(\mathbf{y})
can be computed as
|\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}|
, where
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}
denotes the category of cospans
(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})
in
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})
with
\mathbf{x}\to\mathbf{z}
right inert.

Let us say that an object
(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})
in
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}
is
special
if
\mathbf{z}
has the form
([k+1],z)
and if the cospan
[k]\to[k+1]\leftarrow[0]
obtained by projecting to
\mathbf{\Delta}
is the unique cospan exhibiting
[k+1]
as the disjoint union of
[k]\simeq\left\{0,\dots,k\right\}\subset[k+1]
and
[0]\simeq\left\{k+1\right\}\subset[k+1]
.
Let
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}
spanned by special objects.
Then any morphism
in
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}
is an equivalence, since its projection to
\mathbf{\Delta}
is an equivalence: indeed the only map
[k+1]\to[k+1]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
which is compatible with the two given inclusions of
[k]\to[k+1]\leftarrow[0]
is
\mathrm{Id}_{[k+1]}
.
In other words,
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}
is an
\infty
-groupoid, i.e.
\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}\simeq(\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}})^{\simeq}
.
The
\infty
-groupoid core
(\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}})^{\simeq}
is a priori equivalent to the fibre at
(y,x)
of the map
\mathcal{C}([k+1])\to\mathcal{C}([k])\times\mathcal{C}([0])
induced by the two maps
[k]\to[k+1]\leftarrow[0]
, and the Segal condition for
\mathcal{C}
both implies that
(\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}})^{\simeq}\simeq\mathcal{C}(y^{\prime},x)
, and that any right inert morphism
\mathbf{t}\to\mathbf{y}
in
\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C})
induces an equivalence
(\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}})^{\simeq}\overset{\simeq}{\to}(\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{t}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}})^{\simeq}
.

We next argue that
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}
is initial in
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}
. Given an object
(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})
in
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}
lying over some cospan
[k]\to[k^{\prime}]\leftarrow[0]
, we argue that
the overcategory
(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}})_{/(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})}
is weakly contractible because it has a terminal object. To see this, we observe that the two morphisms
[k]\to[k^{\prime}]\leftarrow[0]
combine to a unique
morphism
[k+1]\simeq[k]\sqcup[0]\to[k^{\prime}]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}
, and taking a cartesian lift of
[k+1]\to[k^{\prime}]
with target
\mathbf{z}^{\prime}
gives rise to a morphism of cospans
(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})\to(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})
restricting to the identity on
\mathbf{y}
and
\mathbf{x}
: this is a terminal object in
(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}})_{/(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})}
.

The previous considerations allow us to apply Theorem
2.21
, yielding an equivalence between
\mathcal{C}(y^{\prime},x)
and the mapping space from
\mathbf{y}
to
\mathbf{x}
in
L(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{T}^{1}_{0}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{r}\mathrm{int}})
; and the same equivalence of mapping spaces is also induced by the functor
\hat{\mathbf{F}}
.
∎

Corollary 5.12
.

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
we have
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}(\mathcal{C})\simeq\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
, and the presheaf
\dot{\lambda}^{1}(\mathcal{C})\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}(\mathcal{C}))
from Definition
5.4
corresponds to
\mathcal{C}(-,-)\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})
. In other words, the right fibration
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{1}(\mathcal{C})\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}(\mathcal{C})
is equivalent to the right fibration
(d_{1},d_{0})\colon\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
from Subsection
2.5

Proof.

The construction of the functor
\mathbf{F}
from the proof of Proposition
5.11
can be adapted to obtain the following
commutative square in
\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, whose horizontal arrows are sent to right fibrations along the functor
d_{0}\colon\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{1}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{L}^{1}_{1}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{int}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{T}^{1}_{1}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{T}^{1}_{1}(\mathcal{C})_{\mathrm{int}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}),\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C})^{\simeq})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}\times\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{1},d_{0})}

We argue that the previous square is a pullback square in
\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
: to see that the image along
d_{0}\colon\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
is a pullback square, we fix
(x,x^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
and consider the object
\mathbf{x}=([1],1,(x,x^{\prime}))\in\mathcal{T}^{1}_{1}(\mathcal{C})
; we readily compute
\lambda^{1}_{1}(\mathbf{x})
, i.e. the fibre at
\mathbf{x}
of
\mathcal{L}^{1}_{1}(\mathcal{C})]\to\mathcal{T}^{1}_{1}(\mathcal{C})
, as the fibre at
(x,x^{\prime})
of the map
\tau^{*}(\mathcal{C})([1],1)\to\iota_{*}(\mathcal{C})([1],1)
: this is the map
(d_{1},d_{0})\colon\mathcal{C}([1])\to\mathcal{C}([0])^{2}
, so the required fibre is precisely
\mathcal{C}(x,x^{\prime})
, i.e. it agrees with the fibre at
(x,x^{\prime})
of
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
. For generic
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{1}_{1}(\mathcal{C})
we may find an inert morphism
\mathbf{x}\to\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
with
\mathbf{x}
as above; by Lemma
5.3
we have
\lambda^{1}_{1}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\lambda^{1}_{1}(\mathbf{x})
, and the functor
\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}
sends
\mathbf{x}\to\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
to an equivalence: this allows us to reduce the generic case of comparing the fibres of the horizontal maps to the specific one discussed above. We finally observe that the image of the above square along
d_{1}
is also a pullback square, obtained by selecting compatible wide subcategories from the image along
d_{0}
.

The horizontal arrows of the above pullback square in
\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
satisfy the assumptions of Lemma
2.19
, so that the localised square is again a pullback square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
. By Proposition
5.11
, the localisation of the right vertical functor
\mathbf{F}^{\prime\prime}
is an equivalence; it follows that the localisation of
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
is an equivalence as well.
∎

5.3.
The partial localisation
\ddot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})

In this subsection and the next we give a recursive formula for the mapping spaces of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
and
n\geq 2
. A key result that we will prove, inductively on
n
, is the following, which for
n=1
is a direct consequence of Corollary
5.12
.

Proposition 5.13
.

The functor

(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})\simeq(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n},\dot{\lambda}^{n})\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}\to\mathscr{P}

preserves finite products, i.e. it is a symmetric monoidal functor. More generally,
(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n},\dot{\lambda}^{n})
preserves pullbacks of cospan diagrams
\mathcal{C}\to Y\leftarrow\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
with
Y\in\mathcal{S}
.

We fix
n\geq 2
throughout this and the next subsection, and keep considering
s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}
as a full subcategory of
s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
via the inclusion
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})_{*}
.

For every
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{n-1}
we have a map
-\otimes-\colon\mathbb{N}^{\underline{k}}\times\mathbb{N}^{\underline{\mathbf{k}}}\to\mathbb{N}^{\underline{k}\times\underline{\mathbf{k}}}
induced by multiplication of natural numbers: explicitly, we send a pair
(\nu,\nu^{\prime})
of functions
\nu\colon\underline{k}\to\mathbb{N}
and
\nu^{\prime}\colon\underline{\mathbf{k}}\to\mathbb{N}
to the function
(i,j)\in\underline{k}\times\underline{\mathbf{k}}\mapsto\nu(i)\cdot\nu^{\prime}(j)\in\mathbb{N}
.
The maps
-\otimes-
assemble into a map of
n
-fold simplicial spaces

-\otimes-\colon\mathbf{B}\mathbb{N}\boxtimes\mathbf{B}^{n-1}\mathbb{N}\to\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}
, and taking categories of
n
-simplices we obtain a functor
-\otimes-\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{\mathbb{N}}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{\mathbb{N}}\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}
. The latter restricts to a functor
-\otimes-\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{01}\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{01}
, and further to an equivalence
-\otimes-\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{1}\overset{\simeq}{\to}\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}
.

Notation 5.14
.

For
[k]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
and
Y\in s^{n}\mathcal{S}
we denote by
Y_{[k]}=Y([k]\times-)\in s^{n-1}\mathcal{S}
the pullback of the presheaf
Y
along the functor
[k]\times-\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}
.
Similarly, for
([k],\nu)\in\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}
and
X\in s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}
we denote by
X_{([k],\nu)}:=X(([k],\nu)\otimes-)\in s^{n-1}_{01}\mathcal{S}
the pullback of
X
along
([k],\nu)\otimes-\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{01}\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{01}
.

We obtain a functor
\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}^{\mathrm{op}}\times s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}\to s^{n-1}_{01}\mathcal{S}
sending
(([k],\nu),X)\mapsto X_{([k],\nu)}
, which restricts to
\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n-1)}^{01}
. For
X\in s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}
the following hold:

•

the restricted functor
X_{(-)}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}_{0}^{\mathrm{op}}}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{0}^{\mathrm{op}}\to s^{n-1}_{01}\mathcal{S}
agrees with
\tau^{*}(\iota^{*}(X)_{(-)})
, i.e. for
[k]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
we have
X_{([k],(0,\dots,0))}\simeq\tau^{*}(\iota^{*}(X)_{[k]})
;

•

the cartesian fibration
\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X)\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
unstraightening the composite functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}\subset\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{n-1}_{01}\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(-)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}}

is the composite of the right fibration
\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X)\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}
and the projection on the first factor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}\simeq(\mathbf{\Delta}_{1})^{n}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
.

Lemma 5.15
.

Recall Definitions
5.2
and
5.10
. Let
X\in s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}
be
0
-truncated, i.e.
X\simeq T_{0}(X)
. Then the following square in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1},\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
,
whose arrows are induced by
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}},\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}
and by the unit of the adjunction
\iota^{*}\dashv\iota_{*}
, is cartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(X_{(-)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(X_{\mathfrak{a}(-)})\times\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(X_{\mathfrak{o}(-)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(X_{(-)}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(X_{\mathfrak{a}(-)}))\times\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(X_{\mathfrak{o}(-)})).}

Proof.

It suffices to fix
\mathbf{x}=([k],\nu)\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}
and prove that the square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
obtained by evaluation at
\mathbf{x}
is cartesian.
This square admits a canonical map to the following other square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, which is obviously cartesian; the mentioned canonical map is pointwise a right fibration:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{1}\times\mathbf{\Delta}^{n-1}_{1}.}
diagonal
diagonal

It is therefore sufficient to fix
\mathbf{y}=([\mathbf{k}],\nu^{\prime})\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n-1}_{1}
and prove that the square of spaces formed by the fibres at
\mathbf{y}
, respectively at
(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y})
, of the above four right fibrations is cartesian. The resulting square of spaces is the following:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\mathbf{y})\times X(\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\mathbf{y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\in(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n-1}_{0})_{\mathbf{y}/}}X(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\in(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n-1}_{0})_{\mathbf{y}/}}\begin{array}[]{c}X(\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\times\\
X(\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\mathbf{y}^{\prime}).\end{array}}

The pullback of the right-bottom cospan in the previous square admits the following interpretation. Let
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}^{\mathrm{special}}
denote the full subcategory of
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}
spanned by those arrows
\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{01}
such that, along the composite
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{01}\xrightarrow{\tau}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0}\simeq(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}
, the image of
\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}
is either
\mathrm{Id}_{[k]}
or the left inert morphism
[k]\to\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})
or the right inert morphism
[k]\to\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})
. Then the pullback of the right-bottom cospan in the above square agrees with the limit of
X
over
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}^{\mathrm{special}}
. More precisely, we have an immediate identification after rewriting the top right corner as the limit over the category
(\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}^{\mathrm{op},n-1})_{\mathbf{y}/}
(which has
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{y}}
as initial object) of the functor
X(\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})\otimes-)\times X(\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})\otimes-)
.

We next argue that
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}^{\mathrm{special}}
is initial in
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}
. We fix
(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z})\in(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}
and claim that the overcategory
((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}^{\mathrm{special}})_{/(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z})}
is weakly contractible; we expand
\mathbf{z}=[k_{\mathbf{z}}]\otimes[\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{z}}]
with
[k_{\mathbf{z}}]\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}
and
[\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{z}}]\in(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0}
. We say that a factorisation
(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}\overset{\text{special}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{z})\in((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}^{\mathrm{special}})_{/(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z})}
is
very special
if the morphism
\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{z}
is sent to the identity of
[\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{z}}]
along the composite
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{01}\xrightarrow{\tau}\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0}\simeq(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\to(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n-1}
ending with the projection on the last
n-1
factors.
Then on the one hand the full subcategory
((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}^{\mathrm{special}})_{/(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z})}^{\mathrm{veryspecial}}
spanned by very special factorisations is final in
((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}^{\mathrm{special}})_{/(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z})}
; and on the other hand
((\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}^{\mathrm{special}})_{/(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z})}^{\mathrm{veryspecial}}
is isomorphic to either of the following weakly contractible categories:

•

it is isomorphic to
[1]
if the morphism
[k]\to[k_{\mathbf{z}}]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}
lifts to a morphism
\mathbf{x}\to([k_{\mathbf{z}}],0)
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}
, and the morphism
[\mathbf{k}]\to[\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{z}}]
in
(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n-1}
lifts to a morphism
\mathbf{y}\to[\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{z}}]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n-1}_{01}
;

•

it is isomorphic to
[0]
otherwise.

It follows that the last square of spaces is indeed cartesian, since the assumption that
X
be
0
-truncated implies that
X(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y})
is equivalent to
\lim_{(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op},n}_{0})_{\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y}/}}X
.
∎

Corollary 5.16
.

Let
Y\in s^{n}\mathcal{S}
; then the straightening of the cartesian fibration
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(Y)\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
is the functor

\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
sending
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}
to the pullback of the following cospan

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})})\times\mathcal{L}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\tau(\mathbf{x})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})})\times\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{x})})\times\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}(\mathbf{x})})}

i.e. sending
\mathbf{x}
to the source of the cartesian fibration over
\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\tau(\mathbf{x})})
corresponding to the pullback of the presheaf
\lambda^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})})\boxtimes\lambda^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})})
along the functor

\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{x})})\times\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}(\mathbf{x})})
.

Proof.

We apply Lemma
5.15
to
\iota_{*}(Y)
, and obtain after evaluation at
\mathbf{x}
the following pullback square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(\iota_{*}(Y)_{\mathbf{x}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})})\times\mathcal{L}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\tau(\mathbf{x})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})})\times\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})}).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{x})})\times\mathcal{T}^{n-1}_{1}(Y_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}(\mathbf{x})})}

Here we have used that for any
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}^{\mathrm{op}}
and any
X\in s^{n}_{01}\mathcal{S}
we have an equivalence
\iota^{*}(X_{\mathbf{y}})\simeq\iota^{*}(X)_{\tau(\mathbf{y})}
; and that if moreover
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}
we also have an equivalence
X_{\mathbf{y}}\simeq\tau^{*}(\iota^{*}(X)_{\mathbf{y}})
. Specifically, we have set
X=\iota_{*}(Y)
and
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{x},\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x}),\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})
, and we also have used the equivalence
\iota^{*}\iota_{*}\overset{\simeq}{\to}\mathrm{Id}_{s^{n}\mathcal{S}}
.

We conclude by recalling that the straightening of
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(X)\simeq\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(\iota_{*}(X))\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
evaluates
\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(\iota_{*}(X)_{\mathbf{x}})
, i.e. the top left corner, at
\mathbf{x}
.
∎

Definition 5.17
.

Let
X\in s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
and
Y\in s^{n}\mathcal{S}
. A morphism in
\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X)
is
vertically inert
if it is inert and its image along the composite
\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X)\to\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\mathbf{\Delta}
is an identity in
\mathbf{\Delta}
. We use the index “
(-)_{\mathrm{v}\mathrm{int}}
” to denote wide subcategories spanned by vertically inert morphisms.
We define
\ddot{\mathcal{K}}^{n}(X):=L(\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X),\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X)_{\mathrm{v}\mathrm{int}})
. We define
\ddot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(Y):=\ddot{\mathcal{K}}^{n}(\iota_{*}(Y))
, and by abuse of notation we also define
\ddot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(X)
as
\ddot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\iota^{*}(X))
. Finally, we set
\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y):=\ddot{\mathcal{K}}^{n}(\tau^{*}(Y))
.

Lemma 5.18
.

For
X\in s^{n}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}
, the localised functor
\ddot{\mathcal{K}}^{n}(X)\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
is a cartesian fibration corresponding to the functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
sending
\mathbf{x}\mapsto\dot{\mathcal{K}}^{n-1}(X_{\mathbf{x}})
.

Proof.

We apply Lemma
2.18

to the
relative category
(\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X),\mathcal{K}^{n}_{1}(X)_{\mathrm{v}\mathrm{int}})
, which is obtained by unstraightening the functor
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
sending

\mathbf{x}\mapsto(\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(X_{\mathbf{x}}),\mathcal{K}^{n-1}_{1}(X_{\mathbf{x}})_{\mathrm{int}}).

∎

Corollary 5.19
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
; then the localised functor
\ddot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
is again a cartesian fibration, and it corresponds to the functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
sending
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}
to the
pullback of the following cospan

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})})\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\tau(\mathbf{x})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})})\times\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{x})})\times\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}(\mathbf{x})})}

i.e. sending
\mathbf{x}
to the
source of the cartesian fibration over
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\tau(\mathbf{x})})
corresponding to the pullback of the presheaf
\dot{\lambda}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x})})\boxtimes\dot{\lambda}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})})
along the functor
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{x})})\times\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}(\mathbf{x})})
.

Proof.

The pullback square from the proof of Corollary
5.16
can be enhanced, naturally in
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}
, to a pullback square in
\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
by endowing each category with its wide subcategory of inert morphisms; the vertical arrows are then right fibrations fitting into the hypotheses of Lemma
2.19
: for the right vertical arrow we invoke Lemma
5.3
(here we use that
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
), and for the left vertical arrow we use that the square is a pullback. Lemma
2.19
allows us to localise the square pointwise at inert morphisms, obtaining again a pullback square whose vertical arrows are right fibrations. The localised top left corner is then by Lemma
5.18
the functor
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
straightening the cartesian fibration
\ddot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
.
∎

We can make the statement of Corollary
5.19
more explicit as follows.

Definition 5.20
.

Let
\partial(\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}^{\mathrm{op}}\times[1])\subset\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\times[1]
denote the full subcategory

\partial(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\times[1]):=(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}\times[1])\cup(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\times\left\{1\right\}).

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
we denote by
\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}\times[1]\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
the adjoint of the composite functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n-1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}\subset\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{(-)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1})}

and we denote by
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}\times[1]\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
the right Kan extension to
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\times[1]
of the restriction to
\partial(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\times[1])
of
\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
. For
j=0,1
we let
\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{j},\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{j}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
denote the restriction of
\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C}),\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})
to
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\times\left\{j\right\}\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}
.

Corollary
5.19
then identifies the straightening of
\ddot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
with the restriction
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
. By Lemma
5.18
we can similarly identify the straightening of
\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
with the restriction
\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}}
; the left fibration
\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\to\ddot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
corresponds to the unit of the adjunction
\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C})_{0}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}}\to\Gamma(\mathcal{C})_{0}|_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}}
.

Lemma 5.21
.

Assume that Proposition
5.13
holds for
n-1
, and let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
. Then the functors
\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0},\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
satisfy the
01
-Segal condition, i.e. their restriction to
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01,\mathrm{int}}
are right Kan extended from
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01,\mathrm{int},\leq 1}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
, we have that
\mathcal{C}_{[0]}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n-1)}
is a space. The restrictions of both
\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
and
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})
to
\left\{([0],0)\right\}\times[1]\simeq[1]
give the arrow
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{[0]})\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{[0]})
in
\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
, which by Lemma
5.8
can be identified with the diagonal arrow of spaces
\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}\to(\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})^{S^{n-2}}
.

We next observe that both restrictions of
\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}
and
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}
to
\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}\simeq\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}
are equivalent to
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{(-)})
; and given
[k]\in\mathbf{\Delta}
, we may identify

\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{[k]})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}\left(\mathcal{C}_{[1]}\underset{\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}{\times}\dots\underset{\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}{\times}\mathcal{C}_{[1]}\right)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})\underset{\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}{\times}\dots\underset{\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}{\times}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]}),

using the Segal condition for
\mathcal{C}_{(-)}
and the hypothesis that
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}
preserves pullbacks over spaces. Recalling that
\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C})_{0}
is obtained from
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{(-)})
by precomposing with
\tau
, we immediately get the
01
-Segal condition for
\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C})_{0}
.

The formula for right Kan extension allows us to compute
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)
as the following pullback in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([0],0)\times\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([0],0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}\times\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{1}([1],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{1}([0],0)\times\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{1}([0],0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})^{S^{n-2}}\times(\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})^{S^{n-2}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\to 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{1},d_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\to 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{1},d_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

For generic
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}
, we can then compute
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}(\mathbf{x})
as the following pullback square, in terms of
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})|_{\partial(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\times[1])}\simeq\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C})|_{\partial(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{01}\times[1])}
and of
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}(\mathbf{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}(\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x}))\underset{\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}{\times}\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)\underset{\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}{\times}\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}(\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{1}(\mathbf{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{1}(\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{x}))\underset{(\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})^{S^{n-2}}}{\times}\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{1}([1],1)\underset{(\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})^{S^{n-2}}}{\times}\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{1}(\mathfrak{o}(\mathbf{x})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\to 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\to 1}

We now observe that the bottom row in the last square is an equivalence, since
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{1}
agrees with
\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{1}
, and hence with
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}(\mathcal{C}_{\tau(-)})
: here we use that
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n-1}
preserves pullbacks over spaces. Hence the top row in the last square is also an equivalence, and this readily implies the
01
-Segal condition for
\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}
at
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}_{1}
.
∎

5.4.
Mapping spaces in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
for
n\geq 2

Definition 5.22
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
be a cartesian fibration. We say that a morphism in
\mathcal{D}
is
horizontally inert
if it is a cartesian lift of an inert morphism in
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
. We let
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}
denote the wide subcategory of
\mathcal{D}
spanned by horizontally inert morphisms.

We remark that in the cases
\mathcal{D}=\ddot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(Y)
and
\mathcal{D}=\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)
for some
Y\in s^{n}\mathcal{S}
, the localisation
L(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}})
recovers
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(Y)
and
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)
, respectively. To save notation, we fix in the rest of the subsection a cartesian fibration
\mathcal{E}\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}
, whose associated functor
\gamma\colon\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
satisfies the
01
-Segal condition, and whose value
\gamma_{0}:=\gamma([0],0)
is a space. The examples to keep in mind are of course
\gamma=\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C})_{0}
and
\gamma=\Gamma^{n}(\mathcal{C})_{0}
for some
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
. We denote henceforth by
\mathcal{D}:=\mathcal{E}\times_{\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}}\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
the restricted cartesian fibration. Our goal is to compute mapping spaces in
L(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}})
, and we start with the observation that, by same argument used in the proof of Lemma
5.6
, for every object
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}
we can find an object
\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{D}
lying over
([1],1)\in\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
such that
\mathbf{x}
and
\mathbf{y}
become equivalent inside
L(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}})
.

We fix therefore
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}
of the form
([1],1,x)
, i.e.
x
is an object in
\gamma([1],1)
, and we aim at computing mapping spaces into
\mathbf{x}
in
L(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}})
.

Definition 5.23
.

We define
W(\mathbf{x})
as the full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{x}/}
spanned by horizontally inert morphisms, and let
\pi(\mathbf{x})\colon W(\mathbf{x})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{/\mathbf{x}}
denote the natural inclusion.

We fix
\mathbf{y}=([k],\nu,y)\in\mathcal{D}
, i.e.,
y\in\gamma([k],\nu)
, and aim at computing the space
(d_{0}\pi(\mathbf{x}))_{!}(*)(\mathbf{y})\simeq|\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}|
, where
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}
denotes the category of cospans
\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}\leftarrow\mathbf{x}
in
\mathcal{D}
with
\mathbf{x}\to\mathbf{z}
horizontally inert.

Notation 5.24
.

We denote by
\mathbf{y}_{\mathfrak{a}}=(\mathfrak{a}([k],\nu),y_{\mathfrak{a}})\to\mathbf{y}
,
\mathbf{y}_{\mathfrak{o}}=(\mathfrak{o}([k],\nu),y_{\mathfrak{o}})\to\mathbf{y}
and
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}=([1],1,y^{\prime})\to\mathbf{y}
cartesian lifts in
\mathcal{E}
with target
\mathbf{y}
of the morphisms
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}([k],\nu)
,
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}([k],\nu)
and of the inert morphism

in
\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}
, respectively. We further denote by
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{a}}=([0],0,y^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{a}})\to\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{o}}=([0],0,y^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{o}})\to\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
cartesian lifts at
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
of
d_{1}=\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}([1],1)
and
d_{0}=\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}([1],1)
, respectively.

Notation 5.25
.

For
([k^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})\in\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
we abbreviate the object
([k^{\prime}+2],(0,\nu^{\prime},0))\in\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
by
([k^{\prime}+2],0\nu^{\prime}0)
, and we denote by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([k^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([k+2],0\nu^{\prime}0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([1],1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{o}}(\nu^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i(\nu^{\prime})}

\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
with

\mathbf{\Delta}
along

as the disjoint union of the sets

and

.

Note that
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}([k^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})

as the concatenation of

”.

Definition 5.26
.

A cospan
(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}
is
special
if:

(i)

the image of
\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}
along
\mathcal{D}\to\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
is
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{o}}(\nu)
;

(ii)

the composite morphisms
\mathbf{y}_{\mathfrak{a}}\to\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}
and
\mathbf{y}_{\mathfrak{o}}\to\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}
are cartesian lifts of their projections
\mathfrak{a}([k],\nu)\to[k+2]
and
\mathfrak{o}([k],\nu)\to[k+2]
to
\mathbf{\Delta}_{01}
.

We denote by
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}\subset\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}
the full subcategory of special objects, and by
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}
the full subcategory spanned by objects that satisfy (i).

Lemma 5.27
.

The following hold:

(1)

\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}
is initial in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}
;

(2)

a horizontally inert morphism
\mathbf{t}\to\mathbf{y}
in
\mathcal{D}
induces an equivalence

\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}\overset{\simeq}{\to}\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{t}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}.

Proof.

For (1), we first prove that
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}^{\prime}}
is initial in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}
: to see this, we observe that for an object
(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}
lying over a cospan in
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
of the form
([k],\nu)\to([k^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})\leftarrow([1],1)
, the overcategory
(\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}^{\prime}})_{/(\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathbf{x})}
admits a terminal object
\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}\leftarrow\mathbf{x}
, obtained by letting
\mathbf{z}\to\mathbf{z}^{\prime}
be a cartesian lift of the unique morphism
([k+2],0\nu 0)\to([k^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
making the following diagram in
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([k],\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([k+2],0\nu 0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([1],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([k^{\prime}],\nu^{\prime}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{o}}(\nu)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i(\nu)}
inert

We next argue that
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}
is initial in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}^{\prime}}
.
The
01
-Segal condition for
\gamma
allows us to write a commutative diagram as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([1],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([1],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([k+2],0\nu 0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\mathfrak{a}([k],\nu))\times_{\gamma_{0}}\gamma([3],010)\times_{\gamma_{0}}\gamma(\mathfrak{o}([k],\nu))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([k],\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\mathfrak{a}([k],\nu))\times_{\gamma_{0}}\gamma([1],1)\times_{\gamma_{0}}\gamma(\mathfrak{o}([k],\nu))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([k],\nu)_{y/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\mathfrak{a}([k],\nu))_{y_{\mathfrak{a}}/}\times_{(\gamma_{0})_{y^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{a}}/}}\gamma([1],1)_{y^{\prime}/}\times_{(\gamma_{0})_{y^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{o}}/}}\gamma(\mathfrak{o}([k],\nu))_{y_{\mathfrak{o}}/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{o}}(\nu))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(i(\nu))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}\times\gamma(\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{o}}(1))\times\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(i(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}\times d_{0}\times d_{0}}

Note that since
\gamma_{0}
is a space, overcategories of
\gamma_{0}
are contractible and hence the bottom right term in the previous diagram is a plain product of
{(\infty,1)}
-categories.

Using the left column in the previous diagram we can identify
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}^{\prime}}
with the limit of the following W-shaped diagram in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([k+2],0\nu 0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([k],\nu)_{y/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([1],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([k],\nu),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(i(\nu))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{o}}(\nu))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}

whereas
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}
is equivalent to the fibre at
(\mathrm{Id}_{y_{\mathfrak{a}}},\mathrm{Id}_{y_{\mathfrak{o}}})
of the composite functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([k],\nu)_{y/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\mathfrak{a}([k],\nu))_{y_{\mathfrak{a}}/}\times\gamma(\mathfrak{o}([k],\nu))_{y_{\mathfrak{o}}/}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}}([k],\nu))\times}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\epsilon_{\mathfrak{o}}([k],\nu))}

Using the right column, we can then give fibre product decompositions:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*\quad\quad\times\!\left(\!*\!\!\underset{\gamma([1],1)}{\times}\!\gamma([3],010)\underset{\gamma([1],1)}{\times}\!\gamma([1],1)_{y^{\prime}/}\!\right)\!\times\quad\quad*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}^{\prime}}\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\mathfrak{a}([k],\nu))_{y_{\mathfrak{a}}/}\!\times\!\!\left(\!*\!\!\underset{\gamma([1],1)}{\times}\!\gamma([3],010)\underset{\gamma([1],1)}{\times}\!\gamma([1],1)_{y^{\prime}/}\!\right)\!\!\times\!\gamma(\mathfrak{o}([k],\nu))_{y_{\mathfrak{o}}/},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}_{y_{\mathfrak{a}}}\times(\mathrm{Id})\times\mathrm{Id}_{y_{\mathfrak{o}}}}

where the fibre products in the parentheses are taken once along
\gamma(i(1))
and once along
\gamma(\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{o}}(1))
; since a product of initial functors is initial, and
\mathrm{Id}_{y_{\mathfrak{a}}}
and
\mathrm{Id}_{y_{\mathfrak{o}}}
are initial objects in their respective overcategories,

\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}
is initial in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}^{\prime}}
.

For (2), the top equivalence in the previous diagram is nothing but the functor
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{special}}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{special}}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}
induced by the horizontally inert morphism
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{y}
. If
\mathbf{t}\to\mathbf{y}
is horizontally inert, then we have a composition of horizontally inert morphisms
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{t}\to\mathbf{y}
and we conclude by “two out of three”.
∎

Corollary 5.28
.

For objects
\mathbf{x}=([1],1,x)
and
\mathbf{y}=([1],1,y)
in
\mathcal{D}
, the morphism space
L(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}})(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
is equivalent to the classifying space
|\Xi(\gamma,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|
of the limit
\Xi(\gamma,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
of the following W-shaped diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([3],010)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([1],1)_{y/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([1],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma([1],1).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(i(1))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(\epsilon_{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{o}}(1))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}

Proof.

By Lemma
5.27
, Theorem
2.21
is applicable and we can identify the spaces

L(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}})(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\simeq|\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}|\simeq|\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}|,

and the proof of
5.27
identifies
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{y}/,\mathbf{x}/\mathrm{h}\mathrm{int}}^{\mathrm{special}}\simeq\Xi(\gamma,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.13
.

We assume that the statement holds for
n-1
and aim at proving it for a fixed
n\geq 2
. We first prove that
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}
preserves pullbacks over spaces. Let therefore
\mathcal{C}\to Y\leftarrow\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
be a cospan in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
with
Y\in\mathcal{S}
. By Lemma
5.6
, the rows in the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}\Big(\mathcal{C}\underset{Y}{\times}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\Big)([1^{n}],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\Big(\mathcal{C}\underset{Y}{\times}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\Big)^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(\mathcal{C})([1^{n}],1)\underset{Y}{\times}\tau^{*}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})([1^{n}],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\underset{Y}{\times}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\right)^{\simeq}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

are surjective on
\pi_{0}
, implying that the right vertical arrow is also surjective on
\pi_{0}
; in other words, the canonical functor
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}\times_{Y}\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\to\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\times_{\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
is essentially surjective.
To prove full faithfulness, we first use the inductive hypothesis to obtain that the canonical map
\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}\times_{Y}\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\to\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\times_{\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)}\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
is an equivalence of cartesian fibrations over
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
.
Now let
\mathbf{x}=([1],1,x)
and
\mathbf{y}=([1],1,y)
be objects in
\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}\times_{Y}\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
, with
x,y\in\hat{\Gamma}^{n}(\mathcal{C}\times_{Y}\mathcal{C}^{\prime})_{0}([1],1)
. Denote by
\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{C}},\mathbf{x}_{Y},\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}
the images of
\mathbf{x}
in
\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)
,
\hat{\Gamma}(Y)_{0}([1],1)
and
\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})_{0}([1],1)
, and define similarly
\mathbf{y}_{\mathcal{C}},\mathbf{y}_{Y},\mathbf{y}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}
. We may then write a sequence of equivalences of spaces

\begin{split}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}\times_{Y}\mathcal{C}^{\prime})(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})&\simeq|\Xi(\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C}\times_{Y}\mathcal{C}^{\prime})_{0},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|\\
&\simeq|\Xi(\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C})_{0},\mathbf{y}_{\mathcal{C}},\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{C}})\times_{\Xi(\hat{\Gamma}(Y)_{0},\mathbf{y}_{Y},\mathbf{x}_{Y})}\Xi(\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})_{0},\mathbf{y}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}},\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}|\\
&\simeq|\Xi(\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C})_{0},\mathbf{y}_{\mathcal{C}},\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{C}})|\times_{|\Xi(\hat{\Gamma}(Y)_{0},\mathbf{y}_{Y},\mathbf{x}_{Y})|}|\Xi(\hat{\Gamma}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})_{0},\mathbf{y}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}},\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}})|\\
&\simeq\Big(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\times_{\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\Big)(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x});\end{split}

the first and fourth equivalences are given by Corollary
5.28
;
the second equivalence comes from the formula for
\Xi
as an iterated limit, and the fact that limits commute with limits; the third equivalence comes from the fact that
\Xi(\hat{\Gamma}(Y)_{0},\mathbf{y}_{Y},\mathbf{x}_{Y})
is a space, and taking classifying spaces commutes with fibre products over a space. This proves that the canonical functor
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}\times_{Y}\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\to\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\times_{\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(Y)}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
is fully faithful, and therefore it is and equivalence of
{(\infty,1)}
-categories.

The proof that
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}
preserves pullbacks over spaces is completely analogous: we just replace “
\tau^{*}
” by “
\iota_{*}
” and “
Y
” by “
Y^{S^{n-1}}
” in the first diagram above, and we replace “
\hat{\Gamma}
” by “
\Gamma
” in the rest of the argument.
∎

5.5.
A symmetric monoidal square

The pullback square from Proposition
5.5
is not a square in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}})
, for instance because the left vertical functor
(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n},\dot{\kappa}^{n})
is not endowed with a symmetric monoidal structure. In this subsection we introduce an equivalent pullback square admitting a lift to
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}})
.

In the following, let
M
be an ungroup-like abelian monoid. Consider the functor
(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}\to\mathscr{P}
, which is symmetric monoidal (it preserves finite products) by Proposition
5.13
. The terminal algebra
*\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
is sent to the terminal algebra
(*\to*)\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P})
, and
\mathbf{B}^{n}M\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
is sent along
(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})
to some commutative algebra
(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P})
, about which we make the following conjecture.

Conjecture 5.29
.

For
X\in\mathrm{Top}
, denote by
\mathrm{SP}(X,M)
the free abelian (strictly commutative) topological monoid generated by
X
many copies of
M
, i.e. with the following universal property: for any abelian topological monoid
Y
we have a bijection of sets, naturally in
Y
:

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{TopAbMon}}(\mathrm{SP}(X,M),Y)\cong\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{Top}}(X,\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{AbMon}}(M,Y)).

Then we have an equivalence
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)\simeq\mathbf{B}(\mathrm{SP}(S^{n-1},M))
, and the right fibration
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
corresponds to the action of
\mathrm{SP}(S^{n-1},M)
on
M
induced by the topological monoid homomorphism
\mathrm{SP}(S^{n-1},M)\to\mathrm{SP}(*,M)\cong M
.

We will content ourselves with a milder analysis of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
and
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
.
We observe that the fibre of the right fibration
(\mathcal{L}^{n}_{1}\to\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1})(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
at any object
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
is
\lambda^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)(\mathbf{x})\simeq M
; the same holds for the localised right fibration
(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
; in particular, taking the fibre at the monoidal unit of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
, we obtain the following commutative diagram in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
with right fibrations as vertical arrows, and featuring a pullback square on right:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([1^{n}],1,*)}

Now let
(\mathbf{E}M\to\mathbf{B}M)\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P})
denote the right fibration corresponding to the action of
M
on itself, and note that both
\mathbf{E}M
and
\mathbf{B}M
are plain 1-categories.

Definition 5.30
.

Let
M
be an ungroup-like abelian monoid.
For an object
\mathbf{x}=([\mathbf{k}],\nu,x)\in\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
, we consider the set of
(\prod_{i=1}^{n}k_{i})-1
inert morphisms
([1^{n}],0)\to([\mathbf{k}],\nu)
in
\mathbf{\Delta}^{n}_{01}
, take for each of these a cartesian lift
([1^{n}],0,y)
with
y\in M
, and define the total grading of
\mathbf{x}
as the sum of the
(\prod_{i=1}^{n}k_{i})-1
elements
y
thus obtained. We denote by

\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)\to\mathbf{B}M

the functor sending each object to
*\in\mathbf{B}M
, and sending a morphism
f\colon\mathbf{x}\to\mathbf{y}
in
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
to the total grading of the essentially unique object
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
for which there is a commutative diagram in
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([1^{n}],1,*)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{z}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
inert
active
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
inert

We similarly define a functor
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{L}}\colon\mathcal{L}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)\to\mathbf{E}M
, sending an object
([\mathbf{k}],\nu,x)
to the element
y\in M
such that
([1^{n}],1,y)\to([\mathbf{k}],\nu,x)
is an inert morphism, and whose behaviour on morphisms is dictated by the existence of the
pullback square on left in the following:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}M;}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{T}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}M.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{d}}

We denote by
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{T}}
also the induced functors on localisations, as in the square on right, which by virtue of Lemma
2.19
is again a pullback.

A direct inspection shows that both localised functors
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{L}},\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{T}}
are symmetric monoidal: since
\mathbf{E}M
and
\mathbf{B}M
are plain categories, this is a property. In fact the second pullback square in Definition
5.30
is one in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
.

Remark 5.31
.

Given an
M
-graded
{(\infty,n)}
-category
\mathcal{C}
, the map
\mathfrak{d}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathbf{B}^{n}M
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}
gives rise to a map
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathfrak{d})\colon\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
, which composed with
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{T}}
gives an
M
-grading on the
{(\infty,1)}
-category
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
.

Now suppose that we have a presheaf
A\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}))
and a map
(\mathcal{C},A)\to(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n},\dot{\lambda}^{n})(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)=(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})(\mathbf{B}^{n}M)
in
\mathscr{P}
. Then composing with
(\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{L}},\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{T}})
we obtain a
twisted
M
-grading
on
A
in the following sense: for each
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{C}
we have the datum of a map of spaces
A(\mathbf{x})\to M
, which we call an
M
-grading
on the space
A(\mathbf{x})
; these
M
-gradings satisfy that for each
f\colon\mathbf{x}\to\mathbf{y}
in
\mathcal{C}
,
x\in A(\mathbf{x})
and
y\in A(\mathbf{y})
with
A(f)(y)\simeq x
we have that the
M
-grading of
x
is the sum of the
M
-grading of
y
and that of
f
. Note that this structure on
A
is different than that of
M
-graded presheaf, i.e. a lift of
A
to a functor
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{S}_{/M}
: in the latter case we would just have that the
M
-gradings of
x
and
y
are equal.

Notation 5.32
.

For
M=\mathbb{N}
, we let
\hat{\iota}
and
\varphi
denote the two morphisms fitting in the following composition in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P})
, which is the result of the previous discussion:

(*\to*)\xrightarrow{\hat{\iota}}(\mathbb{N}\to*)\xrightarrow{\varphi}(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})\xrightarrow{(\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{L}},\mathfrak{d}_{\mathcal{T}})}(\mathbf{E}\mathbb{N}\to\mathbf{B}\mathbb{N}).

We observe that
\varphi
and
\mathfrak{d}
are cartesian lifts of their projections along
d_{0}\colon\mathscr{P}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, and that
\varphi\hat{\iota}
recovers the morphism
(*\to*)\to(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P})
obtained by applying the symmetric monoidal functor

(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})
to the morphism
\mathbf{B}^{n}\iota\colon\mathbf{B}^{n}0\simeq*\to\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
.
Passing to overcategories, we obtain a sequence of lax symmetric monoidal functors:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{\mathbb{N}}\simeq(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})_{/\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{/(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{/(\mathbb{N}\to*)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathbb{N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}^{*}}

The first functor
\dot{\mathcal{L}}\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathbb{N}}
is induced on overcategories over commutative algebras by the symmetric monoidal functor
(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})
, and it is also symmetric monoidal. The other two functors

\varphi^{*}
and
\hat{\iota}^{*}
, which take pullbacks in
\mathscr{P}
along
\varphi
and
\hat{\iota}
, respectively, are a priori only lax symmetric monoidal, as their left adjoints
\varphi_{!}
and
\hat{\iota}_{!}
, given by postcomposition with
\varphi
and
\hat{\iota}
, are symmetric monoidal.

Lemma 5.33
.

Consider
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})
as an
\mathbb{N}
-graded
{(\infty,1)}
-category via the
\mathbb{N}
-grading
\mathfrak{d}
constructed above. Then the functor
*\simeq\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(*)\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})
restricts to an equivalence of
{(\infty,1)}
-categories
*\xrightarrow{\simeq}\iota^{*}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}))
.

Proof.

Consider also
\mathfrak{d}\colon\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})\to\mathbf{B}\mathbb{N}
as an
\mathbb{N}
-graded
{(\infty,1)}
-category.
Recall Definition
4.25
, and let
I=\left\{0\right\}\subset\mathbb{N}
. The counit of the adjunction
\iota_{!}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathbb{N}}\colon\iota^{*}
gives rise to the following morphism in
\mathrm{Rel}\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathbb{N}}
:

(\iota_{!}\iota^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})),\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})_{\mathrm{int}})\to(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}),\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})_{\mathrm{int}}).

This morphism satisfies the hypotheses of Lemma
4.26
, so we have an equivalence

T_{0}(L(\iota_{!}\iota^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})),\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})_{\mathrm{int}}))\xrightarrow{\simeq}T_{0}(L(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}),\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})_{\mathrm{int}})).

The counit of the adjunction
\iota^{*}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathbb{N}}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}\colon\iota_{*}
induces an equivalence
\iota^{*}T_{0}\simeq\iota^{*}\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\iota^{*}
, using that the unit of the same adjunction is an equivalence. Using this, and applying
\iota^{*}
to the previous equivalence, we obtain a new equivalence involving
\iota^{*}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}))
on the right hand side; therefore we reduce to the computation of the new left hand side, namely

\iota^{*}(L(\iota_{!}\iota^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})),\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})_{\mathrm{int}}))\simeq L(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})),\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})_{\mathrm{int}}).

We next argue that every morphism
f
in
\iota^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}))
is inverted if we localise at inert morphisms. The commutative square used to define the
\mathbb{N}
-grading
\mathfrak{d}
on
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})
, together with the assumption that
f
has vanishing
\mathbb{N}
-grading, allows us to only check that the corresponding active morphism of the form
f^{\prime}\colon([1^{n}],1,*)\to\mathbf{z}
in
\iota^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}))
is inverted; note that the
\mathbb{N}
-grading of
f^{\prime}
is also zero. We observe that
\mathbf{z}
itself must have vanishing total
\mathbb{N}
-grading, i.e.
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}0)\subset\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})
, so that there exists a retraction
g\colon\mathbf{z}\to([1^{n}],1,*)
of
f^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}0)
, and hence also in
\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})
. The composition of
g
with the inert morphism
([1^{n}],1,*)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{z}
is forced to be the identity of
([1^{n}],1,*)
, so that inverting inert morphisms forces us to invert
g
, which forces us to invert
f^{\prime}
, which forces us to invert
f
.

We have therefore
L(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})),\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})_{\mathrm{int}})\simeq|\iota^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})))|\in\mathcal{S}
; to prove that the latter space is contractible, we observe that the full subcategory of
\iota^{*}(\mathcal{T}^{n}_{1}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})))
spanned by objects of vanishing total
\mathbb{N}
-grading is initial, and that

([1^{n}],1,*)
is terminal in this full subcategory.
∎

Lemma 5.34
.

The functors
\varphi^{*}
and
\hat{\iota}^{*}
are symmetric monoidal.

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}),(\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{P}_{/(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})}
, and consider a commutative diagram as the following, in which
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
and the right square is a pullback square by Lemma
5.33
and by the fact that
\mathbb{N}
is ungroup-like:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*\times*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})\times\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Then
\mathcal{E}
is a pullback of the left square if and only if it is a pullback of the composite square; a priori, the first pullback computes
d_{0}(\varphi^{*}(\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\otimes\varphi^{*}(\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))
, whereas the second computes
d_{0}(\varphi^{*}((\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\otimes(\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime})))
. Similarly, we may consider a commutative diagram as the following, in which the right square is a pullback square by a standard argument combining the previous pullback square on the right with the pullback square defining
\varphi
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Again we observe that
\mathcal{E}
is a pullback of the left square if and only if it is a pullback of the composite square; by identifying the two pullbacks we obtain an equivalence
d_{1}(\varphi^{*}(\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\otimes\varphi^{*}(\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))\simeq d_{1}(\varphi^{*}((\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\otimes(\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime})))
. This proves that
\varphi^{*}
is symmetric monoidal.
Similarly,
d_{0}(\hat{\iota}^{*})
is symmetric monoidal because it agrees with
d_{0}\colon\mathscr{P}_{/(\mathbb{N}\to*)}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, and the equivalence
*\simeq*\times_{\mathbb{N}}(\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N})
ensures that
d_{1}(\hat{\iota}^{*})
is symmetric monoidal.
∎

Corollary 5.35
.

We have a commutative square in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{\mathbb{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{/(\mathbb{N}\to*)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{*}\dot{\mathcal{L}}\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathbb{N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}^{*}}

Proof.

Both composite functors
\hat{\iota}^{*}\varphi^{*}\dot{\mathcal{L}}\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathbb{N}}
and
(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})\iota^{*}
are symmetric monoidal, and we have already identified them as lax symmetric monoidal functors.
∎

Notation 5.36
.

We let
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}}:=s^{0}_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbb{N};\mathcal{S})\simeq\mathcal{S}_{/\mathbb{N}}
, and refer to an object in
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}}
as an
\mathbb{N}
-graded space
. We let
\mathscr{P}^{\mathbb{N}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
denote a cartesian fibration corresponding to the functor
\mathscr{P}(-;\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}})\colon(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}}
; the latter functor carries a lax symmetric monoidal structure given by exterior product of presheaves (see Subsection
2.4
), so that
\mathscr{P}^{\mathbb{N}}
as well as the projection
\mathscr{P}^{\mathbb{N}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
carry symmetric monoidal structures. We refer to an object in
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}})
as an
\mathbb{N}
-graded presheaf
over
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
. Equivalently, we may define
\mathscr{P}^{\mathbb{N}}
as
\mathscr{P}_{/(\mathbb{N}\to*)}
.

Similarly, we let
\mathcal{S}^{01}:=s^{0}_{01}\mathcal{S}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\left\{0,1\right\},\mathcal{S})\simeq\mathcal{S}^{2}
. The functor
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})_{*}
exhibits
\mathcal{S}^{01}
as the full subcategory of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}}
spanned by
01
-truncated functors, i.e. functors
\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{S}
that restrict to the terminal functor on
\mathbb{N}\setminus\left\{0,1\right\}
. We denote by
\mathscr{P}^{01}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
a cartesian fibration corresponding to
\mathscr{P}(-;\mathcal{S}^{01})\colon(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}}
. The adjunction
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}\colon\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{S}^{01}\colon(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})_{*}
gives rise to an analogous adjunction
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}\colon\mathscr{P}^{\mathbb{N}}\rightleftarrows\mathscr{P}^{01}\colon(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})_{*}
, with
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})_{*}
exhibiting
\mathscr{P}^{01}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
as the full sub-cartesian fibration of
\mathscr{P}^{\mathbb{N}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
spanned by objects
(\mathcal{C},A)
with
A
taking values in
01
-truncated
\mathbb{N}
-graded spaces.

Moreover the functor
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}
endows
\mathscr{P}^{01}
with a symmetric monoidal structure for which it is a symmetric monoidal functor, and such that the projection
\mathscr{P}^{01}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
is symmetric monoidal as well.

Proposition 5.37
.

There is a pullback square in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}^{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{*}\dot{\mathcal{L}}\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathbb{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}^{*}}

Proof.

Recall the symmetric monoidal equivalence
\mathscr{P}^{\mathbb{N}}\simeq\mathscr{P}_{/(\mathbb{N}\to*)}
. Since the horizontal functors
\iota^{*}
and
\hat{\iota}^{*}
in the square from Corollary
5.35
factor through the localisations
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}
, we obtain a localised commutative square in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}})
as in the statement.
Now observe that we have an equivalence
\mathscr{P}^{01}\simeq\mathscr{P}\times_{\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}}\mathscr{P}
, by taking the parts of a presheaf of grading
0
and
1
: under this equivalence,
\hat{\iota}^{*}
corresponds to taking the part of a presheaf of degree 0. By Proposition
5.5
we then obtain that the square in the statement is a pullback square.
∎

Informally, the functor
\varphi^{*}\dot{\mathcal{L}}\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathbb{N}}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01}\to\mathscr{P}^{01}
sends
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01}
to the triple
(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{C})),\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{C})),\dot{\kappa}^{n}(\mathcal{C}))
, in which
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{C})),\dot{\kappa}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{C})))
account for gradings 0 and 1, respectively, of an
\mathbb{N}
-graded
01
-truncated presheaf over
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\iota^{*}(\mathcal{C}))
.

Corollary 5.38
.

There is a pullback square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P}^{01})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{*}\dot{\mathcal{L}}\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathbb{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}^{*}}

Remark 5.39
.

Let
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]^{\mathrm{sm}}
be an
\mathbb{N}
-admissible extension of symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,n)}
-categories as in Definition
4.14
. Then
Corollary
5.38
allows us to compute
T_{01}(\mathcal{D})\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01})
by computing its three coordinates in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
,
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P})
and
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P}^{01})
.
The first is
\iota^{*}(\mathcal{D})\simeq\mathcal{C}
by Corollary
4.23
. The second is therefore
(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}))\simeq(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}),\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C}))\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P})
.

Consider now
\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}})
as a symmetric monoidal category; by identifying
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
with
\iota_{!}(\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C}))
, we may regard
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C});\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}}))
. We can then define
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}}))
as the free commutative
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
-algebra generated by
\underline{X}
, which we put in grading 1, relative to
\underline{\partial X}
. Then the third coordinate of
\mathcal{D}
is
(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}),(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}(\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}]))\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P}^{01})
.

The part of
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}(\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})[\underline{X},\underline{\partial X}])
of grading
0
is
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
; the part of grading 1 is
\dot{\kappa}^{n}(\mathcal{D})
, which agrees with the free
\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})
-module in
\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C});\mathcal{S})
generated by
\underline{X}
relative to
\underline{\partial X}
. In the basic case in which
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}[X]^{\mathrm{sm}}
we can explicitly identify:

\dot{\kappa}^{n}(\mathcal{D})\simeq\Big(X\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{n-1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\Big)_{!}(*)\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C})).

In the general case, using Notation
4.3
, we may iteratively identify
\mathcal{C}_{i}
as the pushout in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{\mathbb{N}})
of the span
\mathcal{C}_{i-1}\leftarrow\mathcal{C}_{i-1}[\partial X_{i}]\to\mathcal{C}_{i-1}[X_{i}]
; correspondingly, we may compute
\dot{\kappa}(\mathcal{D})
as an iterated pushout in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\dot{\lambda}^{n}(\mathcal{C})}(\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{n}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{S}))
.

5.6.
Mapping spaces in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{2}(\mathcal{C})

We conclude the section with an explicit description of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{2}(\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}
and for objects
\mathbf{x}=([1^{2}],1,x),\mathbf{y}=([1^{2}],1,y)\in\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{2}(\mathcal{C})
, starting from Corollary
5.28
for
\gamma=\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}
. We expand
x,y\in\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
as pairs of parallel 1-morphisms
x_{u},x_{d}\colon x_{0}\to x_{1}
and
y_{u},y_{d}\colon y_{0}\to y_{1}
in
\mathcal{C}
.

The top row of the second pullback square in the proof of Lemma
5.21
, which is an equivalence, together with Corollary
5.12
, allows us to compute
\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([3],010)
as the iterated fibre product

\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})\times_{\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)\times_{\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})
. It follows that the pullback of the left cospan in the W-shaped diagram from Corollary
5.28
is equivalent to the fibre at
(x_{0},x_{1})
of the functor

(\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{d_{0}}),\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{d_{1}}))\colon\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})\to\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[0]})\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[0]})\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}\times\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}.

We abbreviate this fibre by
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{0}x_{0}}\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{1}x_{1}}
. We can now compute
\Xi(\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
as the following pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi(\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{0}x_{0}}\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{1}x_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)\big)_{y/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

The first pullback square in the proof of Lemma

computes
\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)
as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}\times\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{[1]}\times\mathcal{C}_{[1]}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})^{2}\times(\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})^{2},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{diagonal}\times\text{diagonal}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{1},d_{0},d_{1},d_{0})}

allowing us to identify
\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)_{y/}
and
\mathcal{C}(y_{0},y_{1})_{y_{u}/}\times(\mathcal{C}(y_{0},y_{1})_{/y_{d}})^{\mathrm{op}}
.

The functor
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{0}x_{0}}\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{1}x_{1}}\to\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}([1],1)
occurring in the pullback computing
\Xi(\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
has top right coordinate given by the functor

\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{d_{1}})\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{d_{0}})\colon\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{0}x_{0}}\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{1}x_{1}}\to\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}\times\mathcal{C}^{\simeq},

and it has bottom left coordinate given by the following composite, where we abbreviate by

the fibre at

of
