:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{0}x_{0}}\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{1}x_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big((\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{0}x_{0}}\times(\mathcal{C}_{[1]}^{\mathrm{op}})_{d_{0}x_{0}}\big)\times\big((\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{1}x_{1}}\times(\mathcal{C}_{[1]}^{\mathrm{op}})_{d_{1}x_{1}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big((\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{0}x_{0}}\times(\mathcal{C}_{[1]})_{d_{1}x_{1}}\big)\times\big((\mathcal{C}_{[1]}^{\mathrm{op}})_{d_{0}x_{0}}\times(\mathcal{C}_{[1]}^{\mathrm{op}})_{d_{1}x_{1}}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{[1]}\times\mathcal{C}_{[1]}^{\mathrm{op}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-\circ_{\mathrm{h}}x_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}-)\times(-\circ_{\mathrm{h}}x_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}-)^{\mathrm{op}}}

Combining all of the previous remarks, we obtain the following simplified formula for
\Xi(\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
, which we state as a proposition for future reference.

Proposition 5.40
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}
and let
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
; using the notation above, we have a pullback square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
whose horizontal arrows are left fibrations:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi(\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}(y_{0},x_{0})\times\mathcal{C}(x_{1},y_{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}(y_{0},x_{0})\times\mathcal{C}(x_{1},y_{1}))\times(\mathcal{C}(y_{0},x_{0})\times\mathcal{C}(x_{1},y_{1}))^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(y_{0},y_{1})_{y_{u}/}\times(\mathcal{C}(y_{0},y_{1})_{/y_{d}})^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(y_{0},y_{1})\times\mathcal{C}(y_{0},y_{1})^{\mathrm{op}}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-\circ_{\mathrm{h}}x_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}-)\times(-\circ_{\mathrm{h}}x_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}-)^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}\times d_{1}}

6.
The universal property of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}

In this section we prove Theorem
3.19
; meanwhile, we start the proof of
A
under the assumption of the validity of Theorem
3.19
: some of the steps of the proofs of the two theorems are parallel, and we treat them simultaneously.

6.1.
Coloured graphs and coloured graph collapses

We start by introducing
S
-coloured versions of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}
and of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}
, for a finite set
S
.

Definition 6.1
.

Recall Definition
3.2
and Notation
3.3
.
We let
\mathrm{VE}\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}\to\mathbf{B}(\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq})
denote the symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,1)}
-functor associating with a gaf
G
the disjoint union
V\sqcup E
of its sets of inner vertices and of edges. Recall also Definition
3.6
. We let
\mathrm{CE}\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}\to\mathbf{B}^{2}(\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq})
denote the symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,2)}
-functor associating with a morphism of gaf’s
f\colon G\to G^{\prime}
the subset of
E
comprising edges that are collapsed along
f
.

For
S\in\mathrm{Fin}
we identify
(\mathrm{Fin}_{/S})^{\simeq}
as the
S
-fold coproduct of
\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{S})
, so that the forgetful map
(\mathrm{Fin}_{/S})^{\simeq}\to\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}
agrees with the fold map. We define
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
and
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)})
by the following pullbacks:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}((\mathrm{Fin}_{/S})^{\simeq})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}(\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq});}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{VE}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
fold
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{VE}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{2}((\mathrm{Fin}_{/S})^{\simeq})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{2}(\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CE}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CE}}

Remark 6.2
.

The fact that the functors
\mathrm{VE}
and
\mathrm{CE}
are well defined on higher morphisms and carry a symmetric monoidal structure can in principle be checked “manually” by using that
\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}
is an aspherical space, so that
\mathbf{B}\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}
and
\mathbf{B}^{2}\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}
have discrete spaces of 2-morphisms and 3-morphisms, respectively; hence all coherences of dimension at least 2 and 3, respectively, are properties and not structure.
Alternatively, we could directly define
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)
and
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)
by replacing Definition
3.2
, respectively Definition
3.6
, with their counterparts in which we adjoin the datum of a suitable map of finite sets to
S
, from
V\sqcup E
or from
E\setminus f^{-1}(E^{\prime})
, respectively, and by then repeating verbatim the constructions from Section
3
.

Remark 6.3
.

We remark that
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)
is a plain (2,1)-category, and
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)
is a plain 2-category: that is, if we consider both
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S),{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)
as objects in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}
, then all spaces of 2-morphisms are discrete. We also remark that
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)
is in general
not
equivalent to
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{2\simeq}
: the latter is instead equivalent to
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}
.

The assignments
S\mapsto{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S),{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)
give rise for
n=1,2
to functors
\mathrm{Fin}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)})
. Moreover, we have symmetric monoidal functors

\mathbf{B}^{n}(\#_{/S})\colon\mathbf{B}^{n}((\mathrm{Fin}_{/S})^{\simeq})\to\mathbf{B}^{n}\mathbb{N}^{S}

induced by taking cardinalities of finite sets, fibrewise over
S
; these give
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-gradings on
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)
and
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)
, respectively.

.

Notation 6.5
.

Recall Notation
4.29
. For finite sets
T\subseteq S
we consider
\mathbb{N}^{T}
as a submonoid of
\mathbb{N}^{S}
and regard
1^{T}
also as an element of
\mathbb{N}^{S}
.

Lemma 6.6
.

The following implications hold.

(1)

Assume that for all
S\in\mathrm{Fin}
the
{(\infty,1)}
-functor
\mathfrak{F}(S)
induces an equivalence on spaces of 1-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading
1^{S}
. Then Theorem
3.19
holds.

(2)

Assume Theorem
3.19
, and assume that for all
S\in\mathrm{Fin}
the
{(\infty,2)}
-functor
\mathfrak{G}(S)
induces an equivalence on spaces of 2-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading
1^{S}
. Then Theorem
A
holds.

Proof.

For (1), by Example
4.24
, it suffices to check that
\mathfrak{F}
induces an equivalence on spaces of 1-morphisms. Since
\mathfrak{F}
is an
\mathbb{N}
-graded functor, we can check the last statement in each grading
r\in\mathbb{N}
separately, i.e. prove that
\mathfrak{F}
induces an equivalence of spaces
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathcal{U})^{\simeq}_{r}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq})^{\simeq}_{r}
.
Let
S
be a set of cardinality
r\geq 0
; we may write the following commutative diagram of spaces, in which the top arrow is an equivalence by assumption, and the right arrow by direct inspection:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathcal{U}(S))^{\simeq}_{1^{S}}/\mathfrak{S}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S))^{\simeq}_{1^{S}}/\mathfrak{S}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathcal{U})^{\simeq}_{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq})^{\simeq}_{r}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

It follows that the left arrow admits a retraction; by Proposition
4.30
it also admits a section, so that it has to be an equivalence of spaces. Hence also the bottom arrow is an equivalence.
The argument for (2) is analogous. By the assumption that Theorem
3.19
holds and by Example
4.24
, it suffices to check, for each finite set
S
of cardinality
r\geq 0
, that the bottom arrow in the following diagram is an equivalence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{2},\mathcal{V}(S))^{\simeq}_{1^{S}}/\mathfrak{S}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{2},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S))^{\simeq}_{1^{S}}/\mathfrak{S}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{2},\mathcal{V})^{\simeq}_{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{2},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}})^{\simeq}_{r}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

We invoke Proposition
4.30
and conclude as before.
∎

Notation 6.7
.

For a finite set
S
we denote by
S_{+}
the finite set
S\sqcup\left\{\bullet\right\}
.

Definition 6.8
.

For a finite set
S
we denote by
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+}
the pushout of the span
\mathcal{U}(S_{+})\leftarrow\mathcal{U}(S)\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}(S)}{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
, in which the left pointing arrow is induced by the inclusion
S\hookrightarrow S_{+}
. Note that
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+}
is an extension of the form
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)[\mathbf{B}C_{2}\sqcup*]^{\mathrm{sm}}
as in Definition
4.2
.
Similarly, we denote by
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}
the pushout of
\mathcal{V}(S_{+})\leftarrow\mathcal{V}(S)\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{G}(S)}{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)})
; it is an extension of the form
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)[S|*\ ;\ \mathbf{B}C_{2},*]^{\mathrm{sm}}
as in Notation
4.3
.

We denote by
\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S)\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+}\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+})
and
\mathfrak{j}(S)\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})
the symmetric monoidal functors induced on pushouts by the following commutative squares in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
and
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(S_{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+});}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\hookrightarrow S_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{F}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{F}(S_{+})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\hookrightarrow S_{+}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}(S_{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\hookrightarrow S_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{G}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{G}(S_{+})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\hookrightarrow S_{+}}

We restrict the
\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}
-gradings on
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+})
and
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})
along
\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S)
and
\mathfrak{j}(S)
.

Lemma 6.9
.

The following implications hold.

(1)

Let
r\geq 0
and assume that
\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(T)
induces an equivalence
on spaces of 1-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{T_{+}}
-grading
1^{T_{+}}

for all
T\in\mathrm{Fin}
with
\#T<r
. Then for all
T\subseteq S\in\mathrm{Fin}
with
\#S\leq r
,
\mathfrak{F}(S)
induces an equivalence on spaces of 1-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading
1^{T}
for all
T\subseteq S
; in particular the hypothesis of Lemma
6.6
(1) holds for
S
.

(2)

Assume Theorem
3.19
. Let
r\geq 0
and assume that
\mathfrak{j}(T)
induces an equivalence
on spaces of 2-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{T_{+}}
-grading
1^{T_{+}}

for all
T\in\mathrm{Fin}
with
\#T<r
. Then for all
T\subseteq S\in\mathrm{Fin}
with
\#S\leq r
,
\mathfrak{G}(S)
induces an equivalence on spaces of 2-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading
1^{T}
; in particular the hypothesis of Lemma
6.6
(2) holds for
S
.

Proof.

For (1), we proceed inductively on
\#S\leq r
. For
S=\emptyset
we have to set
T=\emptyset
and we may identify both spaces
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathcal{U}(S))^{\simeq}_{1^{\emptyset}}
and
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S))^{\simeq}_{1^{\emptyset}}
with
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq})
by Corollary
4.23
. Suppose that the statement holds for a certain
S
with
\#S<r
, let
T\subseteq S_{+}
, and define the coideal

I:=\left\{0,1\right\}^{T}\times\left\{0\right\}^{S_{+}\setminus T}\subset M:=\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}.

If
T\neq S_{+}
, we write a commutative diagram as follows, in which we embed
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathbb{N}^{T}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M})
via the functor
(\mathbb{N}^{T}\hookrightarrow M)_{!}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(T))^{\simeq}_{1^{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathcal{U}(T))^{\simeq}_{1^{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},T_{I}(\mathcal{U}(T)))^{\simeq}_{1^{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+}))^{\simeq}_{1^{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathcal{U}(S_{+}))^{\simeq}_{1^{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},T_{I}(\mathcal{U}(S_{+})))^{\simeq}_{1^{T}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{F}(T)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{F}(S_{+})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The left vertical arrow is an equivalence by inspection, the top left horizontal arrow is an equivalence by inductive hypothesis, the two right horizontal arrows are equivalences by Corollary
4.21
, and the right vertical arrow is an equivalence by Corollary
4.23
:
It follows that the bottom left horizontal arrow is also an equivalence.

Assume now
T=S_{+}
, and
factor the map
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathfrak{F}(S_{+}))^{\simeq}_{1^{S_{+}}}
as a composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},\mathcal{U}(S_{+}))^{\simeq}_{1^{S_{+}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+})^{\simeq}_{1^{S_{+}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{1},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+}))^{\simeq}_{1^{S_{+}}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S)}

where the second map is an equivalence by assumption. The first map is induced by the
M
-graded symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,1)}
-functor
\mathcal{U}(S_{+})\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+}
induced on pushouts by the following map of spans in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M})
, where again we use the functor
(\mathbb{N}^{S}\hookrightarrow M)_{!}
to put all objects in the same category:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(S_{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(S_{+})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\hookrightarrow S_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{F}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{F}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\hookrightarrow S_{+}}

By inductive hypothesis and by Corollary
4.21
, the right vertical map is a
T_{I}
-equivalence: in particular, we observe that for all
\bullet\in T\subseteq S_{+}
the spaces of 1-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}
-grading
1^{T}
in both
\mathcal{U}(S)
and
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)
are empty. By applying the functor
(\iota^{I,M})^{*}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{M})\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{I})
, which preserves pushouts, we obtain an equivalence
(\iota^{I,M})^{*}(\mathcal{U}(S_{+}))\xrightarrow{\simeq}(\iota^{I,M})^{*}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+})
, yielding the desired equivalence on spaces of 1-morphiss of
M
-grading
1^{T}
for all
T\subseteq S_{+}
.

The analogous argument for (2) is omitted.
∎

For
S\in\mathrm{Fin}
, the projections
\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}\to\mathbb{N}^{\left\{\bullet\right\}}\simeq\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}\to\mathbb{N}^{S}
allow us to split an
\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}
-grading
as a pair of an
\mathbb{N}
-grading and an
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading. The first grading allows us to consider
\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S)
and
\mathfrak{j}(S)
as morphisms in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{\mathbb{N}})
for
n=1,2
. By Lemma
6.9
, it suffices for us to focus on the maps induced by
\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S)
and
\mathfrak{j}(S)
on spaces of
n
-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}
-grading 1 (and
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading
1^{S}
), for
n=1,2
.
We can neglect the
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading and detect all
n
-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}
-grading 1 after having applied the functor
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{\mathbb{N}})\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01})
,
i.e. we can focus on the following morphisms in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,n)}^{01})
:

\begin{split}(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}(\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S))\colon&(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+})\to(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+}));\\
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}(\mathfrak{j}(S))\colon&(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+})\to(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})).\end{split}

For
n=1
we shall prove that
(\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}(\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S))
is an equivalence by analysing its three coordinates with respect to the pullback square from Corollary
5.38
. The top right coordinate
\iota^{*}(\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S))
is an equivalence in the category
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
: by Corollary
4.23
and by a direct inspection, respectively, we have that both the source
\iota^{*}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+})
and the target
\iota^{*}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+}))
can be identified with
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)
. It follows that also the bottom right coordinate is an equivalence in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P})
. The proof of Theorem
3.19
is complete once we prove that the bottom left coordinate
\varphi^{*}\dot{\mathcal{L}}\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathbb{N}}((\iota^{01,\mathbb{N}})^{*}(\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S)))
is an equivalence in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathscr{P}^{01})
as well; this boils down to proving that the morphism
\dot{\kappa}^{1}(\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S))\colon\dot{\kappa}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+})\to\dot{\kappa}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+}))
is an equivalence in
\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)))
, and this is what we shall prove in Subsection
6.2
.

For
n=2
we will first make a reduction: we will introduce the property of being
leaf-like
for 2-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}
-grading 1 in
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}
and
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})
; it will be apparent that
\mathfrak{j}(S)
preserves leaf-like morphisms. We will then reduce the proof of Theorem
A
to checking that
\mathfrak{j}(S)
induces equivalences on spaces of leaf-like 2-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading
1^{S}
. After this we will again neglect the
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading and proceed in a similar manner as in the case
n=1
, showing that
\dot{\kappa}^{2}(\mathfrak{j}(S))\colon\dot{\kappa}^{2}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+})\to\dot{\kappa}^{2}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+}))
induces objectwise an equivalence on spaces of leaf-like 2-morphisms.

6.2.
Proof of Theorem
3.19

We fix
S\in\mathrm{Fin}
and aim at proving that the morphism
\dot{\kappa}^{1}(\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S))\colon\dot{\kappa}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+})\to\dot{\kappa}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+}))
in
\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)))
is an equivalence.
We start by analysing the target presheaf. Let
(B,A)\in\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S))\simeq{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)\times{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{\mathrm{op}}
, i.e.
A,B
are finite sets. We have
\dot{\kappa}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+}))(B,A)\simeq{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+})(B,A)_{1}
; the latter space can be identified with the moduli space of graph cobordisms
G
from
B
to
A
endowed with a map of finite sets
\mathrm{VE}(G)\to S_{+}
whose fibre over
\bullet
is a singleton, i.e. the pullback of the cospan of spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{2\simeq}(B,A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Fin}_{/S})^{\simeq}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{VE}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}(-)_{+}}

Notation 6.10
.

We decompose
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+})(B,A)_{1}
as a disjoint union of spaces
X(B,A)_{\mathfrak{t}}\sqcup X(B,A)_{\mathfrak{e}}
, according to whether the unique element in the fibre over
\bullet
of the map
\mathrm{VE}(G)\to S_{+}
is an inner vertex or an edge.

We next pass to the analysis of
\dot{\kappa}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+})
.

Notation 6.11
.

Recall Remark
5.39
. We denote by
\theta\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)))
the left Kan extension of the terminal presheaf
*\in\mathscr{P}(*\sqcup\mathbf{B}C_{2})
along the composite

\mathbf{B}C_{2}\sqcup*\xrightarrow{F^{\vee}}\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}\times\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}\simeq({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)\times{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{\mathrm{op}})^{\simeq}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)\times{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{\mathrm{op}},

where the map
F^{\vee}
is adjoint to the map
F
from Example
4.4
.
We similarly let
\theta_{\mathfrak{t}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)))
denote the left Kan extensions of
*\in\mathscr{P}(*)\simeq\mathcal{S}
and
*\in\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{B}C_{2})
along the restrictions of the above composite to
*
and
\mathbf{B}C_{2}
, respectively.

We abbreviate
\mathcal{C}={\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)

and
\dot{\lambda}=\dot{\lambda}^{1}(\mathcal{C})\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{1}(\mathcal{C}))\simeq\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})
for the rest of the subsection.
As observed in Remark
5.39
, we can identify
\dot{\kappa}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+})
with the tensor product
\theta\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda}
in
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})
.
Moreover
\theta
splits as a coproduct
\theta_{\mathfrak{t}}\sqcup\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}
in
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})
, so that also
\theta\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda}
also splits as
\theta_{\mathfrak{t}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda}\sqcup\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda}
.

We start by analysing the second presheaf
\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda}
.
The restriction of
F^{\vee}
to
\mathbf{B}C_{2}
is the product of the constant map at
\underline{0}\in\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}
and the inclusion
j\colon\mathbf{B}C_{2}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fin}^{\simeq}\simeq(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})\simeq
, so that
\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda}
can be computed as the left Kan extension of
*\boxtimes\dot{\lambda}\in\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}};\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}^{S}})
along the composite functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq*\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{0},\mathrm{Id},j,\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes,\otimes}

Since
\underline{0}
is the monoidal unit in
\mathcal{C}
, the previous composite functor agrees with

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\mathcal{C}\times\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id},j,\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id},\otimes}

Note that the last composition features the product of the identity of
\mathcal{C}
(appearing as middle factor in the source) and the functor
j\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\colon\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
, so that left Kan extension along the last composite fits as top row in the following commutative square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C};(j\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}})_{!})}

By Corollary
5.12
, the presheaf
*\boxtimes\dot{\lambda}
in the top left category corresponds to the functor
*\boxtimes\!\text{\char 135\relax}\!_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(-)\colon\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{B}C_{2}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})
in the bottom left category.
We may moreover identify
*\simeq\mathrm{colim}_{\mathbf{B}C_{2}}\!\text{\char 135\relax}\!_{\mathbf{B}C_{2}}\in\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{B}C_{2})
, and write a chain of equivalences in the bottom right category
\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}))\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}))
:

\begin{split}(j\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}})_{!}\circ\big(*\boxtimes\!\text{\char 135\relax}\!_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(-)\big)&\simeq j_{!}(*)\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\!\text{\char 135\relax}\!_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(-)\\
&\simeq j_{!}(\mathrm{colim}_{\mathbf{B}C_{2}}\!\text{\char 135\relax}\!_{\mathbf{B}C_{2}})\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\!\text{\char 135\relax}\!_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(-)\\
&\simeq\mathrm{colim}_{\mathbf{B}C_{2}}\!\text{\char 135\relax}\!_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(j\otimes-).\end{split}

Hence for
B,A\in\mathrm{Fin}
we may identify
(\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(B,A)\simeq{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)(B\sqcup\underline{2},A)/C_{2}
, where
C_{2}
acts trivially on
B,A
and by swap on
\underline{2}
.
An analogous, but slightly simpler computation allows us to identify

(\theta_{\mathfrak{t}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(B,A)\simeq{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)(B,A\sqcup\underline{1}).

The map of presheaves
\dot{\kappa}^{1}(\mathfrak{j}^{2\simeq}(S))\colon\dot{\kappa}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)^{+})\to\dot{\kappa}^{1}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+}))
gives, after evaluating at
(B,A)
, a map of spaces
(\theta\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(B,A)\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+})(B,A)_{1}
which restricts to maps

(\theta_{\mathfrak{t}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(B,A)\to X(B,A)_{\mathfrak{t}}
and
(\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(B,A)\to X(B,A)_{\mathfrak{e}}
.

Under the above identifications, the second map can be identified with the map induced on the
C_{2}
-quotient by the precomposition map

-\circ(\mathrm{Id}_{B}\sqcup\mathfrak{e}_{\bullet})\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)(B\sqcup\underline{2},A)\to X(B,A)_{\mathfrak{e}}\subset{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+})(B,A)_{1},

where
\mathfrak{e}_{\bullet}\in{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+})(\underline{0},\underline{2})_{1}
is the morphism
\mathfrak{e}
with the unique edge labeled
\bullet
; and where we also identify
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)(B\sqcup\underline{2},A)\simeq{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+})(B\sqcup\underline{2},A)_{0}
.
A direct inspection shows that
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)(B\sqcup\underline{2},A)/C_{2}\xrightarrow{\simeq}X(B,A)_{\mathfrak{e}}
is indeed an equivalence of spaces. By a similar, slightly simpler argument, the first map
(\theta_{\mathfrak{t}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(B,A)\to X(B,A)_{\mathfrak{t}}
can be identified with the postcomposition map

(\mathrm{Id}_{A}\sqcup\mathfrak{t}_{\bullet})\circ-\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S)(B,A\sqcup\underline{1})\xrightarrow{\simeq}X(B,A)_{\mathfrak{t}}\subset{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}(S_{+})(B,A)_{1},

which is an equivalence. This concludes the proof of Theorem
3.19
.

7.
The universal property of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}

In this section we conclude the proof of Theorem
A
.
We introduce some notations and conventions that we will use in the rest of the section.

•

We fix
S\in\mathrm{Fin}
; by virtue of Lemmas
6.6
and
6.9
, our goal
is to prove that
\mathfrak{j}(S)
induces an equivalence on spaces of 2-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}
grading
1^{S_{+}}
.

•

We abbreviate
\mathcal{C}={\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)
and
\mathcal{C}_{+}={\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})
.

•

Recall Definition
5.4
. We abbreviate

\dot{\mathcal{T}}:=\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{2}(\mathcal{C})\simeq\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{+})
(using the equivalence
\mathcal{C}\simeq\iota^{*}\mathcal{C}_{+}
),
\dot{\mathcal{K}}:=\dot{\mathcal{K}}^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{+})
,
\dot{\mathcal{L}}:=\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{2}(\mathcal{C})
, and we write
\dot{\lambda}:=\dot{\lambda}^{2}(\mathcal{C})
and
\dot{\kappa}:=\dot{\kappa}^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{+})
, which are objects in
\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}})
. We further write
p\colon\dot{\mathcal{L}}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}
and
p_{+}\colon\dot{\mathcal{K}}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}
for the projections.

•

We suppress the first entry “
\gamma
” in a category of the form
\Xi(\gamma,-,-)
as in Corollary
5.28
, leaving it understood to be equal to
\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0}=\Gamma^{2}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S))_{0}
.

Notation 7.1
.

Given
B,A\in\mathrm{Fin}
and
G_{u},G_{d}\in{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\simeq}
, we usually denote by
\mathcal{O}=(B,A,G_{u},G_{d})\in\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{1},{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}})^{\simeq}
the corresponding
\mathrm{O}_{1}
-shaped diagram. We consider
(B,A,G_{u},G_{d})
also as an object of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}
: by Lemma
5.6
, up to equivalence we can represent any object in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}
in this way. We similarly expand
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}=(B^{\prime},A^{\prime},G^{\prime}_{u},G^{\prime}_{d})
, and so on.

A 2-morphism in
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})
from
G_{u}
to
G_{d}
is usually represented as a pair
(f,m)
of a 2-morphism
f\colon G_{u}\to G_{d}
in
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}
and a map
m\colon\mathrm{CE}(f)\to S_{+}
. Given
\mathcal{D}\in(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)})_{/\mathcal{C}_{+}}
with
\mathcal{D}^{2\simeq}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{2\simeq}\simeq\mathcal{C}_{+}^{2\simeq}
, we denote by
\mathcal{D}(f,m)\subseteq\mathcal{D}(B,A)(G_{u},G_{d})
the subspace of 2-morphisms mapping to
(f,m)
.

If
(f,m)
has
\mathbb{N}
-grading 0, we consider
(f,m)
also as an object in
p^{-1}(\mathcal{O})\subseteq\dot{\mathcal{L}}
; and if
(f,m)
has
\mathbb{N}
-grading 1, we consider it also as an object in
p_{+}^{-1}(\mathcal{O})\subseteq\dot{\mathcal{K}}
.

7.1.
The leaf-like reduction

Definition 7.2
.

A
leaf
in a gaf
G
is an edge
e\in E
at least one of whose half-edges
h
is attached, along
\sigma
, to an inner vertex
v\in V
of valence 1, i.e.
\sigma^{-1}(v)=\left\{h,v\right\}
. An orientation on a leaf is a choice of a half-edge
h
with the above properties (it is unique unless the component of
e
in
G
is isomorphic to the gaf
G_{\tilde{\beta}_{1}}
from Subsection
3.3
, after disregarding the markings by
B
and by
\underline{2}
, respectively).

We say that
(f,m)
as in Notation
7.1
is
leaf-like
if the following hold:

•

the
\mathbb{N}=\mathbb{N}^{\left\{\bullet\right\}}
-grading of
(f,m)
is 1; that is, the fibre
m^{-1}(\bullet)
is a singleton;

•

the unique element in
m^{-1}(\bullet)
is a leaf in the based or unbased sub-trees of
G_{u}
containing it, among those sub-trees that are collapsed along
f
.

In this case, we usually fix an orientation
h_{\ell}\in e_{\ell}
of the special leaf
e_{\ell}=m^{-1}(\bullet)\in E_{u}
, and we denote
v_{\ell}=\sigma(h_{\ell})\in V_{u}
the corresponding vertex of valence 1.

Given
\mathcal{D}\in(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)})_{/{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})}
, we say that a 2-morphism in
\mathcal{D}
is
leaf-like
if its image in
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})
is leaf-like.

Lemma 7.3
.

Assume the following:

•

for all
T\in\mathrm{Fin}
with
\#T<\#S
, the map
\mathfrak{j}(T)\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(T)^{+}\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(T_{+})
induces an equivalence on spaces of 2-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{T_{+}}
-grading
1^{T_{+}}
.

•

for all
(f,m)
as in Notation
7.1
such that
(f,m)
is leaf-like and has
\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}
-grading
1^{S_{+}}
, the map of spaces
\mathfrak{j}(S)(f,m)\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}(f,m)\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})(f,m)
is an equivalence (equivalently,
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}(f,m)
is contractible).

Then
\mathfrak{j}(S)
induces an equivalence on spaces of 2-morphisms of
\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}
-grading
1^{S_{+}}
.

Proof.

We have to prove that
\mathfrak{j}(S)(f,m)
is an equivalence also when
(f,m)
has
\mathbb{N}^{S_{+}}
-grading
1^{S_{+}}
but is not leaf-like. Since
m\colon\mathrm{CE}(f)\xrightarrow{\simeq}S_{+}
is non-empty, we may find
s\in S
that corresponds along
m
to a leaf in some tree collapsed by
f
. Let
\bar{m}
denote the composite of
m\colon\mathrm{CE}(f)\xrightarrow{\simeq}S_{+}
with the transposition of
S_{+}
swapping
s
and
\bullet
. We may then write a commutative diagram of spaces as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(S_{+})(f,\bar{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}(S_{+})(f,m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}(f,m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}(f,\bar{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})(f,\bar{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})(f,m).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The first hypothesis implies by Lemma
6.9
that
\mathfrak{G}(S)\colon\mathcal{U}(S)\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)
is a
T_{01^{S}}
-equivalence, so that the map between extensions
\mathfrak{G}(S)[*\ ;\ *\sqcup\mathbf{B}C_{2}]\colon\mathcal{U}(S_{+})\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}
is a
T_{01^{S_{+}}}
-equivalence; in particular the top right horizontal map is an equivalence. An analogous argument applies to the left vertical map, which is therefore an equivalence. By the second hypothesis the bottom left horizontal map is an equivalence; we conclude that the right vertical map is also an equivalence.
∎

Corollary 7.4
.

Assume
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}(f,m)\simeq*
for all
S\in\mathrm{Fin}
and all leaf-like 2-morphisms
(f,m)
in
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S_{+})
as in Notation
7.1
. Then Theorem
A
holds.

Remark 7.5
.

Recall from Definition
7.2
that a leaf-like 2-morphism
(f,m)
is in particular required to have
\mathbb{N}
-grading 1. In principle we could weaken the assumption in Corollary
7.4
by only requiring
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}(f,m)
to be contractible when
(f,m)
has
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading
1^{S}
. In practice it will be easier to just neglect the
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-grading from now on and focus on the
\mathbb{N}=\mathbb{N}^{\left\{\bullet\right\}}
-grading. This will also spare us the difficulty to formalise twisted
\mathbb{N}^{S}
-gradings as introduced in Remark
5.31
.

7.2.
A core groupoid computation

Definition 7.6
.

Given a full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory inclusion
\mathcal{D}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{2}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, we say that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
is a
split
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory
of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
if there exists a full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory
\mathcal{D}_{3}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{2}
such that the two inclusions exhibit
\mathcal{D}_{2}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{1}\sqcup\mathcal{D}_{3}
.

Definition 7.7
.

For
\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\in\dot{\mathcal{T}}
,
(f,m)\in\dot{\kappa}(\mathcal{O})
and
(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})\in\dot{\kappa}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
we denote by
\Xi((f,m),(f^{\prime},m^{\prime}))
the split
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory of
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})=\Xi(\Gamma^{2}(\mathcal{C})_{0},\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
corresponding to the subspace
\dot{\mathcal{K}}((f,m),(f^{\prime},m^{\prime}))\subseteq\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})\simeq|\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})|
. We define similarly
\Xi((f,m),(f^{\prime},m^{\prime}))
for
(f,m)\in\dot{\lambda}(\mathcal{O})
and
(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})\in\dot{\lambda}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
.

Notation 7.8
.

We usually represent a morphism
\zeta\colon\mathcal{O}\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}
by an object in
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
, i.e. by an annular diagram as follows, using Notation
7.1
and relying on Proposition
5.40
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{u,l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{d,l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{u,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{d,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{u},m_{u})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{d},m_{d})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{l},m_{l})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{r},m_{r})}

Here for
\star=l,r,u,d
, the pair
(f_{\star},m_{\star})
is a 2-morphism in
\mathcal{C}
, in particular it has
\mathbb{N}
-grading 0.
We say that a diagram as the one above is “special” if
f_{l}
and
f_{r}
are equivalences; up to equivalence, in a special diagram we have
G_{u,l}=G_{d,l}
and
G_{u,r}=G_{d,r}
: we then denote the first gaf by
G_{l}
and the second by
G_{r}
.

We represent a morphism
\zeta\colon(f,m)\to(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})
in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
or in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
using the same notation, i.e. by an object in
\Xi((f,m),(f^{\prime},m^{\prime}))
.

Remark 7.9
.

We observe each object in
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
is connected by some morphism to an object corresponding to a special diagram; hence any point in the space
|\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})|
is equivalent to one represented by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
. In other words, every morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}
can be represented up to (non-canonical) equivalence by a special diagram. A similar remark holds for
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
and
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
.

Lemma 7.10
.

The map
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{\simeq}
from Lemma
5.6
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
be as in Notation
7.1
. We have a functor
\mathfrak{d}\colon\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{N}
, sending an object as in Notation
7.8
to the sum of the cardinalities of the finite sets
\mathrm{CE}(f_{l}),\mathrm{CE}(f_{r}),\mathrm{CE}(f_{u}),\mathrm{CE}(f_{d})
; this assignment on objects is constant along morphisms, so it descends indeed to a functor
\mathfrak{d}\colon\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{N}
and hence to a map of spaces
\mathfrak{d}\colon\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{N}
, using Proposition
5.40
.
The maps of spaces
\mathfrak{d}\colon\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{N}
assemble into an
\mathbb{N}
-grading on
\dot{\mathcal{T}}
. If we let
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0}
denote the split
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory
\mathfrak{d}^{-1}(0)\subseteq\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
,
it follows that the subspace of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
comprising equivalences is contained in the subspace
|\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0}|\subset|\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})|
. Note also that
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0}
is already an
\infty
-groupoid.

We can now define another functor
\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}\colon\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0}\to\mathbb{N}
: this time we send an object, which is in particular represented by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
, to the sum
\#\mathrm{VE}(G_{l})+\#\mathrm{VE}(G_{r})
; again this assignment is constant on (iso)morphisms in
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0}
, so it descends indeed to a map of spaces
\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}\colon\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0}\to\mathbb{N}
; the subspaces of morphism spaces
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0}\subset|\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})|\simeq\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
assemble into a wide subcategory
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\subseteq\dot{\mathcal{T}}
, and the maps
\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}
give rise to an
\mathbb{N}
-grading on
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
. It follows that the subspace of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
comprising equivalences is contained in the subspace
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0,\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}=0}=(\mathfrak{d}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)\subseteq\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0}
.

Finally, we have a functor
\omega\colon\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\to\mathrm{Fin}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathrm{Fin}
, sending
\mathcal{O}\mapsto(B,A)
and sending a morphism
\zeta\colon\mathcal{O}\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
represented by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
to the pair of
maps of finite sets

G_{l}\colon B^{\prime}\to B
and
G_{r}\colon A\to A^{\prime}
. It follows that the subspace of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
comprising equivalences is contained in the subspace
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0,\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}=0,\omega\simeq}\subseteq\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0,\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}=0}
of morphisms of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
that are sent to equivalences along
\omega
.

We now observe that
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})_{\mathfrak{d}=0,\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}=0,\omega\simeq}
is equivalent to the space of natural isomorphisms between
\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
. This proves that the map of spaces
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}^{\simeq}
from Lemma
5.6
is fully faithful; and by the same lemma, it is essentially surjective.
∎

Corollary 7.11
.

We have
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{2},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\simeq\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{\simeq}
and
\mathrm{Fun}(\Theta_{2},\mathcal{C}_{+})_{1}^{\simeq}\simeq\dot{\mathcal{K}}^{\simeq}
.

Proof.

We use that
p\colon\dot{\mathcal{L}}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}
and
p_{+}\colon\dot{\mathcal{K}}\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}
are right fibrations corresponding to the presheaves
\dot{\lambda},
and
\dot{\kappa}
, whose restrictions to the space
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{O}_{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
are the presheaves sending
\mathcal{O}
to the space of 2-morphisms in
\mathcal{C}_{+}
from
G_{u}
to
G_{d}
having
\mathbb{N}
-grading 0 and 1, respectively.
∎

We conclude the subsection with a construction of functors between categories of the form
\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
, and by restriction of the form
\Xi((f,m),(f^{\prime},m^{\prime}))
.

Definition 7.12
.

Let
\zeta\colon\mathcal{O}\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
be a morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}
represented by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
, and let
\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime}\in\dot{\mathcal{T}}
. We define a functor
\zeta\circ-\colon\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O})\to\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
.
Consider the following lax commutative square, whose vertical functors are given by the right vertical functors in the pullback decompositions for
\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O})
and
\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
as in Proposition
5.40
, respectively:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},B))\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}(A,A^{\prime\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},B^{\prime}))\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}(A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime\prime})\times\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime\prime})^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime\prime})\times\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime\prime})^{\mathrm{op}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tw}(G_{l}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tw}(-\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{r})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}(-)\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}d_{1}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}(-)\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}d_{0}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}(-)\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G^{\prime}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}d_{1}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}(-)\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G^{\prime}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}d_{0}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{d}}

We consider the pullback decompositions of
\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O})
and
\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
from
5.40
.
We define the top right coordinate of
\zeta\circ-
as the composite of the top right projection
\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O})\to\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},B))\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}(A,A^{\prime\prime}))
with the top horizontal functor above; we define the bottom right coordinate of
\zeta\circ-
as the further composite of the previous with the right vertical functor above; and we define the bottom left coordinate of
\zeta\circ-
as the concatenation of the bottom left projection
\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O})\to\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime\prime})_{G^{\prime\prime}_{u}/}\times\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime\prime})^{\mathrm{op}}_{G^{\prime\prime}_{d}/}
with the composite functor

\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O})\to\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},B))\times\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}(A,A^{\prime\prime}))\to\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime\prime})\times\mathcal{C}(B^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime\prime})^{\mathrm{op}}),

where the second arrow is given by the above lax square.

We define similarly a functor
-\circ\zeta\colon\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})\to\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})
. Whenever we have objects
(f,m),(f^{\prime},m^{\prime}),(f^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime})
in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
or
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
as in Notation
7.1
and we have that
\dot{\lambda}(\zeta)
or
\dot{\kappa}(\zeta)
sends
(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})\mapsto(f,m)
, we also write
\zeta\circ-\colon\Xi((f^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}),(f,m))\to\Xi(\Gamma(f^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}),(f,m))
and
-\circ\zeta\colon\Xi((f^{\prime},m^{\prime}),(f^{\prime\prime},m^{\prime\prime}))\to\Xi(\Gamma(f,m),(f^{\prime},m^{\prime}))
for the restricted functors.

In the setting of Definition
7.12
, the induced maps between classifying spaces
\zeta\circ-\colon|\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O})|\to|\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})|
and
-\circ\zeta\colon|\Xi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})|\to|\Xi(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})|
are equivalent to the maps between morphism spaces
\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O})\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
and
\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})\to\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})
induced by precomposition and postcomposition with
\zeta\in\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
, respectively. A similar remarks holds for the restricted functors, compared to morphism spaces in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
and
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
.

7.3.
A pushout of Day convolutions

Notation 7.13
.

For a presheaf
A\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}})_{/\dot{\kappa}}
and for
(f,m)
as in Notation
7.1
with
(f,m)
of
\mathbb{N}
-grading 1, we denote by
A(f,m)\subseteq A(\mathcal{O})
the preimage of
(f,m)\in\dot{\kappa}(\mathcal{O})
, using that the latter space is homotopy discrete.

Using Notations
7.1
and
7.13
, we have an identification of spaces
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}(f,m)\simeq\dot{\kappa}^{2}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+})(f,m)
.
By virtue of Corollary
7.4
, our next goal becomes to compute the presheaf
\dot{\kappa}^{2}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+})\in\mathscr{P}(\dot{\mathcal{T}})
.
Recall from Example

that
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+}\simeq\mathcal{C}[*\ ;\ \mathbf{B}C_{2},*]^{\mathrm{sm}}
is a two-step symmetric monoidal extension of
{(\infty,2)}
-categories. This can be rephrased as follows in terms of elementary and non-relative extensions, i.e. as in Definition
4.2
with
\partial X=\emptyset
.

Lemma 7.15
.

For

as in Notation

of

7.4.
A categorical interpretation

We next introduce a categorical interpretation of the spaces
(\theta_{i}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(f,m)
.

Notation 7.16
.

We will use in the following that
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
has a structure of module over
\dot{\mathcal{L}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
; the module structure is obtained by restricting the symmetric monoidal structure on
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{+})
to its full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategories
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
and
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
. We shall use the notation “
-\sqcup-
” for this module structure, i.e. the same notation used for the symmetric monoidal structure on
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
.

Lemma 7.17
.

Let
\mathcal{O}\in\dot{\mathcal{T}}

and
(f,m)

be as in Notation
7.1
.
with
(f,m)
of
\mathbb{N}
-grading 1.
Then for
i=1,2,3
the spaces
(\theta_{i}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(\mathcal{O})
and
(\theta_{i}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(f,m)
are equivalent to the classifying spaces of the categories
Q_{i}(\mathcal{O})
and
Q_{i}(f,m)
defined by the following pullback squares in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, respectively:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{i}(\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{O}/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{T}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{O}}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X_{i}}\times\pi^{i}_{\dot{\mathcal{L}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{i}(-)\sqcup p(-)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{i}(f,m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f,m)/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{K}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{(f,m)}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X_{i}}\times\pi^{i}_{\dot{\mathcal{L}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}(-)\sqcup-}

Proof.

Using the equivalences
\theta_{i}\simeq(\phi_{i})_{!}(*)
and
\dot{\lambda}\simeq p_{!}(*)
, the Day convolution
\theta_{i}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda}
can be computed as the left Kan extension of
*
along the composite functor
X_{i}\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}\xrightarrow{\phi_{i}\times p}\dot{\mathcal{T}}\times\dot{\mathcal{T}}\xrightarrow{-\sqcup-}\dot{\mathcal{T}}
. The formula for left Kan extension of presheaves then readily implies the statement for
Q_{i}(\mathcal{O})
. The second statement follows from the splitting
\dot{\mathcal{K}}\times_{\dot{\mathcal{T}}}\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{O}/}\simeq\coprod_{(f,m)\in\dot{\kappa}(\mathcal{O})}\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f,m)/}
, implying a splitting of
{(\infty,1)}
-categories
Q_{i}(\mathcal{O})\simeq\coprod_{(f,m)\in\dot{\kappa}(\mathcal{O})}Q_{i}(f,m)
which is compatible with the splitting of spaces

(\theta_{i}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(\mathcal{O})\simeq\coprod_{(f,m)\in\dot{\kappa}(\mathcal{O})}(\theta_{i}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(f,m)
.
∎

Notation 7.19
.

Recall Definition
7.2
.
We consider the unique edges
e_{\beta}
and
e_{\tilde{\beta}}
of the graphs
G_{\beta,1}
and
G_{\tilde{\beta},1}
as leaves; we equip the first leaf with the unique possible orientation, which we denote
h_{\beta}\in H_{\beta,1}
, and the second with the orientation selecting the first half-edge
1\in\underline{2}\cong H_{\tilde{\beta},1}
, which we denote
h_{\tilde{\beta}}\in H_{\tilde{\beta},1}
. The second orientation is of course not invariant under the
C_{2}
-action on
G_{\tilde{\beta},1}
.

Notation 7.20
.

Let
(f,m)
be leaf-like as Definition
7.2
.
We say that an object
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}(f,m)
(respectively,
\bar{D}\in Q_{3}(f,m)
) as in Notation
7.18
is
orientation-preserving
if
\bar{f}_{u}
sends
h_{\ell}\in H_{u}
to
h_{\beta}
(respectively, to
h_{\tilde{\beta}}
); otherwise, if
\bar{f}_{u}(h_{\ell})
is the other half-edge, we say that
\bar{D}
is
orientation-reversing
. We observe that a morphism in
Q_{1}(f,m)
or
Q_{3}(f,m)
is always between a pair of orientation-preserving objects, or between a pair of orientation-reversing objects. For
i=1,3
we denote by
Q_{i}(f,m)^{\to}
and
Q_{i}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
the split
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategories of
Q_{i}(f,m)
spanned by orientation-preserving and orientation-reversing objects, respectively; we thus have a splitting
Q_{i}(f,m)\simeq Q_{i}(f,m)^{\to}\sqcup Q_{i}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
.

For
(\bar{f},\bar{m})\in\dot{\mathcal{L}}
we similarly consider the decomposition

\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))\simeq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))^{\to}\sqcup\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))^{\leftarrow}.

Lemma 7.21
.

For
(f,m)
leaf-like as in Notation
7.1
, the map
(\theta_{3}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(f,m)\to(\theta_{2}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(f,m)
is a trivial 2-fold covering of spaces.

Proof.

The definition of
Q_{i}(f,m)
as a pullback implies that we have an equivalence of categories
Q_{3}(f,m)\simeq Q_{2}(f,m)\times_{\mathbf{B}C_{2}}*
; since geometric realisations preserve pullbacks over spaces, the map
(\theta_{3}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(f,m)\to(\theta_{2}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(f,m)
is a 2-fold covering of spaces.
To see that it is trivial, we consider the
C_{2}
-action on
Q_{3}(f,m)
whose quotient is
Q_{2}(f,m)\simeq Q_{3}(f,m)/C_{2}
: this
C_{2}
-action is induced by the
C_{2}
-action on
G_{\tilde{\beta},1}
, which inverts the orientation of the unique leaf of
G_{\beta,1}
; consequently, this
C_{2}
-action swaps the subcategories
Q_{3}(f,m)^{\to}
and
Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
. Hence the space
(\theta_{3}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(f,m)\simeq|Q_{3}(f,m)^{\to}|\sqcup|Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}|
is the disjoint union of two spaces, each of which is equivalent to
(\theta_{2}\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}\dot{\lambda})(f,m)
along the covering map.
∎

Combining Lemmas
7.15
and
7.21
, for
(f,m)
leaf-like as in Notation
7.1
we may express
\dot{\kappa}^{2}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+})(f,m)
as the colimit of the following W-shaped diagram in
\mathcal{S}
, whose middle arrows are equivalences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|Q_{2}(f,m)|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|Q_{3}(f,m)^{\to}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}|}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

In order to prove that
\dot{\kappa}^{2}({\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(S)^{+})(f,m)
is contractible, and thus complete the proof of Theorem
A
, we shall
prove the following proposition.

Proposition 7.22
.

Let
(f,m)
be leaf-like as in Notation
7.1
.
With reference to the above diagram, the following hold:

(1)

the space
|Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}|
is contractible;

(2)

the map
|Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}|\to|Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}|
is an equivalence of spaces.

7.5.
Morphism spaces in
Q_{i}(f,m)

In the rest of the section we let the index

be equal to 1 or 3, in particular
X_{i}\simeq*
and we may think of
\phi_{i}
and
\psi_{i}
as objects in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}
and
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
, respectively.
Let
\mathcal{O}
and
(f,m)
be as in Notation
7.1
with
(f,m)
of
\mathbb{N}
-grading 1, and let
\bar{D},\check{D}
be objects in
Q_{i}(f,m)
as in Notation
7.18
. By the definition of

as a pullback from Lemma

, the morphism space

is the pullback of the cospan of spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f,m)/}(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{K}}(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}\sqcup-}

Lemma 7.23
.

The above right diagonal map
\psi_{i}\sqcup-
is an inclusion of spaces.

Proof.

We may regard
\dot{\mathcal{L}}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
and
\dot{\mathcal{K}}(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
as subspaces of
\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\bar{\mathcal{O}},\check{\mathcal{O}})
and
\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\phi_{i}\sqcup\bar{\mathcal{O}},\phi_{i}\sqcup\check{\mathcal{O}})
, respectively. We may then identify the map
\psi_{i}\sqcup-
as the restriction of the map
\phi_{i}\sqcup-
between the last two morphism spaces in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}
. The last map agrees with the map induced on geometric realisations by the functor
\phi_{i}\sqcup-\colon\Xi(\bar{\mathcal{O}},\check{\mathcal{O}})\to\Xi(\phi_{i}\sqcup\bar{\mathcal{O}},\phi_{i}\sqcup\check{\mathcal{O}})
. This last functor exhibits the source category as a split
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory of the target, as a consequence of the following observations:

•

for all
\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{2},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\in\dot{\mathcal{T}}
, the category
\Xi(\mathcal{O}_{1}\sqcup\mathcal{O}_{2},\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
splits as the disjoint union of the categories
\Xi(\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{1})\times\Xi(\mathcal{O}_{2},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{2})
, indexed by the set of all splittings of
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
as a disjoint union
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{2}
;

•

in the case
\mathcal{O}_{1}=\phi_{i}
, we have
\Xi(\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{2})\simeq\emptyset
unless
\mathcal{O}_{2}\simeq\phi_{i}
, in which case we have that
\Xi(\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{2})
is equivalent to
*
(
i=1
) or to
*\sqcup*
(
i=3
).

The functor
\phi_{i}\sqcup-
exhibits
\Xi(\bar{\mathcal{O}},\check{\mathcal{O}})
as (one of) the split
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategories of
\Xi(\phi_{i}\sqcup\bar{\mathcal{O}},\phi_{i}\sqcup\check{\mathcal{O}})
corresponding to the choice
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{1}=\phi_{i}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{2}=\check{\mathcal{O}}
.
∎

It follows that we may also regard
Q_{i}(\bar{D},\check{D})
as a subspace of the morphism space
\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f,m)/}(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
. The latter space can be identified with the fibre at
\check{\zeta}\in\dot{\mathcal{K}}((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
of the map of spaces

-\circ\bar{\zeta}\colon\dot{\mathcal{K}}(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))\to\dot{\mathcal{K}}((f,m),(\check{f},\check{m})).

Lemma 7.24
.

The map of spaces
Q_{i}(\bar{D},\check{D})\to\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f,m)/}(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
induced by the functor
\pi^{i}_{(f,m)}
is essentially surjective.

Proof.

Let
\zeta\colon\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m})\to\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m})
be a morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f,m)/}
, i.e. we are equipped with an equivalence
\zeta\circ\bar{\zeta}\simeq\check{\zeta}
of morphisms in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
, and use Notation
7.8
for
\zeta
. Consider first the case
i=1
;
then
f_{u}\colon G_{\beta,1}\sqcup\bar{G}_{u}\to G_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(G_{\beta,1}\sqcup\check{G}_{u})\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{l}
must send
h_{\beta}\mapsto h_{\beta}
, since both half-edges denoted
h_{\beta}
are the image of
h_{\ell}\in H_{u}
along
\bar{f}_{u}
and
\check{f}_{u}
, respectively. It follows that
\zeta
has the form
\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{1}}\sqcup\zeta^{\prime}
for some
\zeta^{\prime}\colon(\bar{f},\bar{m})\to(\check{f},\check{m})
in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
. The case
i=3
is analogous, replacing
G_{\beta,1}
by
G_{\tilde{\beta},1}
.
∎

Lemmas
7.23
and
7.24
prove that the functor
\pi^{i}_{(f,m)}
is fully faithful, allowing us to identify morphism spaces in
Q_{i}(f,m)
with corresponding morphism spaces in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f,m)/}
. We conclude the subsection with three lemmas comparing morphism spaces of categories of the form
Q_{i}(f,m)
.

Lemma 7.25
.

Let
\tilde{D}
be an object in
Q_{i}(f,m)
as in Notation
7.18
, let
\zeta\colon(\bar{f},\bar{m})\to(\check{f},\check{m})
be a morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
represented by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
, and fix an equivalence
(\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{i}}\sqcup\zeta)\circ\bar{\zeta}\simeq\check{\zeta}
, upgrading
(\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{i}}\sqcup\zeta)
to a morphism in
Q_{i}(\bar{D},\check{D})
. Consider the following commutative diagram of categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\circ\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\circ\check{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\circ\bar{\zeta}}

Suppose that there are split subcategories

\mathcal{D}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m})),\quad\mathcal{E}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m})),\quad\ \mathcal{E}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))

such that

\tilde{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}
, the preimage of
\mathcal{D}
along the left vertical composite
(\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\check{\zeta}
is contained in
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, and similarly
((\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta})^{-1}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{2}
. Assume also that the top horizontal functor
-\circ\zeta
restricts to a functor
\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}
inducing an equivalence
|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
on classifying spaces.
Then we have an equivalence of spaces

-\circ\zeta\colon Q_{i}(f,m)(\check{D},\tilde{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{i}(f,m)(\bar{D},\tilde{D}).

Proof.

We can take classifying spaces in the above diagram of categories, and identify the map between vertical fibres at
\tilde{\zeta}\in|\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))|
with the map
-\circ\zeta\colon Q_{i}(f,m)(\check{D},\tilde{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{i}(f,m)(\bar{D},\tilde{D})
. We can compute these vertical fibres after passing to the following commutative square of subspaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\mathcal{E}_{1}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\mathcal{E}_{2}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\zeta}\in{|\mathcal{D}|}\quad\quad}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\quad\quad{|\mathcal{D}|}\ni\tilde{\zeta}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\circ\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\check{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta}}

As the horizontal arrows are equivalences, so is the induced map between fibres.
∎

Lemma 7.26
.

In the setting of Lemma
7.25
, consider the following commutative diagram of categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\bar{f},\bar{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi(\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m})\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta\circ-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\circ\tilde{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\circ\tilde{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{i}}\sqcup\zeta)\circ-}

Assume that there are split subcategories

\bar{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m})),\quad\check{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))

such that the bottom functor
(\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{i}}\sqcup\zeta)\circ-
restricts to a functor
\mathcal{D}_{1}\to\mathcal{D}_{2}
inducing an equivalence
|\mathcal{D}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{D}_{2}|
. Assume also that there are split subcategories

\begin{split}((\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\tilde{\zeta})^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{1})&\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\bar{f},\bar{m})),\\
((\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\tilde{\zeta})^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{2})&\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))\end{split}

such that the top functor
\zeta\circ-
restricts to a functor
\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}
inducing an equivalence
|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
.
Then we have an equivalence of spaces

\zeta\circ-\colon Q_{i}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\bar{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{i}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\check{D}).

Proof.

The argument is analogous to the one for Lemma
7.25
: we take classifying spaces of the categories in the above diagram and we observe that the hypotheses imply that the induced map between vertical fibres at
\bar{\zeta}
and
\check{\zeta}
is an equivalence.
∎

Lemma 7.27
.

Let
\zeta\colon(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})\to(f,m)
be a morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
represented by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
, and denote by
-\circ\zeta\colon Q_{i}(f,m)\to Q_{i}(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})
the functor induced by
\zeta
in the light of the pullback definition of
Q_{i}(f,m)
and
Q_{i}(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})
from Lemma
7.17
. Let
\bar{D},\check{D}\in Q_{i}(f,m)
as in Notation
7.18
; assume that there are split subcategories
\check{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
and
\check{\zeta}\circ\zeta\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((f^{\prime},m^{\prime}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
such that
-\circ\zeta
restricts to a functor
\mathcal{D}_{1}\to\mathcal{D}_{2}
inducing an equivalence
|\mathcal{D}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{D}_{2}|
.
Then the map
Q_{i}(f,m)(\bar{D},\check{D})\to Q_{i}(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})(\bar{D}\circ\zeta,\check{D}\circ\zeta)
induced by
-\circ\zeta
on morphism spaces is an equivalence.

Proof.

The argument is analogous to the one for Lemma
7.25
, and is based on taking vertical fibres at
\check{\zeta}
and
\check{\zeta}\circ\zeta
in the following square of spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\Xi((f^{\prime},m^{\prime}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))|}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta}\circ\zeta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\circ\zeta}

∎

7.6.
The one-vertex reduction

Our next step is to reduce the proof of Proposition
7.22
to the case in which the target
G_{d}
of
f
is a gaf consisting of a single vertex and having no edges.

Definition 7.28
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
and
(f,m)
be as in Notation
7.1
, with
(f,m)
leaf-like.
For
i=1,3
we denote by
Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)\subseteq Q_{i}(f,m)
the full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory spanned by special diagrams
\bar{D}
as in Notation
7.18
satisfying the following property:

(i)

the set
\bar{f}_{d}^{-1}(E_{d})
is contained in
\bar{E}_{l}\sqcup\bar{E}_{d}
.

We further denote by
Q^{\prime\prime}_{i}(f,m)\subseteq Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)
the full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory spanned by objects
\bar{D}
satisfying the following property:

(ii)

the set
\bar{f}_{d}^{-1}(E_{d})
is contained in
\bar{E}_{l}
.

In Definition
7.28
, note that a priori
\bar{f}_{d}^{-1}(E_{d})
is a subset of the set
\bar{E}_{l}\sqcup\bar{E}_{d}\sqcup\bar{E}_{r}
.

Definition 7.29
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
and
(f,m)
be as in Notation
7.1
,
with
(f,m)
of
\mathbb{N}
-grading
\leq 1
,
and let
X\subseteq E_{d}
be a subset. For
x\in X
, regard
x
as a subset of
H_{d}
with two elements and fix an identification
\underline{2}\simeq x
(an edge orientation). We let
\mathcal{O}^{X}
and
(f^{X},m^{X})
be the essentially unique data fitting in a diagram in
\mathcal{C}_{+}
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\sqcup X\times\underline{2}=B^{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{X}=A,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}_{B}\sqcup\coprod_{X}G_{\mathfrak{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{X}_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{X}_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{X},m^{X})}

with horizontal composition identified with
(f,m)\colon G_{u}\Rightarrow G_{d}
in such a way that for all
x\in X
, the inclusion of finite sets
\underline{2}\simeq x\hookrightarrow H_{d}
coincides with the inclusion of
\underline{2}=H_{\mathfrak{e}}
into the set of half-edges of
G^{X}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\mathrm{Id}_{B}\sqcup\coprod_{X}G_{\mathfrak{e}}
coming from the
x
th
copy of
G_{\mathfrak{e}}
.

We let
\zeta^{X}\colon(f,m)\to(f^{X},m^{X})
be the morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
or
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
given by the above factorisation: in the light of Notation
7.8
, we have

G^{X}_{l}=\mathrm{Id}_{B}\sqcup\coprod_{X}G_{\mathfrak{e}}
and all other data
G^{X}_{r},f^{X}_{u},f^{X}_{d}
are invertible 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms.

In the setting of Definition
7.29
we have a canonical identification
\mathrm{CE}(f)\simeq\mathrm{CE}(f^{X})
; in particular
(f,m)
and
(f^{X},m^{X})
have the same
\mathbb{N}
-grading; it is also immediate that if
(f,m)
is leaf-like, then so is
(f^{X},m^{X})
. We also observe that in the case
X=E_{d}
we have that
G_{d}^{X}
is a gaf without edges.

Lemma 7.30
.

Let
(f,m)
be leaf-like as in Notation
7.1
, and let
i=1,3
.
Consider the functor
-\circ\zeta^{E_{d}}\colon Q_{i}(f^{E_{d}},m^{E_{d}})\to Q_{i}(f,m)
induced by the morphism
\zeta^{E_{d}}
in the light of the pullback definition of
Q_{i}(f,m)
and
Q_{i}(f^{E_{d}},m^{E_{d}})
from Lemma
7.17
. Then
-\circ\zeta^{E_{d}}
is fully faithful with essential image
Q^{\prime\prime}_{i}(f,m)
.

Proof.

The fact that an object is in the essential image of
-\circ\zeta^{E_{d}}
if and only if it lies in
Q_{i}^{\prime\prime}(f,m)
is immediate. To prove that
-\circ\zeta^{E_{d}}
is fully faithful, fix
\bar{D},\check{D}\in Q_{i}(f^{E_{d}},m^{E_{d}})
; we want to prove that
-\circ\zeta^{E_{d}}
induces an equivalence of spaces
Q_{i}(f^{E_{d}},m^{E_{d}})(\bar{D},\check{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{i}(f,m)(\bar{D}\circ\zeta^{E_{d}},\check{D}\circ\zeta^{E_{d}})
. By Lemma
7.27
, it suffices to prove that the functor

-\circ\zeta^{E_{d}}\colon\Xi((f^{E_{d}},m^{E_{d}}),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))\to\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))

is a split inclusion of categories. This is evidently the case: in fact we may characterise the essential image of the last functor as the split subcategory of
\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that
f_{d}^{-1}(E_{d})
is contained in
E_{d,l}
(a priori, it is contained in
E_{d,l}\sqcup\check{E}_{d}\sqcup E_{d,r}
).
∎

Lemma 7.31
.

In the setting of Lemma
7.30
, the inclusions of
{(\infty,1)}
-categories
Q^{\prime\prime}_{i}(f,m)\subseteq Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)\subseteq Q_{i}(f,m)

induce equivalences on classifying spaces.

Proof.

We will prove that the inclusion
Q^{\prime\prime}_{i}(f,m)\hookrightarrow Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)
admits a left adjoint, and that the inclusion
Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)\hookrightarrow Q_{i}(f,m)
admits a right adjoint.

For the first claim, given an object
\bar{D}\in Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)
as in Notation
7.18
, we denote
X=\bar{f}_{d}^{-1}(E_{d})\cap\bar{E}_{d}
and we define a candidate left adjoint object
\check{D}\in Q^{\prime\prime}_{i}(f,m)
together with a morphism
\eta\colon\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)
as follows:

•

we let

(\check{f},\check{m}):=(\bar{f}^{X},\bar{m}^{X})\in\dot{\mathcal{L}}
, we denote by
\bar{\zeta}^{X}\colon(\bar{f},\bar{m})\to(\bar{f}^{X},\bar{m}^{X})
the morphism from Definition
7.29
, and we let
\check{\zeta}
be the composite in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}

(f,m)\xrightarrow{\bar{\zeta}}\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{i}}\sqcup\bar{\zeta}^{X}}\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f}^{X},\bar{m}^{X});

•

the definition of
\check{\zeta}
allows us to upgrade
\eta:=\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{i}}\sqcup\bar{\zeta}^{X}
to a morphism
\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q_{i}(f,m)
.

It is left to prove that for all objects
\tilde{D}\in Q^{\prime\prime}_{i}(f,m)
, precomposition with
\eta
induces an equivalence of spaces

Q_{i}(f,m)(\check{D},\tilde{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{i}(f,m)(\bar{D},\tilde{D})
. By Lemma
7.25
, it suffices to find a split subcategory
\tilde{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
such that
-\circ\eta
restricts to an equivalence
\mathcal{E}_{1}:=((\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\check{\zeta})^{-1}(\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{E}_{2}:=((\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta})^{-1}(\mathcal{D})
.
This is achieved by letting
\tilde{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}
be the split subcategory of
\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that
f_{d}^{-1}(E_{d})
is contained in
E_{d,l}
: note that a priori we have
f_{d}^{-1}(E_{d})\subseteq E_{d,l}\sqcup\tilde{E}_{d}\sqcup E_{d,r}
. We may then identify
\mathcal{E}_{2}
with the split subcategory of
\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
spanned by objects
\zeta
such that
f_{d}^{-1}(X)\subseteq E_{d,l}
, whereas
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\Xi((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
; the functor
-\circ\eta
restricts to an equivalences
\mathcal{E}_{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{E}_{2}
, which a fortiori induces an equivalence of spaces
|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
.
This completes the proof that
Q^{\prime\prime}_{i}(f,m)\hookrightarrow Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)
admits a left adjoint.

We similarly show that the inclusion
Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)\hookrightarrow Q_{i}(f,m)
admits a right adjoint. Given an object
\check{D}\in Q_{i}(f,m)
as in Notation
7.18
we define a candidate right adjoint object
\bar{D}\in Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)
and a morphism
\varepsilon\colon\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q_{i}(f,m)
as follows:

•

we let
X:=\check{E}_{r}\cap\check{f}_{d}^{-1}(E_{d})\subseteq\check{E}_{r}
; we regard each
x\in X
as a subset of two elements of
\check{H}_{r}
and fix an identification
x\simeq\underline{2}
; by abuse of notation we regard
X
also as a subset of
E_{d}
;

•

we let
k=1
if
i=1
, and
k=0
if
i=3
;

•

we factor
\check{G}_{r}=\bar{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{k}\sqcup\check{A}}\sqcup\coprod_{X}G_{\mathfrak{e}})
in the essentially unique way such that for all
x\in X
, the inclusion
\underline{2}\simeq x\hookrightarrow\check{H}_{r}
agrees with the inclusion of
\underline{2}=H_{\mathfrak{e}}
in the set of half-edges of
\check{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{k}\sqcup\check{A}}\sqcup\coprod_{X}G_{\mathfrak{e}})
coming from the

x
th
copy of
G_{\mathfrak{e}}
.

•

we let
(\bar{f},\bar{m}):=(\mathrm{Id}_{\check{A}}\sqcup\coprod_{X}G_{\mathfrak{e}})\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(\check{f},\check{m})
, and we let
\bar{\zeta}\colon(f,m)\to\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m})
be the morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
given by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
by setting
\bar{G}_{l}=\check{G}_{l}
and
(\bar{f}_{\star},\bar{m}_{\star})=(\check{f}_{\star},\check{m}_{\star})
for
\star=u,d
, as well as by considering the gaf
\bar{G}_{r}
constructed above;

•

we let
\zeta_{X}\colon(\bar{f},\bar{m})\to(\check{f},\check{m})
be the morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
given by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
by setting
G_{X,r}=(\mathrm{Id}_{\check{A}}\sqcup\coprod_{X}G_{\mathfrak{e}})
and letting all other data
G_{X,l}
and
(f_{X,\star},m_{X,\star})
for
\star=u,d
be the obvious equivalences;

•

the definition of
\bar{\zeta}
allows us to upgrade
\varepsilon:=\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{i}}\sqcup\zeta_{X}
to a morphism
\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q_{i}(f,m)
, since we have an evident equivalence
\varepsilon\circ\bar{\zeta}\simeq\check{\zeta}
.

It is left to prove that for all
\tilde{D}\in Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)
, postcomposition with
\varepsilon
induces an equivalence
Q_{i}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\bar{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{i}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\check{D})
; for this we shall apply Lemma
7.26
. By further abuse of notation, let
X\subseteq\tilde{E}_{l}\sqcup\tilde{E}_{d}
denote also the subset
\tilde{f}_{d}^{-1}(X)
.
We let
\mathcal{D}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
denote the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that
f_{d}
restricts to the canonical, edgewise oriented identification
X\cong X
between the two subsets of
\bar{E}_{d}\subseteq E_{d,l}\sqcup\bar{E}_{d}\sqcup E_{d,r}
and of
E_{d}
that we have denoted
X
; and we let
\mathcal{D}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
denote the split
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that
f_{d}^{-1}(X)\subseteq E_{d,r}
. Then

\varepsilon\circ-\colon\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))\to\Xi((f,m),\psi_{i}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))

restricts to an equivalence
\mathcal{D}_{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{D}_{2}
, inducing an equivalence on classifying spaces.

Moreover, the preimage
\mathcal{E}_{1}
of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
in
\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
is the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that
f_{d}
restricts to the canonical, edgewise oriented identification
X\cong X
between the two subsets of
\bar{E}_{d}\subseteq E_{d,l}\sqcup\bar{E}_{d}\sqcup E_{d,r}
and of
\tilde{E}_{d}
denoted
X
; and the preimage
\mathcal{E}_{2}
of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
in
\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
is the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that
f_{d}^{-1}(X)\subseteq E_{d,r}
. Again,
