\zeta_{X}\circ-\colon\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\bar{f},\bar{m}))\to\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
restricts to an equivalence
\mathcal{E}_{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{E}_{2}
, inducing an equivalence
|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
. This completes the proof that
Q^{\prime}_{i}(f,m)\hookrightarrow Q_{i}(f,m)
admits a right adjoint.
∎

The combination of Lemmas
7.30
and
7.31
allows to reduce the proof of Proposition
7.22
to the special case of a leaf-like
(f,m)\in\dot{\mathcal{K}}
as in Notation
7.1
whose target
G_{d}
is a gaf without edges.
We conclude the subsection by further reducing to the case in which
G_{d}
consists of a single (attaching or inner) vertex.

Let
(f,m)\in\dot{\mathcal{K}}
be leaf-like as in Notation
7.1
with
E_{d}=\emptyset
, and recall Notation
7.16
. Then there is a canonical splitting
(f,m)\simeq(f_{1},m_{1})\sqcup(f_{2},m_{2})
such that
(f_{2},m_{2})\in\dot{\mathcal{L}}
and such that
G_{1,d}
consists of a single vertex, which is the image of the special leaf in
G_{d}
along
f
. For
i=1,3
, we have a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
as follows, which we regard as a morphism between cospans:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{K}}\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f_{1},m_{1})/}\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}_{(f_{2},m_{2})/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f,m)/}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi_{i}\sqcup-)\times-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}\times d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}

The induced functor between pullbacks
Q_{i}(f_{1},m_{1})\times\dot{\mathcal{L}}_{(f_{2},m_{2})/}\to Q_{i}(f,m)
is an equivalence; taking classifying spaces, we obtain an equivalence
|Q_{i}(f_{1},m_{1})|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|Q_{i}(f,m)|
, restricting to equivalences between subsapces corresponding to split
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategories with decorations “
\to
” and “
\leftarrow
”. The following corollary summarizes the reductions achieved in this subsection.

Corollary 7.32
.

Assume that Proposition
7.22
holds when
(f,m)
is such that
G_{d}
consists of a single vertex. Then Theorem
A
holds.

7.7.
The equivalence between
|Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}|
and
|Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}|

The goal of this subsection is to prove Proposition
7.22
(2). We fix
\mathcal{O}
and
(f,m)
as in Notation
7.1
throughout the subsection, with
(f,m)
leaf-like and with
G_{d}
consisting of a single vertex. For the arguments in this subsection, it suffices in fact to assume
E_{d}=\emptyset
.

Notation 7.33
.

For
T\in\mathrm{Fin}
we abuse notation and denote by
T
all of the following:

•

the identity gaf of
T
;

•

the object in
\dot{\mathcal{T}}
represented by the
\mathrm{O}_{1}
-shaped diagram comprising twice the identity gaf of
T
;

•

the object in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
represented by the identity of the identity gaf of
T
.

We denote by
\zeta^{+}\colon\psi_{3}\to(\psi_{1}\sqcup\underline{1})
the morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
given, in the light of Notation
7.8
, by letting
G^{+}_{l}:=\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{2}}
and
G^{+}_{r}:=G_{\mathfrak{t}}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\mu
, and by letting
f^{+}_{u}
and
f^{+}_{d}
be the evident identifications.

The map of spaces
|Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}|\to|Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}|
is induced on geometric realisations by the functor
a^{\leftarrow}\colon Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}\to Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
which we describe in the following.
The morphism
\zeta^{+}
from Notation
7.33
induces a natural transformation

z\colon(\psi_{3}\sqcup-)\Rightarrow(\psi_{1}\sqcup\underline{1}\sqcup-)\simeq(\psi_{1}\sqcup-)\circ(\underline{1}\sqcup-)

of functors
\dot{\mathcal{L}}\to\dot{\mathcal{K}}
.
We may now define a functor
a\colon Q_{3}(f,m)\to Q_{1}(f,m)
using the pullback definition of the latter category from Lemma
7.17
: we let the
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
-coordinate of
a
be
\underline{1}\sqcup\pi^{3}_{\dot{\mathcal{L}}}
; we are then forced to let the
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
-coordinate of
a
be
(\psi_{1}\sqcup\underline{1}\sqcup p\pi^{3}_{\dot{\mathcal{L}}})
; and we let the
\dot{\mathcal{K}}_{(f,m)/}
-coordinate of
a
be the concatenation of
\pi_{(f,m)}^{3}
and the functor
Q_{3}(f,m)\to\mathrm{Fun}([1],\dot{\mathcal{K}})
corresponding to the natural transformation
zp\pi_{\dot{\mathcal{L}}}^{3}
.
The functor
a
restricts to a functor
a^{\leftarrow}\colon Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}\to Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
between split
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategories, and our goal is to show that
a^{\leftarrow}
induces an equivalence on classifying spaces.

To make sense of the next definition, we observe that if
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
is an object represented by a diagram as in Notation
7.18
, then the vertex
\bar{\rho}_{r}(1)\in\bar{V}_{r}\sqcup A
is in fact an inner vertex in
\bar{V}_{r}
, and it actually constitutes an isolated component of the gaf
\bar{G}_{r}
: this follows from the fact that
\bar{f}_{u}^{-1}(\bar{v})
must be a subtree of
G_{u}
to which the special leaf
e_{\ell}
is attached along
h_{\ell}
; the maximal such subtree in
G_{u}
consists of the sole vertex
v_{\ell}
: here we are using in particular that
E_{d}=\emptyset
, so all edges of
G_{u}
are collapsed along
f
and hence
e_{\ell}
is the unique edge attached to
v_{\ell}
in
G_{u}
(and not only the unique edge attached to
v_{\ell}
that is collapsed along
f
). Hence
\bar{f}_{u}^{-1}(\bar{v})=\left\{v_{\ell}\right\}
, and it follows that
\bar{v}
has valence
0
in
\bar{G}_{r}
. For the same reason, no edge in either
\bar{G}_{u}
or
\bar{G}_{d}
can be attached to any point in
\bar{\rho}_{r}^{-1}(\bar{\rho}_{r}(1))\cap\bar{A}\subseteq\bar{A}
.

Definition 7.34
.

We denote by
Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}\subseteq Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
the full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory spanned by objects
\bar{D}
as in Notation
7.18
satisfying the following property:

(iii)

let
1\in\underline{1}\subseteq\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{A}
, and let
\bar{v}:=\bar{\rho}_{r}(1)
; then the subset
\bar{\rho}_{r}^{-1}(\bar{v})\subseteq\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{A}
comprises precisely two elements. The first element is
1\in\underline{1}
; we denote in this case by
\bar{1}\in\bar{A}
the second element, and we split
\bar{A}=\bar{\underline{1}}\sqcup\bar{A}^{\vee}
.

We further denote by
Q_{1}^{\vee\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}\subseteq Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
the full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory spanned by objects
\bar{D}
satisfying the following additional property:

(iv)

there is a (necessarily unique) splitting
(\bar{f},\bar{m})\simeq\bar{\underline{1}}\sqcup(\bar{f}^{\vee},\bar{m}^{\vee})
restricting on target objects to the above splitting
\bar{A}=\bar{\underline{1}}\sqcup\bar{A}^{\vee}
.

Lemma 7.35
.

The functor
a^{\leftarrow}
is fully faithful with essential image
Q_{1}^{\vee\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
.

Proof.

It is immediate to identify the essential image of
a^{\leftarrow}
with
Q_{1}^{\vee\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
. To prove full faithfulness, let
\bar{D},\check{D}\in Q_{3}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
; we want to prove that
a^{\leftarrow}
induces an equivalence of spaces
Q_{3}(f,m)(\bar{D},\check{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{1}(f,m)(a^{\leftarrow}(\bar{D}),a^{\leftarrow}(\check{D}))
. Similarly as in Lemma
7.27
, we identify this map of morphism spaces with the map between vertical fibres at
\check{\zeta}
and
(\zeta^{+}\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{(\check{f},\check{m})})\circ\check{\zeta}
in the following commutative square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\Xi((f,m),\psi_{3}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup\underline{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))|}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi_{3}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\zeta^{+}\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{(\check{f},\check{m})})\circ(\psi_{1}\sqcup\underline{1}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\zeta^{+}\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{(\check{f},\check{m})})\circ-}

We conclude by observing that the functor

(\zeta^{+}\sqcup\mathrm{Id})\circ-\colon\Xi((f,m),\psi_{3}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))\to\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup\underline{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))

restricts to an equivalence
\mathcal{D}_{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{D}_{2}
, where
\check{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{1}:=\Xi((f,m),\psi_{3}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))^{\leftarrow}
,
and where
(\zeta^{+}\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{(\check{f},\check{m})})\circ\check{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup\underline{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))^{\leftarrow}
is the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that

\rho_{d,r}^{-1}(\rho_{d,r}(1))=\left\{1,\bar{1}\right\}
.
∎

Lemma 7.36
.

The inclusions of
{(\infty,1)}
-categories
Q_{1}^{\vee\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}\subseteq Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}\subseteq Q_{i}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
induce equivalences on classifying spaces.

Proof.

We prove that the inclusion
Q_{1}^{\vee\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}\subseteq Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
admits a left adjoint, and the inclusion
Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}\subseteq Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
admits a right adjoint. For the first claim, given
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
, we define a candidate left adjoint object
\check{D}\in Q_{1}^{\vee\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
with a morphism
\eta\colon\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
as follows:

•

we let
\bar{1}
be the unique element in
\bar{\rho}_{r}^{-1}(\bar{\rho}_{r}(1))\cap\bar{A}
; we observe that the leaf-like assumption on
(f,m)
implies that no edge in either
\bar{G}_{u},\bar{G}_{d}
can be attached to
\bar{1}
; we may therefore split
(\bar{f},\bar{m})=\mathrm{Id}_{C\to\bar{\underline{1}}}\sqcup(\bar{f}^{\vee},\bar{m}^{\vee})
, where
C=\bar{\rho}_{d}^{-1}(\bar{1})\subseteq\bar{B}
; we have in fact
\bar{B}=C\sqcup\bar{B}^{\vee}
;

•

we let
(\check{f},\check{m})=\bar{\underline{1}}\sqcup(\bar{f}^{\vee},\bar{m}^{\vee})
and we let
\zeta^{C}\colon(\bar{f},\bar{m})\to(\check{f},\check{m})
be the morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
given by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
by letting
G^{C}_{l}
be the map of finite sets
(C\to\bar{\underline{1}})\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{\bar{B}^{\vee}}
, and by letting
G^{C}_{r}
,
f^{C}_{u}
and
f^{C}_{d}
be the evident identifications;

•

we let
\check{\zeta}\colon(f,m)\to(\check{f},\check{m})
be the following composite in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
:

(f,m)\xrightarrow{\bar{\zeta}}\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{1}}\sqcup\zeta^{C}}\psi_{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m});

•

by definition of
\check{\zeta}
we have that
\eta:=\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{1}}\sqcup\zeta^{C}
upgrades to a morphism
\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q_{1}(f,m)
.

We need to check that for all
\tilde{D}\in Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
, precomposition with
\eta
induces an equivalence of spaces
Q_{1}(f,m)(\check{D},\tilde{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{1}(f,m)(\bar{D},\tilde{D})
.
In order to apply Lemma
7.25
, we let
\tilde{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}:=\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))^{\leftarrow}
.
The preimages of
\mathcal{D}
in

\Xi((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
and
\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
are the split subcategories
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
spanned by objects
\zeta
such that
\rho_{d,r}^{-1}(\rho_{d,r}(1))
is the set
\left\{1,\check{1}\right\}\subseteq\underline{1}\sqcup\check{A}
, respectively the set
\left\{1,\bar{1}\right\}\subseteq\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{A}
; the functor
-\circ\zeta^{C}\colon\Xi((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))\to\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
restricts to an equivalence
\mathcal{E}_{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{E}_{2}
, inducing an equivalence
|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
.

For the second claim, given
\check{D}\in Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
, we define a candidate right adjoint objects
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
with a morphism
\varepsilon\colon\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q_{1}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
as follows:

•

we let
C=\check{\rho}_{r}^{-1}(\check{\rho}_{r}(1))\cap\check{A}\subseteq\check{A}
, and we split
\check{A}=C\sqcup\check{A}^{\vee}
;

•

we factor
\check{G}_{r}=\bar{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}}\sqcup(C\to\check{\underline{1}})\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{\check{A}^{\vee}})
in the essentially unique way, where the second factor is a map of finite sets;

•

we let
(\bar{f},\bar{m})=((C\to\check{\underline{1}})\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{\check{A}^{\vee}})\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(\check{f},\check{m})
, and we define
\bar{\zeta}\colon(f,m)\to\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m})
to be the morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
given by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
by setting
\bar{G}_{l}=\check{G}_{l}
and
(\bar{f}_{\star},\bar{m}_{\star})=(\check{f}_{\star},\check{m}_{\star})
for
\star=u,d
, as well as by considering the gaf
\bar{G}_{r}
constructed above;

•

we let
\zeta_{C}\colon(\bar{f},\bar{m})\to(\check{f},\check{m})
be the morphism in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
given by a special diagram as in Notation
7.8
by letting
G_{C,r}
be the map of finite sets
(C\to\check{\underline{1}})\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{\check{A}^{\vee}}
and by letting
G_{C,l},f_{C,u},f_{C,d}
be the evident identifications;

•

the definition of
\bar{\zeta}
allows us to upgrade
\varepsilon:=\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{i}}\sqcup\zeta_{C}
to a morphism
\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q_{i}(f,m)
, since we have an evident equivalence
\varepsilon\circ\bar{\zeta}\simeq\check{\zeta}
.

We need to check that for all
\tilde{D}\in Q_{1}^{\vee}(f,m)^{\leftarrow}
, postcomposition with
\varepsilon
induces an equivalence
Q_{1}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\bar{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{1}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\check{D})
.
In order to apply Lemma
7.26

we let
\check{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))^{\leftarrow}
denote the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that

\rho_{d,r}^{-1}(\rho_{d,r}(1))=\underline{1}\sqcup C\subseteq\underline{1}\sqcup\check{A}
;
and we let
\bar{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))^{\leftarrow}
denote the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that

\rho_{d,r}^{-1}(\rho_{d,r}(1))=\left\{1,\bar{1}\right\}\subseteq\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{A}
. Then
\varepsilon\circ-
restricts to an equivalence
\mathcal{D}_{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{D}_{2}
, inducing and equivalence
|\mathcal{D}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{D}_{2}|
.

Moreover, the preimage
\mathcal{E}_{1}
of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
in
\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
is the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that
G_{r}
splits as a disjoint union of the map of finite sets
(\bar{\underline{1}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\tilde{\underline{1}})
and another gaf; and the preimage
\mathcal{E}_{2}
of
\mathcal{D}_{2}
in
\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
is the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that
G_{r}
splits as a disjoint union of the map of finite sets
(C\to\tilde{\underline{1}})
and another gaf; we have that
\zeta_{C}\circ-
restricts to an equivalence
\mathcal{E}_{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{E}_{2}
, inducing and equivalence
|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.22
(2).

We reduce to the setting in which
G_{d}
consists of a single vertex using Corollary
7.32
; we then apply
Lemmas
7.35
and
7.36
.
∎

7.8.
The spine reduction

The goal of this subsection and the next is to prove Proposition
7.22
(1). We fix
\mathcal{O}
and
(f,m)
as in Notation
7.1
throughout the next two subsections, with
(f,m)
leaf-like and with
G_{d}
consisting of a single vertex
x
. We distinguish two cases: the “attaching case” is the one in which
A=\left\{x\right\}
and
V_{d}=\emptyset
; the “inner case” is the one in which
V_{d}=\left\{x\right\}
and
A=\emptyset
. We let
v_{\ell}\in V_{u}
,
h_{\ell}\in H_{u}
and
e_{\ell}\in E_{u}
be as in Definition
7.2
. The key result of this subsection is that we can reduce to the case in which
G_{u}
is a linear graph, i.e. a connected graph having precisely two vertices of valence 1 (the “endpoints”) and all other vertices of valence 2.
More precisely, in the inner case we will reduce to the case in which
G_{u}
is linear with unique edge
e_{\ell}
; in the attaching case we will reduce to the case in which
G_{u}
is linear and its endpoints are
x\in A
and
v_{\ell}
. Along the way we will replace
Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}
by a sequence of
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategories having equivalent classifying spaces.

The first step is
to replace the categories
\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
, for varying
(\bar{f},\bar{m})
and
(\check{f},\check{m})
in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
, with smaller subcategories having equivalent classifying space.

Remark 7.37
.

Let
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}
as in Notation
7.18
; then each edge in the gaf
\bar{G}_{l}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{r}
is contracted along
\bar{f}_{d}
, since
E_{d}=\emptyset
. Hence
\bar{G}_{l}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{r}
, and a fortiori its subgaf
\bar{G}_{r}
, are disjoint unions of (based and unbased) trees.

Definition 7.38
.

Let
\bar{\mathcal{O}},\check{\mathcal{O}}\in\dot{\mathcal{T}}
be as in Notation
7.1
. We denote by
\Xi^{*}(\bar{\mathcal{O}},\check{\mathcal{O}})
the full subcategory of
\Xi(\bar{\mathcal{O}},\check{\mathcal{O}})
spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that every edge of
G_{d,r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{d,l}
coming from
G_{d,r}
, as well as every leaf coming from
G_{d,l}
, is
not
contracted along
f_{d}
.

We further denote by
\Xi^{**}(\bar{\mathcal{O}},\check{\mathcal{O}})\subseteq\Xi^{*}(\bar{\mathcal{O}},\check{\mathcal{O}})
the full subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that, in addition,
(f_{r},m_{r})
is an equivalence, and no leaf in
G_{u,l}
is contracted along
f_{d,l}
. We will in this case simplify the notation of the data associated with
\zeta
and abbreviate
G_{r}:=G_{u,r}\simeq G_{d,r}
.

For
(\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m})
in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
or in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
we define similarly the full subcategories

\Xi^{**}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))\subseteq\Xi^{*}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))\subseteq\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m})).

Whenever we have a split subcategory inclusion
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
, we denote by
\mathcal{D}^{**}
the intersection
\mathcal{D}\cap\Xi^{**}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
.

Lemma 7.39
.

Let
(\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m})
be objects in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
or in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
. Then the inclusion
\Xi^{**}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))\subseteq\Xi^{*}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
admits a left adjoint, and the inclusion
\Xi^{*}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))\subseteq\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
admits a right adjoint.

Proof.

We omit “
((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
” from the notation throughout the proof.
For the first claim, given an object
\zeta\in\Xi^{*}
, we define a candidate left adjoint object
\zeta^{\prime}\in\Xi^{**}
together with a morphism
\eta\colon\zeta\to\zeta^{\prime}
as follows. We define both
G^{\prime}_{u,r}
and
G^{\prime}_{d,r}
as
G_{d,r}\in\mathcal{C}(\check{A},\bar{A})
, we let

G^{\prime}_{d,l}:=G_{d,l}\in\mathcal{C}(\bar{B},\check{B})
, and we let

G^{\prime}_{u,l}\in\mathcal{C}(\bar{B},\check{B})

be the gaf obtained from
G_{u,l}
by iteratively collapsing all leaves that are collapsed along
f_{l}
. We have essentially unique factorisations

(f_{\star},m_{\star})\colon G_{u,\star}\xrightarrow{(f^{*}_{\star},m^{*}_{\star})}G^{\prime}_{u,\star}\xrightarrow{(f^{\prime}_{\star},m^{\prime}_{\star})}G^{\prime}_{d,\star}=G_{d,\star}

for
\star=l,r
, such that
f_{r}^{*}
is the identity of
G_{d,r}
, and
f_{l}^{*}
is the aforementioned leaf-collapsing map of gaf’s. We further set
(f^{\prime}_{d},m^{\prime}_{d}):=(f_{d},m_{d})
and
(f^{\prime}_{u},m^{\prime}_{u}):=((f^{*}_{r},m^{*}_{r})\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(f^{*}_{l},m^{*}_{l}))\circ(f_{u},m_{u})
. We let
\eta
be the morphism induced by the morphisms
(f^{*}_{\star},m^{*}_{\star})
for
\star=l,r
, in the light of the above factorisations of
(f_{\star},m_{\star})
and
(f^{\prime}_{u},m^{\prime}_{u})
. It is immediate that for any
\zeta^{\prime\prime}\in\Xi^{**}
we have a bijection of sets

-\circ\eta\colon\Xi(\zeta^{\prime},\zeta^{\prime\prime})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Xi(\zeta,\zeta^{\prime\prime})
.

For the second claim, given
\zeta^{\prime}\in\Xi
, we define
\zeta\in\Xi^{*}
and
\varepsilon\colon\zeta\to\zeta^{\prime}
as follows: we let
G_{d,r}
be the gaf obtained from
G^{\prime}_{d,r}
by collapsing all edges that are collapsed along
f^{\prime}_{d}
, and we let
G_{d,l}
be the gaf obtained from
G^{\prime}_{d,l}
by iteratively collapsing all leaves that are collapsed along
f^{\prime}_{d}
; there are then essentially unique 2-morphisms
(f^{*}_{\star},m^{*}_{\star})\colon G^{\prime}_{d,\star}\to G_{d,\star}
and
(f_{d},m_{d})\colon G_{d,r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{d,l}\to\bar{G}_{d}
in
\mathcal{C}
fitting into a factorisation of 2-morphisms
(f^{\prime}_{d},m^{\prime}_{d})=(f_{d},m_{d})\circ((f^{*}_{r},m^{*}_{r})\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G^{\prime}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(f^{*}_{l},m^{*}_{l}))
, such that
f^{*}_{\star}
is the edge-collapsing or iterated leaf-collapsing map described above.
We further set
G_{u,\star}:=G^{\prime}_{u,\star}
,
(f_{\star},m_{\star}):=(f^{*}_{\star},m^{*}_{\star})\circ(f^{\prime}_{\star},m^{\prime}_{\star})
for
\star=l,r
, and
(f_{u},m_{u})=(f^{\prime}_{u},m^{\prime}_{u})
. We let
\varepsilon
be the morphism induced by
(f^{*}_{\star},m^{*}_{\star})
in the light of the above factorisations. It is immediate that for any
\zeta^{\prime\prime}\in\Xi^{*}
we have a bijection of sets
\varepsilon\circ-\colon\Xi(\zeta^{\prime\prime},\zeta)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Xi(\zeta^{\prime\prime},\zeta^{\prime})
.
∎

Notation 7.40
.

Thanks to Lemma
7.39
, we will be able henceforth to represent any object in
Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}
, up to equivalence, by a special diagram
\bar{D}
as in Notation
7.18
, such that
\bar{\zeta}\in\Xi^{**}((f,m),(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
. We denote by
\bar{x}:=\bar{\rho}_{r}(1)\in A\sqcup\bar{V}_{r}
the image of the unique element
1\in\underline{1}\subseteq\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{A}
.

Remark 7.41
.

Let
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}
as in Notation
7.40
; then Remark
7.37
implies that
\bar{G}_{r}
is a gaf without edges. We further observe that the image of
\bar{\rho}_{r}\colon\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{A}\to A\sqcup\bar{V}_{r}
contains all of
\bar{V}_{r}
: if we had an element
v\in\bar{V}_{r}
which is not in the image of
\bar{\rho}_{r}
, then
v\neq\bar{x}
and
v
would constitute on its own a component in
\bar{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{l}
; however the latter gaf must be connected, since it is a quotient of the tree
G_{u}
.

Definition 7.42
.

We denote by
Q_{1}^{\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}\subseteq Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}
the full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory spanned by objects
\bar{D}
as in Notation
7.40
satisfying the following properties:

(v)

\bar{G}_{d}
has no leaves; moreover
A\sqcup\bar{V}_{r}=A\cup\left\{\bar{x}\right\}
, i.e., each vertex in
\bar{G}_{r}
is an attaching vertex or is equal to
\bar{x}
(or both).

We denote by
Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}\subseteq Q_{1}^{\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
the full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory spanned by objects
\bar{D}
satisfying the following additional requirement:

(vi)

\bar{G}_{u}
has no leaves; moreover
\bar{G}_{u}
and
\bar{G}_{d}
have no component consisting of a single inner vertex.

Lemma 7.43
.

The inclusions
Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}\subseteq Q_{1}^{\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}\subseteq Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}
induce equivalences on classifying spaces.

Proof.

We will prove that the first inclusion admits a left adjoint, and the second a right adjoint.
For the first claim, given
\bar{D}\in Q^{\dagger}_{1}(f,m)^{\to}
as in Notation
7.40
, we define
\check{D}\in Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
together with a morphism
\eta\colon\bar{D}\to\check{D}
as follows:

•

we let
G^{\dagger\dagger}\in\mathcal{C}(\bar{B},\bar{A})
denote the gaf obtained from
\bar{G}_{u}
by iteratively collapsing leaves; since
\bar{G}_{d}
has no leaves, we have a factorisation in
\mathcal{C}(\bar{B},\bar{A})

(\bar{f},\bar{m})\colon\bar{G}_{u}\xrightarrow{(f^{\dagger}_{u},m^{\dagger}_{u})}G^{\dagger\dagger}\xrightarrow{(f^{\dagger\dagger},m^{\dagger\dagger})}\bar{G}_{d};

•

we have an essentially unique horizontal factorisation of
(f^{\dagger\dagger},m^{\dagger\dagger})
as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{A}:=\bar{A},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\dagger}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{G}_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{G}_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\check{f},\check{m})}

where
\check{G}_{u},\check{G}_{d}
have no components consisting of a single inner vertex,
G^{\dagger}_{l}
has no edge, and the restricted map
\rho_{l}^{\dagger}\colon(\rho_{l}^{\dagger})^{-1}(\check{B})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\check{B}
is bijective; this restricts to horizontal factorisations
G^{\dagger\dagger}=\check{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G^{\dagger}_{l}
and
\bar{G}_{d}=\check{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G^{\dagger}_{l}
;

•

we let
G^{\dagger}_{r}
be the identity gaf of
\check{A}:=\bar{A}
, and we let

(f^{\dagger}_{d},m^{\dagger}_{d})\colon\bar{G}_{d}\xrightarrow{\simeq}G^{\dagger}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G^{\dagger}_{l}
be the above identification;

•

the above data define a morphism
\zeta^{\dagger}\colon(\bar{f},\bar{m})\to(\check{f},\check{m})
in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
; we let
\check{\zeta}:=(\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{1}}\sqcup\zeta^{\dagger})\circ\bar{\zeta}
;

•

the above data define
\check{D}
, represented by a diagram as in Notation
7.40
; by definition of
\check{\zeta}
, the morphism
\eta:=\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{1}}\sqcup\zeta^{\dagger}
upgrades to a morphism
\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q_{1}(f,m)
.

Given
\tilde{D}\in Q^{\dagger\dagger}_{1}(f,m)^{\to}
, we argue that
-\circ\eta\colon Q_{1}(f,m)(\check{D},\tilde{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{1}(f,m)(\bar{D},\tilde{D})
is an equivalence by checking that the hypotheses of
Lemma
7.25
are fulfilled.
Let
\tilde{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
denote the split subcategory comprising objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that the following holds: at most one component of
G_{d,r}
is an unbased tree, and if such component exists, then it contains the element
\rho_{d,r}(1)
, where
1\in\underline{1}\subseteq\underline{1}\sqcup\tilde{A}
.
Let moreover
\mathcal{E}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
denote the split subcategories spanned by objects
\zeta
such that, in both cases, the following holds: no component of
G_{d,r}
is an unbased tree. Then we have
((\psi_{1}\sqcup-)\circ\check{\zeta})^{-1}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{1}
: indeed, if
\zeta\in\Xi((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
and if we know that
\check{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(\underline{1}\sqcup G_{d,r})
has at most one unbased tree marked by
\underline{1}
, then
G_{d,r}
cannot contain any unbased trees. Similarly,
((\psi_{1}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta})^{-1}(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{2}
.

We observe that the functor

-\circ\zeta^{\dagger}\colon\Xi((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))\to\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))

restricts to a functor
\Xi^{**}((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))\to\Xi^{**}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
, as a consequence of the fact that
(f_{d}^{\dagger},m_{d}^{\dagger})
is an equivalence.
Moreover
\mathcal{E}^{**}_{2}\subseteq\Xi^{**}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
may be characterised as the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that
G_{r}
contains no isolated inner vertices. We observe the following for
\zeta\in\mathcal{E}^{**}_{2}
.

•

Since
\tilde{G}_{u}
contains no isolated inner vertex and no leaf, we have that any leaf in the gaf
G_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\tilde{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{u,l}
must come from a leaf in
G_{u,l}
; however, since
\zeta\in\Xi^{**}((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
, we have that also
G_{u,l}
has no leaves and we conclude that
G_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\tilde{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{u,l}
has no leaves as well. It follows that for
\zeta\in\mathcal{E}^{**}_{2}
we have that
f_{u}\colon\bar{G}_{u}\to G_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\tilde{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{u,l}
must iteratively collapse all leaves of
\bar{G}_{u}
, i.e.
f_{u}
factors through
f^{\dagger}_{u}
.

•

Recall that the set of isolated vertices of
\bar{G}_{d}
is in bijection with
V_{l}^{\dagger}
. For an isolated inner vertex
v
of
\bar{G}_{d}
, the unbased tree
f_{d}^{-1}(v)
, which is a subtree of
G_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\tilde{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{d,l}
, cannot contain any inner vertices coming from
G_{r}
, because the latter has no leaves and no isolated inner vertices. It follows that
f_{d}^{-1}(v)
can be regarded as an unbased subtree of
\tilde{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{d,l}
; again, however, no inner vertices can come from
\tilde{G}_{d}
. We conclude that
f_{d}^{-1}(v)
is an unbased subtree of
G_{d,l}
, and since the latter has no leaves, we have that
f_{d}^{-1}(v)
consists of a single inner vertex coming from
G_{d,l}
. Moreover, every isolated inner vertex in
G_{d,l}
has preimage along
f_{l}
consisting again of an isolated inner vertex, as
G_{u,l}
has no leaves. It follows that the 2-morphism
(f_{l},m_{l})
canonically factors as the horizontal composition of
G_{l}^{\dagger}
and another 2-morphism.

The previous remarks show that the restricted functor
-\circ\zeta^{\dagger}\colon\mathcal{E}^{**}_{1}\to\mathcal{E}^{**}_{2}
is an equivalence of categories; we have therefore
-\circ\zeta^{\dagger}\colon|\mathcal{E}^{**}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}^{**}_{2}|
and hence, by Lemma
7.39
, we also have
-\circ\zeta^{\dagger}\colon|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
.

For the second claim, given
\check{D}\in Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}
as in Notation
7.40
, we define
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}^{\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
together with a morphism
\varepsilon\colon\bar{D}\to\check{D}
as follows:

•

we factor
\check{G}_{r}
in the essentially unique way as a horizontal composition of gaf’s without edges
\underline{1}\sqcup\check{A}\xrightarrow{\underline{1}\sqcup G_{r}^{\dagger}}\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{A}\xrightarrow{\bar{G}_{r}}A
, where
\bar{V}_{r}=\bar{V}_{r}\cap\left\{\bar{\rho}_{r}(1)\right\}
, and the restriction
\rho^{\dagger}_{r}\colon(\rho^{\dagger}_{r})^{-1}(\bar{A})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{A}
is a bijection;

•

we let
G^{\dagger}_{l}
be the identity gaf of
\bar{B}:=\check{B}
, and we let
\bar{G}_{l}:=\check{G}_{l}
;

•

we let
\bar{G}_{d}\in\mathcal{C}(\bar{B},\bar{A})
denote the gaf obtained from
G_{r}^{\dagger}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{d}
by iteratively collapsing all leaves, and we let
\bar{G}_{u}=G_{r}^{\dagger}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{u}
;

•

we factor
(\check{f}_{d},\check{m}_{d})=(\bar{f}_{d},\bar{m}_{d})\circ(\bar{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(\underline{1}\sqcup(f^{\dagger}_{d},m^{\dagger}_{d}))\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{l})
in the essentially unique way for which
f^{\dagger}_{d}\colon G_{r}^{\dagger}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G^{\dagger}_{l}\simeq G_{r}^{\dagger}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{d}\to\bar{G}_{d}
is the above leaf-collapse map;

•

we let
(f^{\dagger}_{u},m^{\dagger}_{u})\colon\bar{G}_{u}\xrightarrow{\simeq}G_{r}^{\dagger}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G^{\dagger}_{l}
be the obvious identification, and we let
(\bar{f}_{u},\bar{m}_{u}):=(\check{f}_{u},\check{m}_{u})
;

•

the above data define
\bar{D}
and a morphism
\zeta^{\dagger}\colon(\bar{f},\bar{m})\to(\check{f},\check{m})
in
\dot{\mathcal{L}}
; the morphism
\varepsilon:=\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{1}}\sqcup\zeta^{\dagger}
in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
upgrades to a morphism
\bar{D}\to\check{D}
in
Q_{1}(f,m)
.

Given
\tilde{D}\in Q_{1}^{\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
, we argue that
\varepsilon\circ-\colon Q_{1}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\bar{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{1}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\check{D})
is an equivalence by checking that the hypotheses of Lemma
7.26
are fulfilled.

We let
\mathcal{D}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
denote the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that
G_{d,r}
contains at most one unbased tree, and if it does, this unbased tree contains the vertex
\rho_{d,r}(1)
; we similarly let
\mathcal{D}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
denote the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that the following holds: there is a bijection between the set of components
\pi_{0}(G_{d,r})
of
G_{d,r}
and the set
A\sqcup\check{V}_{r}
, such that the inclusion
A\hookrightarrow A\sqcup\check{V}_{r}
agrees with the composite
A\hookrightarrow\pi_{0}(G_{d,r})\simeq A\sqcup\check{V}_{r}
,
and such that the composite map of finite sets
\underline{1}\sqcup\check{A}\xrightarrow{\rho_{d,r}}A\sqcup V_{d,r}\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{0}(G_{d,r})\simeq A\sqcup\check{V}_{r}
coincides with
\check{\rho}_{r}
. Note that by Remark
7.41
, such a bijection
\pi_{0}(G_{d,r})\simeq A\sqcup\check{V}_{r}
, if it exists, is unique.

We claim that
\varepsilon\circ-
restricts to an equivalence
|\mathcal{D}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{D}_{2}|
. For this, observe that
\varepsilon\circ-
restricts to a functor
\Xi^{**}((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))\to\Xi^{**}((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
, as a consequence of the fact that
G^{\dagger}_{r}
has no edges and
G^{\dagger}_{l}
is an equivalence.
By Lemma
7.39
, we reduce to proving that the restricted functor
\varepsilon\circ-\colon\mathcal{D}_{1}^{**}\to\mathcal{D}_{2}^{**}
induces an equivalence on classifying spaces.
In fact
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{**}
is characterised as the split subcategory of
\Xi^{**}((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
spanned by objects
\zeta
such that
V_{r}=V_{r}\cap\left\{\rho_{r}(1)\right\}
; similarly,
\mathcal{D}^{**}_{2}\subseteq\Xi^{**}((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
is the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that there is a (unique) bijection
V_{r}\simeq\check{V}_{r}
such that
\check{\rho}_{r}
factors as
\underline{1}\sqcup\check{A}\xrightarrow{\rho_{r}}A\sqcup V_{r}\xrightarrow{\simeq}A\sqcup\check{V}_{r}
. The restricted functor

\varepsilon\circ-\colon\mathcal{D}_{1}^{**}\to\mathcal{D}_{2}^{**}
is already an equivalence of categories.

We further let
\mathcal{E}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
denote the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that no component of
G_{d,r}
is an unbased tree; and we let
\mathcal{E}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
denote the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that the following holds: let
\pi_{0}(G_{d,r}^{\mathrm{ut}})
denote the set of components of
G_{d,r}
that are unbased trees, and consider the subset
A^{\dagger}:=(\rho_{r}^{\dagger})^{-1}(V^{\dagger}_{r})\subseteq\check{A}
; then there exists a (necessarily unique) bijection
V^{\dagger}_{r}\simeq\pi_{0}(G_{d,r}^{\mathrm{ut}})
, such that the following composite maps of finite sets agree:

A^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\rho_{r}^{\dagger}}V^{\dagger}_{r}\simeq\pi_{0}(G_{d,r}^{\mathrm{ut}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{0}(G_{d,r});\quad A^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\rho_{d,r}}\tilde{A}\sqcup V_{d,r}\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{0}(G_{d,r}).

We have that
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
contain the preimages of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
along the two functors denoted
(\psi_{1}\sqcup-)\circ\tilde{\zeta}
, respectively.
Moreover
\zeta^{\dagger}\circ-
restricts to a functor
\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}
and to a functor
\Xi^{**}((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\bar{f},\bar{m}))\to\Xi^{**}((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
. Finally,
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{**}
can be characterised as the full subcategory of
\Xi^{**}((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that
G_{r}
has no isolated inner vertices;
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{**}
as the full subcategory of
\Xi^{**}((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
spanned by objects
\zeta
for which the following holds: let
V_{r}^{\mathrm{iiv}}\subseteq V_{r}
denote the set of isolated inner vertices of
G_{r}
; then there is a (necessarily unique) bijection
V_{r}^{\dagger}\simeq V_{r}^{\mathrm{iiv}}
such that the composite

A^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\rho_{r}^{\dagger}}V_{r}^{\dagger}\simeq V_{r}^{\mathrm{iv}}\hookrightarrow\tilde{A}\sqcup V_{r}

agrees with
\rho_{r}|_{A^{\dagger}}
. The restricted functor
\zeta^{\dagger}\circ-\colon\mathcal{E}_{1}^{**}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}^{**}
is an equivalence, inducing an equivalence
\zeta^{\dagger}\circ-\colon|\mathcal{E}_{1}^{**}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}^{**}|
; it follows from Lemma
7.39
that
\zeta^{\dagger}\circ-\colon|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
is also an equivalence.
∎

Notation 7.44
.

We denote by
G^{s}_{u}
the linear subgraph of
G_{u}
defined as follows:

•

in the inner case,
G^{s}_{u}
consists of the unique edge
e_{\ell}
, together with the two inner vertices giving the endpoints of
e_{\ell}
;

•

in the attaching case,
G^{s}_{u}
is the linear subgraph
joining
x\in A
with
v_{\ell}
.

We refer to
G^{s}_{u}
as the “spine” of
G_{u}
, and denote by
E^{s}_{u}\subseteq E_{u}
the set of edges of
G^{s}_{u}
. We have an essentially unique factorisation in
\mathcal{C}
of the form

(f,m)\colon G_{u}\xrightarrow{(f^{b}_{u},m^{b}_{u})}G^{s}_{u}\xrightarrow{(f^{s},m^{s})}G_{d},

in which
f^{s}
collapses all edges of
G_{u}^{s}
, whereas
f^{b}_{u}
collapses all edges in
E_{u}\setminus E^{s}_{u}
.
Letting
G^{b}_{l}=\mathrm{Id}_{B}
,
G^{b}_{r}=\mathrm{Id}_{A}
and
(f^{b}_{d},m^{b}_{d})=\mathrm{Id}_{G_{d}}
, we obtain a special object
\zeta^{b}\in\Xi((f,m),(f^{s},m^{s}))
, giving a morphism
\zeta^{b}\colon(f,m)\to(f^{s},m^{s})
in
\dot{\mathcal{K}}
.

Definition 7.45
.

Let
\bar{A},\bar{B}\in\mathrm{Fin}
with
\#\bar{A}\geq 2
. We say that a gaf
\bar{G}\in\mathcal{C}(\bar{B},\bar{A})
is a
bridge
if there is a 2-element subset
\partial\bar{G}\subseteq\bar{A}
such that, when the marking by
\bar{B}
is disregarded,
\bar{G}
is the disjoint union of the attaching subset
\bar{A}\setminus\partial\bar{G}
and of a linear graph intersecting
\bar{A}
at its set of endpoints
\partial\bar{G}
.
For uniform notation, whenever a gaf
\bar{G}\in\mathcal{C}(\bar{B},\bar{A})
is
not
a bridge, we declare
\partial\bar{G}:=\emptyset\subseteq\bar{A}
.

Definition 7.46
.

We give a classification of objects in
Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
, represented by diagrams as in Notation
7.40
, according to types.

(I)

An object
\bar{D}
is of type I if
A\sqcup\bar{V}_{r}=\left\{\bar{x}\right\}
is a singleton, where we use Notation
7.40
; in this case the following hold:

–

(\bar{f},\bar{m})
is an equivalence, and the gaf’s
\bar{G}_{u}\simeq\bar{G}_{d}
are given by the same map of finite sets
\bar{\rho}_{u}=\bar{\rho}_{d}\colon\bar{B}\to\bar{A}
;

–

\bar{G}_{l}
is also given by a map of finite sets
\bar{\rho}_{l}\colon B\to\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{B}
, sending
\rho_{u}^{-1}(v_{\ell})
to
1\in\underline{1}
and restricting to a map
B\setminus\rho_{u}^{-1}(v_{\ell})\to\bar{B}
;

–

\bar{f}_{d}\colon\bar{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{l}\to G_{d}
is the unique equivalence, with
\bar{f}_{d}\colon\bar{x}\mapsto x
;

–

\bar{f}_{u}
collapses all edges of
G_{u}
except
e_{\ell}
, and
\bar{m}_{u}
is the restriction of
m
.

Note that if
\bar{D}
is not of type I, then
A\sqcup\bar{V}_{r}
has two elements
x\in A
and
\bar{x}\in\bar{V}_{r}
.

(II)

An object
\bar{D}
not of type I is of type II if
(\bar{f},\bar{m})
is an equivalence, and the gaf’s
\bar{G}_{u}\simeq\bar{G}_{d}
are given by the same map of finite sets
\bar{\rho}_{u}=\bar{\rho}_{d}\colon\bar{B}\to\bar{A}
; in this case the following hold:

–

\bar{G}_{l}
is a bridge, and the set
\bar{E}_{l}
corresponds along
\bar{f}_{u}
to a nonempty subset
\bar{X}_{l}\subseteq E^{s}_{u}\setminus\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}
;

–

\bar{\rho}_{r}\circ\bar{\rho}_{u}
restricts to a bijection
\partial\bar{G}_{l}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\left\{x,\bar{x}\right\}
; in particular
\bar{\rho}_{u}
is injective on
\partial\bar{G}_{l}
;

–

\bar{f}_{u}
collapses all edges in
E_{u}\setminus(\bar{X}_{l}\sqcup\left\{e_{\ell}\right\})
, and
\bar{m}_{u}

is restricted from
m
;

–

\bar{f}_{d}
collapses all edges in
\bar{X}_{l}
, and
\bar{m}_{d}
is the restriction of
m
;

–

\bar{\rho}_{l}\colon B\to\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{B}\sqcup\bar{V}_{l}
is a map of finite sets, sending
\rho_{u}^{-1}(v_{\ell})
to
1
and the rest of
B
to
\bar{B}\sqcup\bar{V}_{l}
.

(III)

An object
\bar{D}
is of type III if it is neither of type I nor of type II; in this case the following hold:

–

\bar{G}_{u}
and
\bar{G}_{d}
are bridges with
\partial\bar{G}_{u}=\partial\bar{G}_{d}\subseteq\bar{A}
, and the sets of edges
\bar{E}_{u}
and
\bar{E}_{d}
correspond, along
\bar{f}_{u}
and
(\bar{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{f}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{l})\circ\bar{f}_{u}
, to two nonempty, nested subsets
\bar{X}_{d}\subseteq\bar{X}_{u}\subseteq E^{s}_{u}\setminus\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}
, respectively;

–

\bar{\rho}_{r}
restricts to a bijection
\partial\bar{G}_{u}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\left\{x,\bar{x}\right\}
;

–

\bar{f}_{u}
,
\bar{f}
and
\bar{f}_{d}
collapse the sets of edges
E_{u}\setminus(\bar{X}_{u}\sqcup\left\{e_{\ell}\right\})
,
\bar{X}_{u}\setminus\bar{X}_{d}
and
\bar{X}_{d}
, respectively, and
\bar{m}_{u}
,
\bar{m}
and
\bar{m}_{d}
are restricted from
m
;

–

\bar{G}_{l}
is given by a map of finite sets sending
\rho_{u}^{-1}(v_{\ell})
to
1\in\underline{1}
and the rest of
B
to
\bar{B}
.

Remark 7.47
.

We observe that only objects of type I may occure in the inner case
A=\emptyset
; the same holds in the attaching case if the edge
e_{\ell}
of
G_{u}
is attached directly to
x\in A
, i.e., if
E^{s}_{u}=\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}
.

We are ready for the main result of the subsection.

Lemma 7.48
.

Recall Notation
7.44
, and
consider the functor
-\circ\zeta^{b}\colon Q_{1}(f^{s},m^{s})\to Q_{i}(f,m)
induced by the morphism
\zeta^{b}
in the light of the pullback definition of
Q_{1}(f,m)
and
Q_{1}(f^{s},m^{s})
from Lemma
7.17
. Then
-\circ\zeta^{b}
restricts to an equivalence
Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f^{s},m^{s})^{\to}\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
.

Proof.

First, we observe that
-\circ\zeta^{b}
indeed restricts to a functor
Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f^{s},m^{s})^{\to}\to Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
, since
f^{b}_{u}\colon h_{\ell}\mapsto h_{\ell}
and since
G^{b}_{\star}
is an identity gaf for
\star=l,r
. The restricted functor is moreover essentially surjective: given
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
as in Definition
7.46
, for all types I, II and III we have that
\bar{f}_{u}
must collapse all edges in
E_{u}\setminus E^{s}_{u}
, so
(\bar{f}_{u},\bar{m}_{u})
factors through
(f^{b}_{u},m^{b}_{u})
.

Finally, we apply Lemma
7.27
to show that the restricted functor is fully faithful: given
\bar{D},\check{D}\in Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f^{s},m^{s})^{\to}
,
we let
\check{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((f^{s},m^{s}),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))^{\to}
and
\check{\zeta}\circ\zeta^{b}\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))^{\to}
denote the split subcategories spanned in both cases by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that
at most one component of
G_{d,r}
is an unbased tree, and if it exists it contains
\rho_{d,r}(1)
.
We have that
-\circ\zeta^{b}
restricts to an equivalence
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{**}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{**}
, inducing an equivalence
|\mathcal{D}_{1}^{**}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{D}_{2}^{**}|
; by Lemma
7.39
we conclude that
-\circ\zeta^{b}\colon|\mathcal{D}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{D}_{2}|
is also an equivalence.
∎

Lemma
7.48
allows us to reduce the proof of Proposition
7.22
(1) to the case in which
G_{u}
is linear and coincides with its own spine. We conclude the subsection with a further mild reduction, which we operate mostly to save notation later. The proof of the following lemma is left to the reader.

Lemma 7.49
.

Let
B^{\prime}:=B\setminus\rho_{u}^{-1}(v_{\ell})\subseteq B
, let
G_{l}
be the gaf corresponding to the inclusion of finite sets
B^{\prime}\hookrightarrow B
, let
(f^{\prime},m^{\prime}):=(f,m)\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{r}
, and let
\zeta\in\Xi((f^{\prime},m^{\prime}),(f,m))
denote the special object defined by the previous factorisation, by letting
G_{l}
,
f_{u}
and
f_{d}
be equivalences. Then the functor
-\circ\zeta\colon Q_{1}(f,m)\to Q_{1}(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})
induced by
\zeta
in the light of the pullback definition of
Q_{1}(f,m)
and
Q_{1}(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})
restricts to an equivalence
-\circ\zeta\colon Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{1}(f^{\prime},m^{\prime})^{\to}
.

Lemma
7.49
allows us to further reduce to the setting in which
\rho_{u}^{-1}(v_{\ell})=\emptyset
.

7.9.
Contractibility of
|Q_{1}(f,m)^{\to}|

In this subsection we conclude the proof of Proposition
7.22
(1), under the assumption that
G_{d}
consists of a single vertex, the tree
G_{u}
is linear and coincides with its own spine, and
\rho_{u}^{-1}(v_{\ell})=\emptyset
.

Definition 7.50
.

Recall Definition
7.45
.
We denote by
Q_{1}^{\flat}(f,m)^{\to}\subseteq Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
the full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory spanned by objects
\bar{D}
as in Definition
7.46
satisfying the following property:

(vii)

\bar{\rho}_{l}
restricts to a bijection
\bar{\rho}_{l}^{-1}(\bar{B}\setminus\partial\bar{G}_{l})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{B}\setminus\partial\bar{G}_{l}
.

We denote by
Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}\subseteq Q_{1}^{\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
the full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory spanned by objects
\bar{D}
satisfying
the following additional property:

(viii)

\bar{\rho}_{u}
restricts to a bijection
\bar{\rho}_{u}^{-1}(\bar{A}\setminus(\partial\bar{G}_{u}\cup\bar{\rho}_{u}(\partial\bar{G}_{l})))\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{A}\setminus(\partial\bar{G}_{u}\cup\bar{\rho}_{u}(\partial\bar{G}_{l}))
.

Lemma 7.51
.

The inclusions
Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}\subseteq Q_{1}^{\flat}(f,m)^{\to}\subseteq Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
induce equivalences on classifying spaces.

Proof.

We prove that the first inclusion admits a left adjoint, and the second a right adjoint. For the first claim, given
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}^{\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
, we define a candidate left adjoint object
\check{D}\in Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
together with a morphism
\eta\colon\bar{D}\to\check{D}
as follows:

•

we let
\check{B}
be the following disjoint union of two finite sets:

\check{B}:=(\partial\bar{G}_{u}\cup\bar{\rho}_{u}(\partial\bar{G}_{l}))\sqcup\bar{\rho}_{u}^{-1}(\bar{A}\setminus(\partial\bar{G}_{u}\cup\bar{\rho}_{u}(\partial\bar{G}_{l})));

we let
G_{r}^{\flat}
be the gaf corresponding to the map of finite sets
\check{B}\to\bar{B}
restricting to the inclusion, respectively to
\bar{\rho}_{u}
, on the two parts;

•

we let
(\bar{f},\bar{m})=G_{r}^{\flat}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(\check{f},\check{m})
be the essentially unique horizontal factorisation; in particular we have
\bar{G}_{\star}=G_{r}^{\flat}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{\star}
for
\star=u,d
;

•

we let the
G_{l}^{\flat}
be the identity gaf of
\check{A}:=\bar{A}
;
we let
(f_{u}^{\flat},m_{u}^{\flat})
be the identification
\bar{G}_{u}\xrightarrow{\simeq}G_{r}^{\flat}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{l}^{\flat}
and
(f_{d}^{\flat},m_{u}^{\flat})
be the identification
G_{r}^{\flat}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{l}^{\flat}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{G}_{d}
;

•

we let
\check{G}_{r}:=\bar{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}(\underline{1}\sqcup G_{r}^{\flat})
; and we let
\check{G}_{l}:=\bar{G}_{l}
and
(\check{f}_{\star},\check{m}_{\star}):=(\bar{f}_{\star},\bar{m}_{\star})
for
\star=u,d
;

•

we upgrade
\eta:=\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{1}}\sqcup\zeta^{\flat}
to a morphism in
Q_{1}(f,m)
.

For
\tilde{D}\in Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
, we show that
-\circ\eta\colon Q_{1}(f,m)(\check{D},\tilde{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{1}(f,m)(\bar{D},\tilde{D})
is an equivalence by checking that the hypotheses of Lemma
7.25
are fulfilled. We let
\tilde{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
denote the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that the following holds: for each
\tilde{b}\in(\tilde{B}\setminus\partial\tilde{G}_{l})
, the component of
G_{d,l}
containing
\tilde{b}
is a tree intersecting
\tilde{B}
only in
\tilde{b}
, and whose preimage along
\rho_{d,l}
consists only of the element
\tilde{\rho}_{l}^{-1}(\tilde{b})\in B
. We let
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
be the preimages of
\mathcal{D}
along the functors
(\psi_{1}\sqcup-)\circ\check{\zeta}
and
(\psi_{1}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta}
.

Since the data giving
\zeta^{\flat}
are all equivalences or maps of finite sets, we have that
-\circ\zeta^{\flat}

restricts to a functor
\Xi^{**}((\check{f},\check{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))\to\Xi^{**}((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\tilde{f},\tilde{m}))
.
We claim that the restricted functor

-\circ\zeta^{\flat}\colon\mathcal{E}_{1}^{**}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}^{**}
is an equivalence. For this, let
\zeta\in\mathcal{E}_{2}^{**}
; denote by
\bar{B}^{\prime}:=\bar{\rho}_{u}^{-1}(\bar{A}\setminus(\partial\bar{G}_{u}\cup\rho_{u}(\partial\bar{G}_{l}))\subseteq\bar{B}
, and let
B^{\prime}:=\bar{\rho}_{l}^{-1}(\bar{B}^{\prime})\subseteq B
; then
\bar{\rho}_{l}\colon B^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{B}^{\prime}
is a bijection. Let moreover
\tilde{A}^{\prime}:=\rho_{u,r}^{-1}(\bar{A}\setminus(\partial\bar{G}_{u}\cup\rho_{u}(\partial\bar{G}_{l}))\subseteq\tilde{A}
. Then the composition of gaf’s

B\xrightarrow{\bar{G}_{l}}\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{B}\xrightarrow{G_{u,l}}\underline{1}\sqcup\tilde{B}\xrightarrow{\tilde{G}_{u}}\underline{1}\sqcup\tilde{A}\xrightarrow{G_{u,r}}\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{A}

induces a composite map of finite sets as follows, using also that all based trees in
G_{u,\star}
are isolated attaching vertices for
\star=l,r
:

B^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\bar{\rho}_{l}}\bar{B}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\rho_{u,l}}\tilde{\rho}_{l}(B^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\tilde{\rho}_{u}}\tilde{\rho}_{u}\tilde{\rho}_{l}(B^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\rho_{u,r}}\bar{A}\setminus(\partial\bar{G}_{u}\cup\rho_{u}(\partial\bar{G}_{l})).

All arrows but the last in the previous composite are bijections. This shows that
G_{u,r}
factors through
G^{\flat}_{r}
in a canonical way. The same argument shows that
G_{d,r}
factors canonically through
G^{\flat}_{r}
; extending the argument to analyse morphisms in
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{**},\mathcal{E}_{2}^{**}
, we obtain that
-\circ\zeta^{\beta}\colon\mathcal{E}_{1}^{**}\to\mathcal{E}_{2}^{**}
is an equivalence. Hence we have equivalences
-\circ\zeta^{\beta}\colon|\mathcal{E}_{1}^{**}|\to|\mathcal{E}_{2}^{**}|
and, by Lemma
7.39
,
-\circ\zeta^{\beta}\colon|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\to|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
.

For the second claim, given
\check{D}\in Q_{1}^{\dagger\dagger}(f,m)^{\to}
, we define
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}^{\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
together with a morphism
\varepsilon\colon\bar{D}\to\check{D}
as follows:

•

we let
\bar{B}
denote the set
\partial\check{G}_{l}\sqcup\check{\rho}_{l}^{-1}(\check{B}\setminus\partial\check{G}_{l})
, and let
G^{\flat}_{l}
be the gaf corresponding to the map of finite sets
\bar{B}\to\check{B}
restricting to the inclusion and to
\check{\rho}_{l}
, respectively, on the two parts;

•

we factor
\check{G}_{l}
in the essentially unique way as a horizontal composition

B\xrightarrow{\bar{G}_{l}}\underline{1}\sqcup\bar{B}\xrightarrow{\underline{1}\sqcup G_{l}^{\flat}}\underline{1}\sqcup\check{B};

•

we let
(\bar{f},\bar{m}):=(\check{f},\check{m})\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{l}^{\flat}
, in particular
\bar{G}_{\star}:=\check{G}_{\star}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{l}^{\flat}
for
\star=u,d
;

•

we let
G_{r}^{\flat}
be the identity gaf of
\bar{A}:=\check{A}
; we let
(f_{u}^{\flat},m_{u}^{\flat})
be the identification
\bar{G}_{u}\xrightarrow{\simeq}G_{r}^{\flat}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{l}^{\flat}
, and
(f_{d}^{\flat},m_{d}^{\flat})
be the identification
G_{r}^{\flat}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\check{G}_{d}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}G_{l}^{\flat}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{G}_{d}
;

•

we let
\bar{G}_{r}:=\check{G}_{r}
,
(\bar{f}_{u},\bar{m}_{u}):=(\check{f}_{u},\check{m}_{u})
, and
(\bar{f}_{d},\bar{m}_{d}):=(\check{f}_{d},\check{m}_{d})
;

•

we upgrade
\varepsilon:=\mathrm{Id}_{\psi_{1}}\sqcup\zeta^{\flat}
to a morphism in
Q_{1}(f,m)
.

For
\tilde{D}\in Q_{1}^{\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
, we show that
\varepsilon\circ-\colon Q_{1}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\bar{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}Q_{1}(f,m)(\tilde{D},\check{D})
by checking tha the hypotheses of Lemma
7.26
are fulfilled. Let
\bar{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{1}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
denote the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
as in Notation
7.8
such that the following holds: for each
\bar{b}\in\bar{B}\setminus\partial\bar{G}_{l}
, the component of
G_{d,l}
containing
\bar{b}
is a tree intersecting
\bar{B}
only in
\bar{b}
, and whose preimage along
\rho_{d,l}
consists only of the element
\bar{\rho}_{l}^{-1}(\bar{b})\in B
. Define similarly
\check{\zeta}\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\subseteq\Xi((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
as the split subcategory spanned by objects
\zeta
such that the following holds: for each
\check{b}\in\check{B}\setminus\partial\check{G}_{l}
, the component of
G_{d,l}
containing
\check{b}
is a tree intersecting
\check{B}
only in
\check{b}
, and whose preimage along
\rho_{d,l}
consists precisely of the set
\check{\rho}_{l}^{-1}(\check{b})\subseteq B
.
Since the data giving
\zeta^{\flat}
are all equivalences or maps of finite sets, we have that
\varepsilon\circ-
and
\zeta^{\flat}\circ-
restrict to functors
\Xi^{**}((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))\to\Xi^{**}((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\check{f},\check{m}))
and
\Xi^{**}((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\bar{f},\bar{m}))\to\Xi^{**}((\tilde{f},\tilde{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
, respectively.
The restricted functor
\varepsilon\circ-\colon\mathcal{D}_{1}^{**}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{**}
is an equivalence. By Lemma
7.39
we obtain an equivalence
\varepsilon\circ-\colon|\mathcal{D}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{D}_{2}
.
Similarly as above, we let
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
denote the preimages of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
along the two functors denoted
((\psi_{1}\sqcup-)\circ\tilde{\zeta})
, respectively.
An analogous but simpler argument as the one above shows that
\zeta^{\flat}\circ-
restricts to an equivalence
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{**}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{**}
; by Lemma
7.39
we obtain an equivalence
\zeta^{\flat}\circ-\colon|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|
.
∎

The
{(\infty,1)}
-category
Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
is small enough to allow a direct analysis. In fact we will show that it is a (plain) poset, with contractible classifying space.

Definition 7.52
.

Given a linear gaf
\bar{G}\in\mathcal{C}(\bar{B},\bar{A})
and a subset
T
of its set of all edges and vertices, we say that
T
is
convex
if whenever
y,y^{\prime}\in T
, then all edges and vertices in
\bar{G}
lying between
y
and
y^{\prime}
also belong to
T
. For a subset
T^{\prime}\subset\bar{A}\sqcup\bar{V}\sqcup\bar{E}
we abbreviate by
\bar{G}\setminus T^{\prime}
the set
(\bar{A}\sqcup\bar{V}\sqcup\bar{E})\setminus T^{\prime}
. A
segment
in
\bar{G}\setminus T^{\prime}
is a maximal convex subset for
\bar{G}
that is contained in
\bar{G}\setminus T^{\prime}
and that contains at least one edge.

Definition 7.53
.

We associate with an object
\bar{D}\in Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
a
profile

\pi(\bar{D})
, i.e. a list of invariants of the object; this is defined according to the type of
\bar{D}
.

(I)

We associate the empty profile, denoted
\pi(\bar{D})=()
.

(II)

We associate the profile
\pi(\bar{D})=(\bar{B}^{\circ},\bar{A}^{\circ},\bar{X})
consisting of two nested subsets
\emptyset\subseteq\bar{A}^{\circ}\subseteq\bar{B}^{\circ}\subseteq B
, and a
nonempty
subset
\bar{X}\subseteq E_{u}\setminus\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}
consisting of all edges of a segment of
G_{u}\setminus(\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}\cup\rho_{u}(\bar{B}^{\circ}))
. We define
\bar{B}^{\circ}=\bar{B}\setminus\partial\bar{G}_{l}
, considered as a subset of
B
by taking the preimage along
\bar{\rho}_{l}
; we similarly define
\bar{A}^{\circ}:=\bar{A}\setminus\bar{\rho}_{u}(\partial\bar{G}_{l})
; and we define
\bar{X}
as the set of edges of the segment of
G_{u}\setminus(\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}\cup\rho_{u}(\bar{B}^{\circ}))
containing
\bar{X}_{l}=\bar{f}_{u}^{-1}(\bar{E}_{l})
.

(III)

We associate the profile
\pi(\bar{D})=(\bar{A}^{\circ},\bar{X}_{u},\bar{X}_{d})
consisting of a subset
\emptyset\subseteq\bar{A}^{\circ}\subseteq B
and two
nonempty
, nested subsets
\bar{X}_{d}\subseteq X_{u}\subseteq E_{u}\setminus\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}
, such that
\bar{X}_{d}
and
\bar{X}_{u}
are contained in a segment of
G_{u}\setminus(\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}\cup\rho_{u}(\bar{A}^{\circ}))
. We define
\bar{A}^{\circ}:=\bar{A}\setminus\partial\bar{G}_{u}
; and we define
\bar{X}_{d}\subseteq\bar{X}_{u}
as the preimages of
\bar{E}_{d}
and
\bar{E}_{u}
along
(\bar{G}_{r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{f}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{l})\circ\bar{f}_{u}
and
\bar{f}_{u}
, respectively.

We denote by
\mathcal{D}_{\pi(\bar{D})}\subseteq\Xi^{**}((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
the split subcategory spanned by those objects
\zeta
such that the following holds: if
\zeta^{\prime}
is a special object connected to
\zeta
by some morphism in
\Xi^{**}((f,m),\psi_{1}\sqcup(\bar{f},\bar{m}))
, we have that
\zeta^{\prime}
and
(\bar{f},\bar{m})
combine in a new object
\bar{D}^{\prime}\in Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
with
\pi(\bar{D}^{\prime})=\pi(\bar{D})
.

Lemma 7.54
.

The core groupoid of
Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
is homotopy discrete, and its equivalence classes of objects are in bijection with all possible profiles.

Proof.

It is clear that every profile can be achieved by some object. Given an object
\bar{D}
, the moduli space of objects exhibiting the profile
\pi(\bar{D})
can be computed as the classifying space of
\mathcal{D}_{\pi(\bar{D})}
.
We have that
\mathcal{D}_{\pi(\bar{D})}

is the terminal category whenever
\bar{D}
is of type I or III. If instead
\bar{D}
is of type II, then
\mathcal{D}_{\pi(\bar{D})}
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{P}_{\neq\emptyset}(\bar{X}))
, where
\mathcal{P}_{\neq\emptyset}
denotes the poset of nonempty subsets. Concretely, we associate with
\zeta\in\mathcal{D}
the pair of nonempty subsets
X_{d,l}\subseteq X_{u,l}\subseteq\bar{X}
obtained as preimages of
E_{d,l}
and
E_{u,l}
along
(G_{u,r}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\bar{G}_{u}\circ_{\mathrm{h}}f_{l})\circ f_{u}
and
f_{u}
, respectively. We conclude by observing that
|\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{P}_{\neq\emptyset}(\bar{X}))|\simeq*
.
∎

Remark 7.55
.

Let
\bar{D},\check{D}\in Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
; using that
|\mathcal{D}_{\pi(\check{D})}|\simeq*
, we obtain that the morphism space
Q_{1}(f,m)(\bar{D},\check{D})
is equivalent to the classifying space of the split subcategory
\mathcal{E}_{\bar{D},\check{D}}:=((\psi_{1}\sqcup-)\circ\bar{\zeta})^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{\pi(\check{D})})\subseteq\Xi((\bar{f},\bar{m}),(\check{f},\check{m}))
, or by Lemma
7.39
, to the classifying space of
\mathcal{E}^{**}_{\bar{D},\check{D}}
. A routine analysis shows the following:

•

\mathcal{E}^{**}_{\bar{D},\check{D}}
is the terminal category whenever one of the following occurs:

–

\bar{D}
and
\check{D}
are of type I;

–

\bar{D}
and
\check{D}
are of type II, and we have inclusions
\bar{A}^{\circ}\subseteq\check{A}^{\circ}\subseteq\check{B}^{\circ}\subseteq\bar{B}^{\circ}
, and correspondingly
\bar{X}\subseteq\check{X}
;

–

\bar{D}
and
\check{D}
are of type III, and we have inclusions
\bar{X}_{d}\subseteq\check{X}_{d}\subseteq\check{X}_{u}\subseteq\bar{X}_{u}
and
\bar{A}^{\circ}\subseteq\check{A}^{\circ}
;

•

\mathcal{E}^{**}_{\bar{D},\check{D}}\simeq\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{P}(T))
for some finite set
T
, where
\mathcal{P}
denotes the poset of all subsets, whenever
\bar{D}
is of type III and one of the following occurs:

–

\check{D}
is of type I; in this case we let
T=\bar{X}_{u}\setminus\bar{X}_{d}
;

–

\check{D}
is of type II, and we have inclusions
\bar{A}^{\circ}\subseteq\check{A}^{\circ}
and
\bar{X}_{d}\subseteq\check{X}
; in this case we let
T=(\check{X}\cap\bar{X}_{u})\setminus\bar{X}_{d}
;

•

in all other cases
\mathcal{E}^{**}_{\bar{D},\check{D}}
is empty.

The previous analysis show in particular that
Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
is equivalent to a (finite) poset, whose underlying set of objects is the set of all possible profiles.

Proof of Proposition
7.22
(1).

By Corollary
7.32
and
Lemmas
7.48
and

7.49
, it suffices to consider the case of an object
(f,m)\in\dot{\mathcal{K}}
as in Notation
7.1
such that
G_{d}
consists of a single vertex,
G_{u}
is a linear graph and coincides with its own spine, and
\rho_{u}^{-1}(v_{\ell})=\emptyset\subseteq B
. By Lemmas

7.43
and

7.51
it suffices to prove that
|Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}|
is contractible. By Lemma
7.54
and Remark
7.55
, we have that
Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
is a finite poset.
We denote by
Q\subseteq Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
the full subposet spanned by objects of type I, and by objects
\bar{D}
of type III whose profile satisfies the following property:
\bar{X}_{u}
is maximal, i.e. it consists of all edges of the segment of
G_{u}\setminus(\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}\cup\rho_{u}(\bar{A}^{\circ}))
containing it. The inclusion
Q\subseteq Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
admits a right adjoint: it fixes all objects of type I (indeed, it has to fix all objects of
Q
); it sends the object
\bar{D}
of type III with profile
(\bar{A}^{\circ},\bar{X}_{u},\bar{X}_{d})
to the object of type III with profile
(\bar{A}^{\circ},\bar{X},\bar{X}_{d})
, where
\bar{X}
is the set of all edges of the segment of
G_{u}\setminus(\left\{e_{\ell}\right\}\cup\rho_{u}(\bar{A}^{\circ}))
containing
\bar{X}_{u}
; and it sends
\bar{D}
of type II with profile
(\bar{B}^{\circ},\bar{A}^{\circ},\bar{X})
to the object of type III with profile
(\bar{A}^{\circ},\bar{X},\bar{X})
. It follows that the inclusion
Q\subseteq Q_{1}^{\flat\flat}(f,m)^{\to}
induces an equivalence on classifying spaces. Finally, we have
|Q|\simeq*
because
Q
has a terminal object, namely the essentially unique object of type I.
∎

8.
The universal property of
\mathrm{Gr}

In this final section we prove Corollaries
A.1
and
A.2
, and we give an application exhibiting
\mathrm{Gr}
as an
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory of the (large)
{(\infty,1)}
-category
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
of cospans between spaces.

8.1.
Proof of the corollaries of Theorem
A

Proof of Corollary
A.1
.

The object
\mathrm{Gr}=|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}|_{2}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
represents the restriction of the functor
\mathbf{GL}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)})\to\mathcal{S}
to the full
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
of
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)})
, so our goal is to provide an equivalence

\mathbf{GL}|_{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})}\simeq\mathrm{CFrob}.

We start by providing a natural transformation between the two functors: we send a graph-like structure
(\mathfrak{I},\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{e},\beta)
on
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
to the pair
(\mathfrak{I},\mathfrak{t})
. We need to check that this pair is indeed a commutative Frobenius algebra in
\mathcal{C}
. Let
a:=\mathfrak{I}(\underline{1})
and let
c:=\mathfrak{t}\circ\mathfrak{I}(\mu)
. To prove that
(a,c)
is a duality datum for
a
, we need to prove that there exists
u
and homotopies as in Definition
2.12
. We let
u:=\mathfrak{e}\colon\mathbf{1}\simeq\mathfrak{I}(\underline{0})\to a\otimes a\simeq\mathfrak{I}(\underline{2})
; then the 2-morphism
\beta
, which is invertible as
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}
, provides an equivalence
(c\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})\circ(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes u)\simeq\mathrm{Id}_{a}
. Now let
\tau\colon a^{\otimes 2}\to a^{\otimes 2}
denote the map swapping the factors, and let
\tau^{\prime}:=(\tau\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes\tau)\colon a^{\otimes 3}\to a^{\otimes 3}
denote the map permuting cyclically the factors. Since both
c
and
u
are
C_{2}
-equivariant morphisms, we have equivalences
c\simeq c\tau
and
u\simeq\tau u
; combining these we get a chain of equivalences

\begin{split}(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes c)(u\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})&\simeq(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes c\tau)(\tau u\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})\simeq(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes c)(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes\tau)(\tau\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})(u\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})\\
&\simeq(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes c)\tau^{\prime}\tau^{\prime}(u\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})\simeq(c\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})\circ(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes u)\simeq\mathrm{Id}_{a}.\end{split}

We next prove that the constructed natural transformation
\mathbf{GL}|_{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})}\Rightarrow\mathrm{CFrob}
is an equivalence. For this, we will show that for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)})
and for
(\mathfrak{I},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathrm{CFrob}(\mathcal{C})
, the fibre at
(\mathfrak{I},\mathfrak{t})
of the map
\mathbf{GL}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{CFrob}(\mathcal{C})
is contractible. The mentioned fibre is the space of ways to upgrade
(\mathfrak{I},\mathfrak{t})
to a graph-like structure. For simplicity we set again
a:=\mathfrak{I}(\underline{1})
and denote by
\mu
also the morphism
\mathfrak{I}(\mu)\colon a^{\otimes 2}\to a
. We start by showing that the space of choices of the following two data is contractible:

•

a
non-
C_{2}
-equivariant
morphism
\mathfrak{e}\colon\mathbf{1}\to a^{\otimes 2}
;

•

a homotopy
\beta
connecting
(\mathfrak{t}\mu\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes\mathfrak{e})
to
\mathrm{Id}_{a}
.

Setting
x=z=a
and
y=\mathbf{1}
in Lemma
2.14
we obtain an equivalence of spaces
\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{1},a^{\otimes 2})\overset{\simeq}{\to}\mathcal{C}(a,a)
, which makes the choice of a point
\mathfrak{e}
in the first space equivalent to the choice of its image
(\mathfrak{t}\mu\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes\mathfrak{e})
in the second space; the joint choice of the latter point together with a path
\beta
joining it to the given point
\mathrm{Id}_{x}
is evidently contractible.

We next prove that, given a completely non-
C_{2}
-equivariant datum
(\mathfrak{I},\mathfrak{e},\mathfrak{t},\beta)
, the space of choices of the following two data is also contractible:

•

a
C_{2}
-equivariant structure on
\mathfrak{e}
;

•

a
C_{2}
-equivariant structure on the homotopy
\tilde{\beta}:=\mathfrak{t}\mu(\beta\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})
connecting the
C_{2}
-equivariant morphisms
\mathfrak{t}\mu((\mathfrak{t}\mu\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes\mathfrak{e})\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})
and
\mathfrak{t}\mu
. Here the first morphism carries a
C_{2}
-equivariant structure by being equivalent to
(\mathfrak{t}\mu)^{\otimes\underline{2}}(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes\mathfrak{e}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})
, where the
C_{2}
-equivariant structure on
(\mathfrak{t}\mu)^{\otimes\underline{2}}
swaps the two tensor factors, and the
C_{2}
-equivariant structure on
(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes\mathfrak{e}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})
swaps the first and last tensor factors, and uses the
C_{2}
-equivariant structure on
\mathfrak{e}
chosen above.

Setting
x=a^{\otimes\underline{2}}
and
y=z=\mathbf{1}
in Lemma
2.14
, we obtain a
C_{2}
-equivariant equivalence
\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{1},a^{\otimes\underline{2}})\overset{\simeq}{\to}\mathcal{C}(a^{\otimes\underline{2}},\mathbf{1})
; here we put the trivial
C_{2}
-action on
\mathbf{1}
and the one swapping the two tensor factors on
a^{\otimes\underline{2}}
; both maps in the composite of Lemma
2.14
have an evident
C_{2}
-equivariant structure. Therefore a choice of a
C_{2}
-equivariant structure on
\mathfrak{e}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{1},a^{\otimes\underline{2}})
is equivalent to a choice of a
C_{2}
-equivariant structure on its image
(\mathfrak{t}\mu)^{\otimes\underline{2}}(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes\mathfrak{e}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})
; the joint choice of the latter
C_{2}
-equivariant structure and of a
C_{2}
-equivariant structure on the path
\tilde{\beta}
joining the point
(\mathfrak{t}\mu)^{\otimes\underline{2}}(\mathrm{Id}_{a}\otimes\mathfrak{e}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{a})
to the given
C_{2}
-equivariant point
\mathfrak{t}\mu
is evidently contractible.
∎

The argument of proof of Corollary
A.2
as a consequence of Corollary
A.1
is due to Barkan–Steinebrunner; it is included here for the sole purpose of completeness.

Proof of Corollary
A.2
.

By Corollary
A.1
, it suffices to construct a natural equivalence
\mathrm{CFrob}|_{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{S})}\overset{\simeq}{\to}\Omega
of functors
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{S})\to\mathcal{S}
.
As a natural transformation, this is given by sending
(\mathfrak{I},\mathfrak{t})\in\mathrm{CFrob}(M)
to the composite path

\mathfrak{t}\circ\mathfrak{I}(\underline{0}\to\underline{1})\colon\mathfrak{I}(\underline{0})\to\mathfrak{I}(\underline{0}).

We next prove that the constructed natural transformation is an equivalence. We first observe that, since
|\mathrm{Fin}|\simeq*
, the space
\mathrm{Fun}^{\otimes}(\mathrm{Fin},M)\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\otimes}(|\mathrm{Fin}|,M)
is contractible. Consequently,
\mathrm{CFrob}(M)
is equivalent to the space of commutative Frobenius algebra structures on the constant symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{Fin}\to M
at
\mathbf{1}\in M
, i.e. of morphism
c\colon\mathbf{1}\simeq\mathbf{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}\to\mathbf{1}
such that
(\mathbf{1},c)
is a duality datum for
c
. The fact that
M
is a space ensures that every endomorphism of
\mathbf{1}\in M
makes
(\mathbf{1},c)
into a duality datum. This concludes the proof that
\mathrm{CFrob}(M)\simeq\Omega M
.
∎

8.2.
Geometric realisation of graphs

Definition 8.1
.

We denote by
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})\in\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,1)}^{\mathfrak{U}_{2}}
the large
{(\infty,1)}
-category of cospans between spaces. Its objects are spaces; for
X,Y\in\mathcal{S}
, the morphism space from
X
to
Y
is the (large) core groupoid
(\mathcal{S}_{X\sqcup Y/})
, i.e. the moduli space of cospans of spaces of the form
X\to W\leftarrow Y
. Composition in
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
is given by taking pushouts. We endow
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
with the symmetric monoidal structure given by disjoint unions of spaces and (pointwise) of cospans.

We have a symmetric monoidal functor
\mathcal{S}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
, sending
X\mapsto X
and sending a map of spaces
X\to Y
to the cospand
X\to Y=Y
.

Definition 8.2
.

By virtue of Corollary
A.1
, we define a symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,1)}
-functor

\Re\colon\mathrm{Gr}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
by providing a commutative Frobenius algebra
(\mathfrak{I}_{\Re},\mathfrak{t}_{\Re})

in
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
:

•

we let
\mathfrak{I}_{\Re}\colon\mathrm{Fin}\to\mathcal{D}
be the composition of the symmetric monoidal functors
\mathrm{Fin}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
.

•

we let
\mathfrak{t}_{\Re}\colon\mathfrak{I}_{\Re}(\underline{1})\to\mathfrak{I}_{\Re}(\underline{0})
be the cospan
\underline{1}\to\underline{1}\leftarrow\underline{0}
.

The fact that
(\mathfrak{I}_{\Re},\mathfrak{t}_{\Re})
is a commutative Frobenius algebra is witnessed by the following data

•

we let
u\colon\mathfrak{I}_{\Re}(\underline{0})\to\mathfrak{I}_{\Re}(\underline{2})
be the cospan
\underline{0}\to\underline{1}\leftarrow\underline{2}
;

•

letting
c=\mathfrak{t}_{\Re}\circ\mathfrak{I}_{\Re}(\mu)
, we have (essentially unique) equivalences of morphisms
(c\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}})\circ(\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}}\sqcup u)\simeq\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}}
and
(\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}}\sqcup c)\circ(u\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}})\simeq\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{1}}
in
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
: we use here that in both cases the source and the target of the equivalence are equivalent to the cospan
\underline{1}\to\underline{1}\leftarrow\underline{1}
, which is a terminal object in
\mathcal{S}_{\underline{1}\sqcup\underline{1}/}
.

Remark 8.3
.

The notation “
\Re
” is similar to the one used in Definition
3.4

to denote the cell complex
\Re(G)
constructed from a gaf
G
: in fact, up to replacing “cell complexes” with the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{S}
, we have that
\Re
sends the 1-morphism
G\colon B\to A
in
\mathrm{Gr}
to the cospan of spaces
A\to\Re(G)\leftarrow B
.

To see this formally, let
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ospan}(\mathrm{Top})
denote the (large) symmetric monoidal 2-category of cospans of topological spaces: its objects are topological spaces; for
X,Y\in\mathrm{Top}
, the (large) 1-category
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ospan}(\mathrm{Top})(X,Y)
is the 1-category
\mathrm{Top}_{X\sqcup Y/}
; horizontal composition is given by taking pushouts in
\mathrm{Top}
; the monoidal product is given by taking disjoint unions. We may similarly introduce a symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,2)}
-category
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ospan}(\mathcal{S})
; the (product preserving, hence symmetric monoidal) localisation at weak equivalences
\mathrm{Top}\to\mathcal{S}
induces a symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,2)}
-functor
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ospan}(\mathrm{Top})\to\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ospan}(\mathcal{S})
.

We may now define a symmetric monoidal 2-functor
\tilde{\Re}\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathrm{Top})
as follows: at the level of objects, we send each finite set to itself, considered as a topological space; for
B,A\in\mathrm{Fin}
the functor
\tilde{\Re}\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathrm{Top})(B,A)
sends
G
to the cospan of topological spaces
B\to\Re(G)\leftarrow A
, and sends
f\colon G\to G^{\prime}
to the evident weak equivalence of topological spaces
\Re(G)\to\Re(G^{\prime})
collapsing 1-cells corresponding to edges collapsed along
f
, and restricting to a linear identification on 1-cells that are not collapsed. The composite symmetric monoidal
{(\infty,2)}
-functor

{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}\xrightarrow{\tilde{\Re}}\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ospan}(\mathrm{Top})\to\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ospan}(\mathcal{S})

factors through
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})\simeq(\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ospan}(\mathcal{S}))^{2\simeq}
, and it agrees with
\Re
, as can be checked by comparing the associated graph-like structures, or even more directly, the associated commutative Frobenius algebras.

We conclude the article with the following theorem.

Theorem 8.4
.

The functor
\Re\colon\mathrm{Gr}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
is faithful, in the sense that it induces an inclusion of spaces at the level of core groupoids and at the level of morphism spaces. More precisely,
\Re
exhibits
\mathrm{Gr}
as the (small)
{(\infty,1)}
-subcategory of
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
whose objects are spaces homotopy equivalent to finite sets, and whose morphisms from
B
to
A
are those cospans of spaces
B\leftarrow W\to A
such that
W
is homotopy equivalent to a finite cell complex of dimension at most 1.

Proof.

Given finite sets
B,A
, representing objects in
\mathrm{Gr}
, our goal is to compute the morphism space
\mathrm{Gr}(B,A)
; by definition of
\mathrm{Gr}
, this is the classifying space
|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)|
of the morphism category
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)
from Definition
3.7
. More precisely, our goal is to prove that the map
|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)|\to(\mathcal{S}_{A\sqcup B/})^{\simeq}
induced by the functor
\Re
is an inclusion of spaces.

Let
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime}
denote the full subcategory of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)
spanned by gaf’s
G
satisfying the following properties:

•

each element of
A
has valence 1 in
G
(it is an “attaching leaf”);

•

the map
\rho\colon B\to A\sqcup V
is injective, has image inside
V
, and all elements of its image are leaves.

Now recall the gaf
G_{\beta,1}
from Subsection
3.3
, and consider the endofunctor

\xi:=\left(\coprod_{B}G_{\beta,1}\right)\circ_{\mathrm{h}}-\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\left(\coprod_{A}G_{\beta,1}\right)\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A).

Then
\xi
has image inside
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime}
; moreover we have a natural transformation
\left(\coprod_{B}\beta_{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}\right)\circ_{\mathrm{h}}-\circ_{\mathrm{h}}\left(\coprod_{A}\beta_{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}\right)\colon\xi\to\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)}
. It follows that
|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)|\simeq|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime}|
.

We next consider the full subcategory
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}
of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime}
spanned by gaf’s
G
whose only vertices of valence 1 are those contained in
A
or in the image of
\rho
. The inclusion
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime}
admits a left adjoint
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}
, sending a gaf
G
to the gaf
G^{\prime}
obtained by repeatedly collapsing all non-marked leaves of
G
and of the gaf’s that one obtains througout the procedure. It follows that
|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime}|\simeq|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}|
.

Let now
(B\to W\leftarrow A)\in(\mathcal{S}_{B\sqcup A/})^{\simeq}
be a cospan of spaces with
W
equivalent to a finite cell complex of dimension
\leq 1
, and let
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{W}
denote the fibre of the
{(\infty,1)}
-functor
\Re\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}\to(\mathcal{S}_{B\sqcup A/})^{\simeq}
at
W
. Our goal is to prove that
|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{W}|
is contractible.
If
W
splits as a disjoint union of connected spaces
\coprod_{i}W_{i}
, letting
A_{i}:=A\cap W_{i}
and
B_{i}:=B\cap W_{i}
we have a product decomposition
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{W}\simeq\prod_{i}{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B_{i},A_{i})^{\prime\prime}_{W_{i}}
, inducing an analogous product decomposition on classifying space. We may therefore assume that
W
is connected.

We first consider the special case
W\simeq*
. Let
*\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{S}^{\simeq}_{B\sqcup A/})^{\simeq}
denote the component of
B\to*\leftarrow A
. Then the fibre product
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{B\sqcup A/}}*
is the subcategory of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}
spanned by the single gaf
G
having precisely one inner vertex not marked by
B
; the classifying space of this category is contractible and hence also
|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{*}|\simeq*
.

We next consider the special case
W\simeq S^{1}
and
A=B=\emptyset
. Let
\mathbf{B}\mathrm{hAut}(S^{1})\subset\mathcal{S}^{\simeq}
denote the component of
S^{1}
, and let
\mathbf{B}\mathrm{hAut}^{+}(S^{1})\simeq\mathbb{C}P^{\infty}
denote the double (and universal) cover of the latter.
Then the fibre product
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(\emptyset,\emptyset)^{\prime\prime}\times_{\mathcal{S}^{\simeq}}\mathbf{B}\mathrm{hAut}(S^{1})
is equivalent to the unstraightening of the cocartesian fibration over
\mathbf{B}C_{2}
corresponding to the
C_{2}
-action on the cyclic category
\Lambda
by reflections. Since
|\Lambda|\simeq\mathbf{B}\mathrm{hAut}^{+}(S^{1})
, we obtain that
|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(\emptyset,\emptyset)^{\prime\prime}_{S^{1}}|
is indeed contractible.

From now on we assume
\chi(W,B\sqcup A)<0
. This implies in particular that the component
\mathbf{B}\mathrm{hAut}_{B\sqcup A/}(W)\subset(\mathcal{S}_{B\sqcup A/})^{\simeq}
containing
B\to W\leftarrow A
is aspherical, and hence that
\subset{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{W}
is a plain category. It is instructive to think of objects in
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{W}
as gaf’s with a marking by
W
, compatible with the marking by
B
and the inclusion of
A
.

Let
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime\prime}\subset{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{W}
denote the full subcategory spanned by objects whose image in
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}
are gaf’s
G
satisfying the following property: there is no “bridge” in
G
, where a bridge is an edge of
G
that is not a leaf (i.e., neither of its endpoints is in
A
or in the image of
\rho
), and it separates
G
into two non-empty gaf’s. The inclusion
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime\prime}\subset{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{W}
admits a left adjoint
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{W}\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime\prime}
, sending a gaf
G
to the gaf obtained by collapsing all bridges of
G
. It follows that
|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime\prime}|\simeq|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime}_{W}|
.

Finally, we invoke the work of
Culler–Vogtmann
[
4
]
, in the case
A=B=\emptyset
and
\chi(W)<0
, and the generalisation of
Hatcher–Vogtmann

[
8
, Section 3]
in the remaining cases. If
n
denotes the rank of
W
and if
s=\#A+\#B
, then the (topological) geometric realisation of the simplicial set given by the nerve of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(B,A)^{\prime\prime\prime}
is homeomorphic to the spine of outer space, denoted
K
in
[
4
]
, or to the spine of the space
A_{n,s}
from
[
8
]
; in both cases it is a contractible space.
∎
